## 2026-09-16

- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: First working slice of the MVP's Moodle connection and change-detection loop, in a new `Esmerelda/` app (backend + frontend), all under `backend/`:
  - `moodle/browser.py` — Playwright connector using a persistent Chromium profile (`browser_profile/`) so login only has to happen once per machine; prompts for manual login when the session isn't already authenticated, then reads active courses by locating Moodle's "Only courses in progress" dashboard text and matching it against `/course/view.php` links.
  - `course_radar.py` — reworked active-course detection to be less dependent on that dashboard text: matches course names against a regex for the current term's course codes (`BUAN`/`DESG`/`VATS` + `2026/27S1`) instead, and moved the persistent browser profile under `moodle/browser_profile/` so all scripts share one logged-in session.
  - `content_radar.py` — for each active course, walks its sections and activity links, classifies each item by type (PDF, PowerPoint, Assignment, Quiz, Video, Forum, etc.) from the link/href, and filters out navigation/UI noise. Persists results to `state/content_snapshot.json` and diffs the new scrape against the last one to find newly-added items — this is the change-detection piece from the plan, so the same resource doesn't get reported twice.
  - `submission_radar.py` — scans Moodle's Timeline block for entries matching "is due", walks up the DOM from each link to find the owning course, deduplicates by assignment+course, and turns the result into a short spoken briefing via `pyttsx3` (e.g. "You have 2 upcoming submissions..."). This is the first text-to-speech output for the proactive digest — earlier than planned (final goals listed real voice I/O as post-MVP), but it was a natural fallback once `voice.py`'s simple `EsmereldaVoice` wrapper class was working.
  - `voice.py` — small standalone `EsmereldaVoice` class wrapping `pyttsx3` (rate/volume set, `speak()` method), used as the first voice-output test before folding TTS into `submission_radar.py`.
  - `frontend/` — scaffolded a Vite + React + TypeScript app; still the default template, not yet wired to the backend.
  - Known rough edges going into the next session: `course_radar.py`'s active-course regex is hardcoded to this term's course codes and will need updating (or a more general rule) next term; `content_radar.py`'s course list is hardcoded rather than pulled from `course_radar.py`'s output; the two active-course-detection approaches (`browser.py`'s text-based one and `course_radar.py`'s regex-based one) haven't been consolidated into one.

## 2026-09-17
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Added a persistent storage layer and a document downloader on top of yesterday's Moodle scrapers, so scraped courses/assignments/resources survive between runs and their actual files get pulled down, not just logged to the console:
  - `storage/` — new SQLAlchemy module: `database.py` (SQLite engine at `storage/esmerelda.db` + `init_db()`), `models.py` (`Course`, `Assignment`, `Resource`, `Document`, `DocumentVersion` tables), and `crud.py` (`save_course`/`save_assignment`/`save_resource`/`save_document`, each an upsert keyed on the Moodle ID so re-running a radar script updates existing rows instead of duplicating them). `save_document` also writes a `DocumentVersion` row whenever a file's SHA-256 hash changes, so document history is kept, not just the latest copy.
  - `course_radar.py` and `content_radar.py` now call `save_course`/`save_resource` after scraping, so active courses and course resources get written to the database instead of only being printed.
  - `submission_radar.py` now also parses an actual due-date/time out of the Timeline block text (via a date-pattern regex applied to the DOM ancestors above each assignment link) and persists each assignment via `save_assignment`; course-matching for each assignment was also switched over to the same current-term course-code regex used in `course_radar.py`, instead of only relying on nearby course links.
  - `moodle/browser.py` gained `get_authenticated_page()`, a reusable helper that opens the persistent browser profile, handles the login-wait prompt, and hands back `(playwright, context, page)` — pulled out so other scripts (the downloader) don't need to duplicate that setup.
  - `moodle/document_downloader.py` (new) — reads every `Resource` row from the database whose URL is a `/mod/resource/view.php` link, fetches it with Playwright's request API, tells apart an actual file response from Moodle serving back an HTML page (e.g. a login/permission page), and saves the file to `storage/documents/` with a hash-based `Document`/`DocumentVersion` record; skips resources it's already downloaded. Ran successfully against real course content — `storage/documents/` now holds ~25 real files (PDFs, slide decks, spreadsheets, a couple of zips) pulled from the active courses.
  - Small test scripts `storage/test_storage.py` and `storage/cleanup_test.py` to sanity-check the DB layer (insert a throwaway course, then delete it) — not a real test suite yet, just manual smoke checks.
  - Known rough edges going into the next session: `submission_radar.py`'s new due-date/course-matching code has leftover debug prints (`DEBUG ERROR at level {level}`) and stray blank lines/an unused `from ast import pattern` import from when it was being iterated on — needs a cleanup pass; the storage layer has no real automated tests yet, just the two manual smoke scripts; `content_radar.py` still hardcodes its course list rather than reading it back from what `course_radar.py` just saved to the database.

## 2026-09-17 (frontend UI build)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Built the Esmerelda frontend UI from scratch on top of the existing bare Vite + React + TS scaffold in `frontend/` (previously just the default template). No backend code was touched — `frontend/` was the only directory modified.
  - **Dependencies installed**: `react-router-dom`, `lucide-react`, `clsx` (runtime); `tailwindcss` + `@tailwindcss/vite` (dev, Tailwind v4's Vite plugin, no separate `tailwind.config.js`/PostCSS setup needed).
  - **Config**: `vite.config.ts` — added the Tailwind plugin, a `@` → `src` path alias, and an `/api` dev-server proxy to `http://localhost:8000` for when FastAPI endpoints exist. `tsconfig.app.json` — added the matching `@/*` path mapping (no `baseUrl`, since TS 6's bundler resolution flagged it deprecated).
  - **Design system**: `src/index.css` defines a dark-first Tailwind v4 `@theme` — a `base-*` charcoal scale (950→50), one `accent-*` blue scale, and `fade-in`/`slide-up` entrance animations. No component library; all styling is Tailwind utility classes.
  - **Types**: `src/types/index.ts` mirrors `backend/storage/models.py` (`Course`, `Assignment`, `Resource`, `Document`) plus frontend-only types (`MoodleSyncStatus`, `ChatMessage`, `Conversation`) not yet backed by a table.
  - **API layer**: `src/lib/api.ts` exports one async function per data need (`getCourses`, `getAssignments`, `getDocuments`, `getMoodleSyncStatus`, `sendMockChatMessage`, etc.) plus a real `apiFetch<T>` helper aimed at `/api/*`. Every function currently resolves data from `src/lib/mockData.ts` (clearly commented as mock, latency-simulated) — pages only ever import from `api.ts`, so wiring a real FastAPI endpoint later means changing one function's body, not any page code. `src/lib/useAsync.ts` is a small hook wrapping loading/error/data state for these calls.
  - **Reusable components** (`src/components/ui/`): `StatCard`, `AssignmentCard`, `CourseCard`, `DocumentCard`, `SearchBar`, `FilterControls`, `EmptyState`, `Badge`, and skeleton loaders (`LoadingSkeleton.tsx`). Layout components (`src/components/layout/`): `AppShell` (sidebar + header + routed outlet), `Sidebar` (nav + mobile overlay), `Header` (page title + live Moodle sync-status badge).
  - **Pages** (`src/pages/`), routed via `react-router-dom` in `App.tsx`: `Dashboard` (greeting, stats, quick actions, upcoming assignments, active courses, recent documents), `Courses` (searchable grid) + `CourseDetail` (assignments/resources for one course), `Assignments` (search, status filter, sort), `Documents` (search, course/file-type filters), `Chat` (conversation UI with suggested prompts, loading dots, simulated error + retry, new-conversation reset — explicitly labeled as mock, not a real AI backend).
  - **Verification**: `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` both clean. Ran the dev server and drove it with a Playwright script (chromium-cli wasn't available in this environment) across all 6 routes at desktop and mobile viewports — zero console/page errors, verified the mobile sidebar overlay opens/closes and a suggested-prompt chat round trip renders correctly.
  - **Known limitations**: All data is mocked; no FastAPI data endpoints exist yet to connect to (only `/` and `/health` in `backend/venv/main.py`). No completion-status field exists on `Assignment` in the DB, so the UI intentionally shows only due-date urgency (overdue/due-soon/upcoming), not completion state. No automated component/e2e tests were added, only a manual Playwright smoke pass.
- **Recommended next step**: Stand up real FastAPI endpoints (`/api/courses`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/resources`, `/api/documents`) backed by `storage/crud.py`, then flip the corresponding functions in `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` from mock to `apiFetch`.

## 2026-09-17 (JARVIS-style command-center redesign)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Full visual redesign of `frontend/` from a generic SaaS dashboard into a cinematic AI command-center interface, at the user's explicit request to rethink the design language rather than make cosmetic tweaks. Backend untouched; only `frontend/` changed.
  - **Fonts & tokens**: `index.html` now loads Space Grotesk (display), Inter (UI text), and JetBrains Mono (code/timestamps) from Google Fonts. `src/index.css` was rewritten with a new `@theme`: a graphite depth scale (950→50, near-black to warm white) instead of the old blue-gray scale, an electric-cyan primary accent + violet secondary accent used sparingly, restrained status colors, and keyframes for the AI core (`orb-rotate`, `orb-pulse`, `core-breathe`, `shimmer`, `caret-blink`). Added `.ambient-field` (layered radial-gradient background glow), `.glass-panel`, and reduced-motion overrides under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`.
  - **AI core visual** (`src/components/core/AiCore.tsx`, new): a layered SVG reactor — counter-rotating dashed rings, orbiting particles, a breathing radial-gradient core — with `idle`/`active`/`processing` states that change animation speed and glow, not layout. Reads `prefers-reduced-motion` via a new `src/lib/useReducedMotion.ts` hook and disables its animations accordingly. Used at three sizes: large in the dashboard hero, small as Esmerelda's "avatar" next to chat messages.
  - **Navigation**: `Sidebar.tsx` rebuilt as a collapsible vertical rail (default 76px icon-only, expandable to 256px), with a glowing left-edge active indicator, hover tooltips when collapsed, and renamed items matching the new mental model — Command Center, Courses, Assignments, Knowledge Base, Conversations, Settings. `Header.tsx` is now a slim bar with a live clock and a sync-status pill instead of a heavy title bar. Added a minimal `/settings` placeholder page (`src/pages/Settings.tsx`) since the nav needed a working link for it — profile fields only, explicitly labeled as not yet backed by real preferences.
  - **Command console** (`src/components/ui/CommandInput.tsx`, new): a large glass-panel textarea with a focus glow ring, a disabled mic button explicitly labeled "coming soon", Enter-to-send / Shift+Enter-for-newline, used both as the dashboard's centerpiece input and (in a compact `docked` variant) as the chat page's input bar.
  - **Dashboard → Command Center** (`src/pages/Dashboard.tsx`): replaced the old stat-card grid entirely with a hero section (AI core + greeting + inline status readout: active courses / documents indexed / last synced) plus the command console and suggested command chips ("Scan my upcoming deadlines", etc., from `src/lib/mockData.ts`). Below the hero: a priority feed, active-courses list, and recently-indexed documents, all as open translucent panels with thin-hairline row separators rather than a grid of cards.
  - **Redesigned/renamed primitives** (`src/components/ui/`): `AssignmentCard`→`PriorityRow.tsx` (urgency color bar + inline status pill, not a boxed card), `CourseCard`→`CourseWorkspaceCard.tsx` (hover glow, gradient initial badge), `DocumentCard`→`DocumentIndexRow.tsx` (adds `indexingStatus`/`lastAccessedAt` — not backed by any column in `backend/storage/models.py` yet, flagged inline in `mockData.ts`), `Badge`→`StatusPill.tsx`. Removed `StatCard.tsx` outright (the brief explicitly asked to avoid "a grid of boring statistic cards"). `SearchBar`, `FilterControls`, `EmptyState`, `LoadingSkeleton` restyled to the graphite/cyan palette; skeletons now use a `shimmer-surface` gradient sweep instead of a flat pulse.
  - **Chat → Conversations** (`src/pages/Chat.tsx` + new `src/components/ui/ChatMarkdown.tsx`): word-by-word streaming reveal (via `setInterval`, not a real token stream) with a blinking caret, a custom lightweight markdown renderer (bold, inline code, fenced code blocks with a language label, bullet lists — no new dependency), source citation chips, clickable AI-suggested follow-up chips, and a keyboard-shortcut hint under the input. The dashboard's command console now navigates to `/chat` with `router` state carrying the typed/chip-selected message, which the chat page auto-sends once on mount. `src/lib/api.ts`'s `sendMockChatMessage` was extended to return richer `{content, sources?, followUps?}` mock payloads (including a markdown+code-block example keyed off "logistic"/"regression" in the prompt) — still clearly mock, still the only place a real `/api/chat` call would be wired in later.
  - **Verification**: `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` both clean. Drove the dev server with Playwright (no `chromium-cli` in this environment) across all 7 routes at 1440px with zero console/page errors; separately verified the code-block/source/follow-up chat rendering, sidebar collapse + hover tooltips via in-SPA navigation, and layouts at mobile (390px), tablet (834px), and laptop (1280px) widths — no horizontal overflow or overlapping elements at any size.
  - **Known limitations**: still 100% mock data (no FastAPI data endpoints exist to connect to yet); voice input is a visibly disabled placeholder button; streaming is a simulated word-reveal timer, not a real token stream; `indexingStatus`/`lastAccessedAt` on documents have no backend column yet.
- **Recommended next step**: unchanged from the prior entry — stand up real FastAPI endpoints backed by `storage/crud.py` and flip `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` functions from mock to `apiFetch` one at a time.

## 2026-09-17 (wired the frontend to the real backend)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Replaced every mock data source in `frontend/` with real reads from `backend/storage/esmerelda.db` via a new small FastAPI API layer, and made every page fail honestly instead of ever silently showing fake data. Confirmed real data exists first (`courses=7, assignments=2, resources=213, documents=34` via a direct sqlite3 query) before writing a single endpoint.
  - **Backend — new `backend/api/` package** (routes mounted from `backend/venv/main.py`, which is the existing FastAPI entrypoint — an unusual location since `venv/` is also the actual Python virtualenv directory, but left in place rather than restructured):
    - `schemas.py` — Pydantic response models with camelCase fields matching the frontend's TS types 1:1, so no field-mapping layer is needed on either side.
    - `presenters.py` — two small derivation helpers, not new stored data: `derive_short_name()` parses a display code like "BUAN301 · 2026/27S1" out of `Course.name` (the DB's `short_name` column exists but `course_radar.py` never populates it), and `derive_file_type()` reads the extension off `Document.name` (the DB's `file_type` column is likewise always `NULL` — `document_downloader.py` never sets it).
    - `routes.py` — `GET /api/courses`, `GET /api/courses/{id}` (404 if missing), `GET /api/courses/{id}/assignments`, `GET /api/courses/{id}/resources`, `GET /api/assignments` (all courses, sorted by due date with nulls last), `GET /api/documents` (left-joined through `Resource`→`Course` so documents with no linked resource still show up with `courseId: null` instead of being dropped), `GET /api/documents/{id}/download` (streams the file via `FileResponse` — the filesystem path itself is never sent to the client, only used server-side), `GET /api/sync-status`, `GET /api/dashboard/summary`.
    - `main.py` — added `sys.path.insert(0, .../backend)` so `storage`/`api` are importable regardless of cwd (needed because of the `venv/main.py` layout), added `CORSMiddleware` with `allow_origin_regex=r"http://localhost:\d+"` for the Vite dev server's variable port, and mounted the router. Root `/` and `/health` were left untouched.
  - **Honest limitations reflected in the API, not hidden**: `DocumentOut.indexingStatus` is always `"indexed"` — that's literally true (every row in the `documents` table is, by definition, a file that downloaded successfully) but there's no real indexing pipeline, so `"indexing"`/`"queued"` are unused reserved values. `AssignmentOut` has no submission-status field — the DB has no such column, and the frontend's urgency badges (overdue/due-soon/upcoming) are the only "status" shown, same as the mock-data version. There is no `/api/chat` endpoint — confirmed by inspection, not assumed.
  - **Frontend — `src/lib/api.ts`** rewritten from mock-resolving functions to real `fetch` calls against `` `${VITE_API_BASE_URL}/api...` ``, added `frontend/.env` (`VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8000`) and `src/vite-env.d.ts` for its typing. New `ApiError` class carries the HTTP status so `getCourseById` can turn a 404 into `undefined` (a real "not found") while any other failure still throws and surfaces as a real error. `sendChatMessage` always rejects with a new `ChatUnavailableError` — since no chat endpoint exists, the chat page now shows a clearly-labeled "AI backend offline" system notice per message instead of the previous mock streaming/markdown/sources demo, per this task's explicit instruction not to fake AI responses as real ones.
  - **`src/lib/useAsync.ts`** gained a `refetch()` — every page's error state now has a working Retry button, not just a static message.
  - **New `src/components/ui/ErrorState.tsx`**, used (instead of silently falling through to an empty-state or mock data) whenever a fetch fails on Dashboard, Courses, CourseDetail, Assignments, and Documents. Verified live: killed the backend process, confirmed every section on every page switched to a red "Couldn't load this" panel with the real error message and a Retry button (and the header's sync pill switched to "Backend offline") — nothing rendered a fake "you're all caught up" or empty list — then restarted the backend and confirmed Retry recovers cleanly.
  - **Types/UI adjusted to match real data**: `types/index.ts` — `DocumentFile` lost `filePath` (never sent to the client) and `lastAccessedAt` (no real data existed for it, so it was removed rather than faked) and gained `downloadUrl`; `ResourceType` changed from an invented pdf/slides/spreadsheet union to the DB's actual categories (`activity`/`resource`/`link`/`quiz`/`assignment`/`video`/`other`). `DocumentIndexRow`'s download button now links to the real `/api/documents/{id}/download` endpoint. `CourseDetail`'s resource-type label map was updated to match. `src/lib/mockData.ts` was trimmed to just the suggested-prompt chip copy (UI strings, not domain data) since every other mock export is now unused.
  - **Verification**: `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` both clean. Ran backend (`uvicorn main:app --app-dir venv --port 8000`, from `backend/`) and frontend (`npm run dev`) together; curl-tested every new endpoint individually (including the 404 case and the actual file download, which returned real bytes with the correct content type); drove the running app with Playwright across all 6 routes — zero console errors and zero non-OK `/api/*` responses — and visually confirmed real Moodle data throughout (7 real course names/codes, the 2 real assignments due Sep 17/18, all 34 real filenames with correct extensions and course attribution, the 213 real weekly-section/resource rows on a course page).
  - **Commands to run both servers**:
    - Backend: `cd backend && venv/Scripts/python.exe -m uvicorn main:app --app-dir venv --port 8000` (Windows) — the venv's own interpreter is used directly since no `requirements.txt` exists yet to reinstall from.
    - Frontend: `cd frontend && npm run dev` (reads `VITE_API_BASE_URL` from `frontend/.env`).
  - **Remaining limitations**: still no AI/chat backend — the chat page is an honest "offline" preview, not a functioning assistant. No automated backend tests were added (the two existing `storage/test_storage.py` / `cleanup_test.py` smoke scripts are untouched). CORS is wide open to any `localhost` port for dev convenience only — not production-safe as-is. `backend/venv/main.py`'s location inside the virtualenv folder is unchanged; it works via the `--app-dir venv` + `sys.path` fix but is worth relocating in a future cleanup pass.
- **Recommended next step**: decide on a real AI backend (even a thin RAG-over-`storage/documents/` prototype) and add `/api/chat`; separately, consider moving `main.py` out of `venv/` into `backend/` directly to remove the current import workaround.

## 2026-09-17 (implemented POST /api/chat — a real, rule-based agent)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Implemented the `/api/chat` endpoint that was previously missing (the prior session's chat page always threw `ChatUnavailableError`). Checked first — no Claude Code Skill named `assignment-action-planner` exists anywhere in this repo's `.claude/skills/`, the plugin catalog available to this session, or the filesystem more broadly (confirmed with a full-disk `find`) — so "integrate the custom assignment-action-planner Skill" was implemented as a named backend planning module fulfilling that role, documented as such at the top of the file so it's easy to correct if a different skill was actually meant.
  - **Backend refactor — `backend/api/queries.py`** (new): pulled every DB query and every ORM-row-to-response-model conversion out of `routes.py` into one shared module, so the new chat agent and the existing REST routes call the exact same functions instead of duplicating query logic. `routes.py` is now a thin HTTP layer over `queries.py`; behavior is unchanged (re-verified every existing endpoint still returns 200 after the refactor).
  - **`backend/api/assignment_action_planner.py`** (new) — the requested planning component. Pure rule-based reasoning over real `Assignment` rows: classifies each into overdue/due-soon/upcoming/unscheduled using the same 48-hour threshold as `getAssignmentUrgency` in the frontend (so the plan it produces agrees with what the UI already shows), sorts by urgency then due date, and renders a short numbered action plan with a plain-language recommendation per item. No model call anywhere in it.
  - **`backend/api/chat_agent.py`** (new) — the agent loop itself, explicitly structured as perceive → reason → act → observe → iterate:
    - *perceive* — keyword-matches the message against four intents (deadlines / documents / sync / help) and separately checks whether it names a real course (by course code or by the subject text after the last `-` in `Course.name`, e.g. "Tangible Interfaces").
    - *reason* — turns that into a bounded list of steps (`MAX_STEPS = 3`) — a matched course always short-circuits to a single `course_lookup` step (which itself gathers assignments + resources + optionally documents for that course), otherwise each matched intent becomes its own step.
    - *act* — each step calls real `queries.py` functions and, for anything assignment-related, `assignment_action_planner.plan_actions`.
    - *observe* — each step appends real findings to a shared context (deduped by id).
    - *iterate* — the loop runs every reasoned step before composing the final reply, so a compound message (a course name plus "documents") triggers more than one real query in a single turn.
    - Explicitly rule-based throughout — no LLM call — and says so in its own docstring and in the reply the frontend shows under "Esmerelda" (`rule-based agent over your real course data, not a generative model`), per this task's "do not fabricate" instruction.
  - **`backend/api/schemas.py`** — added `ChatRequestIn` (validated `message: str`, 1–2000 chars — an empty body now correctly 422s), `ChatSourceOut`, `ChatResponseOut` (`reply` plus real `assignments`/`courses`/`documents`/`sources`/`followUps` arrays, all built from the same `AssignmentOut`/`CourseOut`/`DocumentOut` models the REST endpoints already return).
  - **`backend/venv/main.py`** — bug found and fixed during integration testing: `CORSMiddleware` only allowed `GET`, so every `POST /api/chat` call failed the CORS preflight from the browser (`net::ERR_FAILED`, confirmed in a Playwright console-error capture before the fix). Added `POST` to `allow_methods`.
  - **Frontend — `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`**: `sendChatMessage` now POSTs to the real endpoint and returns the typed `ChatApiResponse` instead of always throwing; `ChatUnavailableError` is now reserved for genuine connectivity/HTTP failures (network down, non-2xx), not "no endpoint exists". `frontend/src/types/index.ts` gained `ChatApiResponse` and extended `ChatMessage` with optional `assignments`/`courses`/`documents` so the chat UI can render real referenced records, not just prose.
  - **`frontend/src/pages/Chat.tsx`** rewritten to call the real agent: a genuine "Thinking…" state while the request is in flight (real latency, not theater), assistant replies render through the existing `ChatMarkdown` component plus new inline chips for any assignments (urgency dot + real due date, linking to the course), courses (linking to `/courses/:id`), and documents (linking to the real download endpoint) the agent returned. The header subheading now reflects actual reachability (`Online` / `Unreachable`, driven by whether the last request succeeded) instead of a hardcoded label — caught and fixed after noticing it still said "Online" in a screenshot taken with the backend deliberately killed.
  - **Bug found & fixed mid-session**: the first "priority plan" follow-up chip read "Tell me more about the top one" — since the agent is stateless (no conversation memory), that phrase doesn't match any intent or course and fell through to the fallback reply on click (reproduced and screenshotted). Changed it to name the actual top-priority assignment's course by code (e.g. "Tell me more about DESG319"), which does resolve via the course-matching logic on the next turn — verified by clicking it and confirming a real course lookup came back.
  - **Verification** (all run against the live app, not simulated): `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` clean. Backend restarted and every endpoint curl-tested individually: `help`, `deadlines` ("what is due this week"), course lookup ("tell me about tangible interfaces"), compound course+documents ("what documents do you have for tangible interfaces" — correctly reported 0 real documents for that specific course), sync status, fallback (nonsense input), and empty-message validation (422). Confirmed the refactor didn't regress `/api/courses`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/documents`, `/api/sync-status`, `/api/dashboard/summary` (all still 200). Drove the running frontend with Playwright: sent real messages through the chat UI end to end (zero console errors after the CORS fix), clicked a follow-up chip and confirmed a second real agent turn, killed the backend process and confirmed the chat page showed the honest "unreachable" state with the real error message (not a canned one) and the header switched to "Backend offline", restarted the backend and confirmed it recovered. Final full-route regression pass (`/dashboard`, `/courses`, `/courses/1`, `/assignments`, `/documents`, `/chat`, `/settings`) — zero console errors, zero failed `/api/*` requests.
  - **Known limitations**: the agent has no conversation memory across turns (each message is perceived independently — a follow-up like "what about that one" won't resolve unless it names something concrete); intent matching is keyword-based, not semantic, so it will miss paraphrases a real language model would catch; `assignments_count` in the demo dataset is only 2, so the "deadlines" and "priority plan" paths were exercised with real but small data — course lookups against the 6 other courses (which have 0 tracked assignments each) were used to confirm the empty-case reply text.
- **Recommended next step**: if genuine natural-language understanding is wanted later, the perceive step is the natural place to swap keyword-matching for an actual LLM call — the reason/act/observe structure and the `assignment_action_planner` module would not need to change.

## 2026-09-17 (real Gemini agent with function calling, replacing the rule-based one)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Replaced the keyword-matching chat agent from the previous session with a real Gemini-powered agent using the official `google-genai` SDK's function-calling, while keeping the exact same `POST /api/chat` contract so nothing else had to change. Verified the API key first (see the prior session's standalone connectivity check) before touching any agent code.
  - **Key/SDK verification redux**: confirmed `google-genai` 2.24.0 and `python-dotenv` 1.2.3 already installed in `backend/venv`. Before writing real code, ran a throwaway probe script (in the session scratchpad, deleted immediately after) against the real API key to confirm the exact SDK shape for automatic function calling — `client.chats.create(model=..., config=types.GenerateContentConfig(tools=[python_func], system_instruction=...))` then `chat.send_message(text)` — rather than guessing at an SDK surface that's changed version to version. That probe is what surfaced the SDK's own recommendation to use `chats.send_message` over `models.generate_content` for tool use, and confirmed passing plain Python functions directly as `tools` works and their real return values get fed back to the model automatically.
  - **`backend/api/matching.py`** (new) — extracted the course-name/code matching heuristic (previously private to the old agent) into its own module so both the new Gemini tools and the old deterministic agent resolve "Tangible Interfaces" / "DESG322" to the same real `Course` row the same way. No duplicated logic.
  - **`backend/api/gemini_tools.py`** (new) — the three required tools as real Python closures over a live DB `Session`: `get_upcoming_assignments` (wraps `queries.fetch_all_assignments` + `assignment_action_planner.plan_actions`, satisfying "integrate the assignment-action-planner Skill" by having Gemini reason over pre-ranked real urgency instead of doing its own date math), `get_course_info` (wraps `queries.fetch_course_assignments`/`fetch_course_resources` via `matching.match_course`), `get_course_documents` (wraps `queries.fetch_all_documents`, optionally scoped to one course). Every tool returns only what the query actually found — an unmatched course returns `{"error": ..., "available_courses": [...]}`, never invented data. A `ToolCollector` captures the real `AssignmentOut`/`CourseOut`/`DocumentOut` objects each call fetched so the frontend still gets structured chips, not just prose.
  - **`backend/api/gemini_agent.py`** (new) — `handle_chat_message_gemini(db, message)`: loads `GEMINI_API_KEY` via `python-dotenv` from `backend/.env` at import time, builds a fresh chat per request (stateless across HTTP requests, matching the existing architecture) with the three tools and a system instruction that explicitly forbids stating any assignment/course/document fact not returned by a real tool call in that conversation. Model: `gemini-3.6-flash` (the name the API itself returned as current when the previous session's `gemini-2.5-flash` probe 404'd — Google retired that model for new users). Raises `GeminiUnavailableError` (message never includes the API key) on a missing key or any SDK/network exception.
  - **`backend/api/deterministic_agent.py`** — the old chat_agent.py, kept verbatim (function renamed to `handle_chat_message_deterministic`) as a fallback rather than deleted, and refactored to import `matching.match_course` instead of its own private copy.
  - **`backend/api/chat_agent.py`** — now a 20-line orchestrator: try `gemini_agent.handle_chat_message_gemini`; on `GeminiUnavailableError`, fall back to `deterministic_agent.handle_chat_message_deterministic` and prefix the reply with an honest, visible note that Gemini was unavailable and this is the deterministic backup. `routes.py`'s `POST /api/chat` handler didn't need to change at all.
  - **System-instruction tuning found and fixed mid-session**: first version of the anti-fabrication rules still let the model call `get_upcoming_assignments` "just in case" after `get_course_info` had already reported a course as not found, producing a reply that correctly said "no such course" but attached unrelated real-assignment chips underneath — confusing even though nothing was fabricated. Added an explicit rule against calling unrelated tools after a not-found result; re-tested the same prompt and got a clean reply with no stray chips and the model correctly listing the real available course names instead.
  - **Frontend**: no contract changes needed (`api.ts`/`Chat.tsx` from the prior session already called the real endpoint and rendered `assignments`/`courses`/`documents`/`sources` chips generically). Only fixed one now-false label — the chat subheader said "rule-based agent... not a generative model", which stopped being true; changed to "Gemini, grounded in your real Moodle data".
  - **Verification — all against the live app, no fabricated results**:
    - `npx tsc -b` / `npm run build`: clean.
    - Backend restarted cleanly with the new imports (no import errors).
    - Direct curl tests against `/api/chat`: "what is due this week" correctly called `get_upcoming_assignments` and returned real Gemini prose naming the real DESG319 assignments with real dates, plus matching `assignments` chips; "tell me about the Tangible Interfaces course" correctly called `get_course_info` and reported the real 0 assignments / 52 resources; "what documents do you have for Service Design" correctly called `get_course_documents` and listed the real 6 indexed files; a made-up course name ("Quantum Basket Weaving 404") correctly triggered a real "not found" response listing the actual 7 real course names, with no fabricated assignment data — this is the specific anti-fabrication probe.
    - Empty message still 422s; `/api/courses`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/documents` still all 200 (no regression from the refactor).
    - **Real rate limit hit during testing** (not simulated): the free-tier Gemini quota is 5 requests/minute for `gemini-3.6-flash`. One test call during this session actually hit `429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, and the app's fallback path fired for real — the response came back from `deterministic_agent` with the honest "_(Gemini is temporarily unavailable — ...)_" prefix, proving the error-handling path works under a genuine failure, not just a mocked one.
    - Full Playwright pass on the running frontend: one real chat round-trip through the actual UI (typed a message, waited for the real network response, confirmed the rendered reply, chips, and sources matched the curl-tested behavior, zero console errors), then a final regression sweep of `/dashboard`, `/courses`, `/courses/1`, `/assignments`, `/documents`, `/settings` — zero console errors, zero failed `/api/*` requests. (Deliberately did not spam repeated chat requests through the UI beyond what was needed, to avoid exhausting the same 5-req/min free-tier quota being tested against.)
    - Confirmed `backend/.env` and `backend/venv/.env` are both still gitignored and show no changes in `git status` — the key was never written anywhere else, never logged, never included in any error message.
  - **Known limitations**: no conversation memory across HTTP requests (each message perceived fresh, same as before — a Gemini-side follow-up referencing earlier context in a *later* request won't resolve, though within a single request the model can still make multiple tool calls before answering); Gemini free-tier is rate-limited to 5 requests/minute, so realistic usage will occasionally hit the honest deterministic fallback rather than a real Gemini answer; the Gemini path doesn't generate `followUps` chips (the deterministic fallback still does) — left out rather than adding structured-output complexity for a nice-to-have.
- **Recommended next step**: if the 5 RPM free-tier limit is a real constraint, either upgrade the Gemini plan or add a short server-side cooldown/backoff before falling back, so occasional bursts of legitimate use don't trip it as often.

## 2026-09-17 (audited and rebuilt assignment-action-planner as a real Skill)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **Audit finding (reported before any changes, as asked)**: the existing `assignment_action_planner.py` did **not** qualify as a proper custom Skill. It was a solid, reusable *utility function library*, but: (1) no dedicated spec document existed anywhere describing its input/process/output/limitations; (2) it was scoped to a *list* of assignments, not "one real assignment → source-grounded action plan"; (3) it was invoked only indirectly, inline inside the `get_upcoming_assignments` tool body — nothing in the agent's loop or in Gemini's own function-call trace ever named it as a discrete step; (4) its output didn't cite the real submission URL / Moodle IDs it was derived from, so "source-grounded" wasn't actually true yet.
  - **`backend/api/skills/assignment_action_planner/SKILL.md`** (new) — the dedicated spec: scope (deliberately narrow to one real assignment), when the agent uses it vs. the batch path, input table (`assignment`, `course`, optional `now`), the 3-step process (classify urgency → attach recommendation → attach grounding), the exact `AssignmentActionPlan` output shape, and an explicit Limitations section. Also explicitly states it is **not** an MCP server and uses no MCP — it's an in-process Python function — per this task's instruction not to overclaim.
  - **`backend/api/skills/assignment_action_planner/__init__.py`** (new location, replacing the old flat `backend/api/assignment_action_planner.py`, which was deleted) — preserved every bit of existing logic verbatim (`classify_urgency`, the urgency-order/recommendation tables, `plan_actions`/`summarize_plan` for the batch "what's due this week" case — still used by `get_upcoming_assignments` and by `deterministic_agent.py`, both updated to import from the new path) and added the narrow single-assignment Skill contract: `plan_for_assignment(assignment, course, now=None) -> AssignmentActionPlan` (dataclass with `assignment_name`, `course_name`, `due_date`, `urgency`, `recommendation`, and a `grounding` sub-object holding the real `submission_url`/`moodle_assignment_id`/`moodle_course_id`) plus `render_plan()` for the human-readable form, which always ends with a `Source:` line citing the real submission link (or says plainly that none is recorded).
  - **`backend/api/matching.py`** — added `match_assignment(query, assignments)` (case-insensitive substring match against real assignment names), alongside the existing `match_course`, so the tool layer resolves a free-text mention like "the MVP assignment" to a real `Assignment` row before the Skill ever runs — the Skill itself still never accepts free text, only a resolved row.
  - **`backend/api/gemini_tools.py`** — added a fourth tool, `plan_assignment_action(assignment_query)`, whose entire body is: resolve the query to a real assignment via `match_assignment`, then call the Skill's `plan_for_assignment()` directly and return its fields (including the grounding block) to Gemini. This is the "explicit invocation within the multi-step loop" requirement: the tool is named after the Skill, appears by that name in Gemini's own function-call log, and — visibly to the frontend/API consumer — appends a `sources` entry literally labeled `"assignment-action-planner Skill"` whenever it fires, so its use is traceable in the actual API response, not just in backend logs.
  - **`backend/api/gemini_agent.py`** — system instruction updated to describe all four tools and added an explicit tool-choice rule: a question about *one named* assignment routes to `plan_assignment_action`, not `get_upcoming_assignments`.
  - **Frontend**: no changes needed or made. `ChatResponseOut`'s shape is unchanged, and `Chat.tsx`'s source-chip rendering (`message.sources.map(...)`) is already generic — it doesn't hardcode which labels to expect, so the new `"assignment-action-planner Skill"` label renders through the same code path already exercised and screenshotted in the prior session for `"Assignment Timeline"` / `"Course Catalog"` / `"Knowledge Base"`. Did not spend Gemini quota re-screenshotting this specific label given the code path is identical; verified by reading the component instead.
  - **Demonstration on a real assignment (two ways, per the task's request)**:
    1. **Direct, isolated invocation** — a throwaway script (scratchpad, deleted after) queried the real DB for the first `(Assignment, Course)` row and called `plan_for_assignment()` directly, with no Gemini or FastAPI involved. Real output: `assignment_name='Assessment 2: Custom Skill, an agent, 1-2 MCP, chained into one workflow'`, `course_name='DESG319-...-Introduction to Artificial Intelligence & Machine Lear'`, `due_date='2026-09-17T23:59:00'`, `urgency='due-soon'`, `grounding.submission_url='https://lms.flame.edu.in/mod/assign/view.php?id=246561'`, `grounding.moodle_assignment_id='246561'`, `grounding.moodle_course_id='24444'` — all values traceable to the real database row printed alongside it.
    2. **Through the full agent loop** — `curl -X POST /api/chat -d '{"message":"What should I do about the MVP assignment?"}'` correctly resolved to the *other* real assignment ("Assessment 3: MVP", not confused with Assessment 2), returned a Gemini-composed reply naming the real Sep 18 23:59 due date and the real submission link, and the response's `sources` array contained exactly `{"label":"assignment-action-planner Skill","courseName":"DESG319-..."}` — direct proof the agent explicitly invoked the Skill as its own step, not the batch path.
  - **Verification**: backend restarted cleanly with the new package structure (no import errors); `grep` confirmed zero remaining references to the old `backend/api/assignment_action_planner.py` path anywhere in the codebase; `npx tsc -b` on the frontend still clean (no contract change); re-confirmed `/api/courses`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/documents` still 200 and empty-message validation still 422 after the restructure — no regression.
  - **Real failure encountered while testing (not simulated)**: while probing the not-found path for the new tool, hit a **daily** Gemini free-tier quota (`GenerateRequestsPerDayPerProjectPerModel-FreeTier`, limit 20/day for `gemini-3.6-flash`) — tighter than the 5-requests-per-minute limit found in the previous session. The deterministic fallback fired correctly again, with the same honest labeled note. Stopped making further live Gemini calls once this was hit, rather than exhausting the day's quota further to force more screenshots; the two demonstrations above were captured before the limit was reached.
  - **Known limitations** (mirrors the SKILL.md): assignment name matching is substring-based, not semantic — an unusual paraphrase of an assignment name may fail to resolve and correctly report "not found" rather than guessing wrong; the Skill has no notion of assignment difficulty/effort, only time-based urgency; it is not and does not claim to be an MCP server.
- **Recommended next step**: if the 20-requests/day free-tier ceiling turns out to be a real constraint for demo/grading purposes, either enable billing on the Gemini project or add a small in-memory response cache for identical recent chat messages to avoid burning quota on repeated manual testing.

## 2026-09-17 (implemented the approved SQLite MCP integration)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Implemented server #1 from the approved audit — a real `mcp-server-sqlite` MCP server, connected over stdio with the official `mcp` Python SDK, chained into the existing Gemini agent's tool-calling loop alongside the four existing Python tools and the assignment-action-planner Skill (all preserved unchanged).
  - **Installation, verified before use, not guessed**: `pip install mcp` → `mcp` 2.2.0. Confirmed a real SQLite MCP server exists on PyPI via `pip index versions mcp-server-sqlite` (`2025.4.25`, real, not assumed) before installing it. Ran `venv/Scripts/mcp-server-sqlite.exe --help` and got real output confirming its only real option is `--db-path` (no read-only flag — noted immediately as a design constraint, not glossed over).
  - **Real compatibility bug found and fixed**: with `mcp` 2.2.0, the server crashed on startup — `AttributeError: 'Server' object has no attribute 'list_resources'` — because `mcp-server-sqlite` (last published April 2025) was written against an older `mcp` server API that 2.x removed. Read its `Requires-Dist: mcp[cli]>=1.6.0` constraint, checked `pip index versions mcp` for the full version list, and downgraded to `mcp==1.6.0` (the exact minimum the package declares) — resolved the crash. This is exactly the kind of thing "verify with --help" doesn't catch (the CLI help text was fine either way); it only surfaced by actually running the server, which is why the isolated bridge test below was run before touching any agent code.
  - **`backend/api/mcp_bridge.py`** (new): launches the real `mcp-server-sqlite.exe` subprocess via `mcp.client.stdio.stdio_client` + `StdioServerParameters`, connects with a `LoggingSqliteSession` (a real subclass of `mcp.ClientSession` — required, since `google-genai`'s own MCP support does `isinstance(tool, ClientSession)` on whatever's in the tools list, confirmed by reading `google/genai/_extra_utils.py` directly), and calls `session.initialize()`. Read-only enforcement is two independent layers, since the server itself has no read-only flag (its installed source, `mcp_server_sqlite/server.py`, was read directly and confirmed it also registers real `write_query`/`create_table`/`append_insight` tools):
    1. `LoggingSqliteSession.list_tools()` is overridden to strip every tool down to an allow-list of exactly `read_query`/`list_tables`/`describe_table` before `google-genai` ever sees the list — so Gemini is never told the write tools exist.
    2. Defense in depth: the server is never pointed at the live `backend/storage/esmerelda.db`. Each session copies it to a throwaway snapshot file and `os.chmod`s the copy read-only *before* the server process opens it, so even a call this bridge didn't intend to allow would fail at the sqlite3 driver itself, not just be asked nicely not to happen. `call_tool()` is also overridden to hard-refuse (raise `PermissionError`) any name outside the allow-list, and to record every call that *does* go through — server, tool, arguments, whether it errored, and a truncated result summary — into a shared `McpCallLog`, so nothing is logged unless a real `tools/call` request actually happened. The snapshot is remade fresh per session and deleted in a `finally` even if the caller raises.
  - **Isolated verification of the bridge itself, before wiring into Gemini** (per the task's explicit ask to verify before integrating): a throwaway script opened a real session, confirmed `list_tools()` returned exactly `['read_query', 'list_tables', 'describe_table']`, called `list_tables` (real result: the 5 real tables — `courses`, `assignments`, `resources`, `documents`, `document_versions`), called `read_query("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM assignments")` (real result: `[{'n': 2}]`), attempted `write_query` and confirmed it was correctly blocked with `PermissionError`, and confirmed the snapshot file existed during the session and was gone after. **Independently cross-checked** the count with a direct `sqlite3` connection to the *live* database (bypassing MCP entirely): `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM assignments` → `(2,)` — matches exactly.
  - **`backend/api/schemas.py`** — added `McpCallOut` (`server`, `tool`, `arguments`, `isError`) and an additive `mcpCalls: list[McpCallOut] = []` field on `ChatResponseOut`. Additive only — existing frontend code that doesn't read this field is unaffected (confirmed: `npx tsc -b` on the frontend still clean, no changes needed there).
  - **`backend/api/gemini_agent.py`** — `handle_chat_message_gemini` is now `async def`; opens the MCP session via `async with sqlite_mcp_session(call_log)`, passes `[*python_tools, sqlite_session]` into the same `GenerateContentConfig(tools=...)` the four existing tools already used, and switches to `client.aio.chats.create(...)` / `await chat.send_message(...)` (MCP calls are async — confirmed by reading `mcp/client/session.py`, `call_tool` is `async def`). The four existing tools and the assignment-action-planner Skill integration are byte-for-byte unchanged. System instruction extended with an explicit fifth capability (`read_query`/`list_tables`/`describe_table` via MCP) and an explicit rule to use it *only* for cross-cutting/aggregate questions the four fixed tools can't answer, never as a substitute for them. MCP-session-launch failures (subprocess/connection problems) are caught separately from Gemini-API failures and degrade gracefully to the four Python tools alone for that turn, rather than failing the whole request or masquerading as a Gemini outage.
  - **`backend/api/chat_agent.py`** / **`backend/api/routes.py`** — orchestrator and the `POST /api/chat` handler converted to `async def`/`await` to carry the async call through; the deterministic fallback (`deterministic_agent.py`, fully synchronous, untouched) is preserved exactly as before, triggered only on a real `GeminiUnavailableError`.
  - **Live testing — real evidence, including two genuine external failures encountered while testing (not simulated)**:
    - Restarted the backend cleanly (no import errors from the async/MCP changes).
    - **Test C (fallback/error), satisfied twice for real**: a single-assignment question and the cross-course aggregate question (see below) both returned a real `ServerError: 503 UNAVAILABLE` directly from Gemini's API on this run. The orchestrator correctly fell back to `deterministic_agent` both times, with the honest labeled prefix, and — critically — `"mcpCalls":[]` in both responses, correctly empty since no real MCP call happened when Gemini itself never got the chance to call it.
    - **Test A (cross-course aggregate) and Test B (single named assignment), attempted for real, blocked by a real, confirmed daily quota, not a code bug**: after the two 503s, direct SDK-level diagnostic probes (bypassing the app) isolated the cause precisely: (1) `client.aio.models.generate_content` with no tools succeeded immediately (`"OK"`) — proving the async client and the model itself were reachable; (2) `client.aio.chats.create(...)` with the four existing Python tools (still no MCP) reached the real API and got a real `ClientError 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` citing `GenerateRequestsPerDayPerProjectPerModel-FreeTier`, `limit: 20`, `model: gemini-3.6-flash` — the exact daily cap already flagged as a risk in the previous session's build log entry, now confirmed exhausted for today. This proves the async chat+tools request itself is well-formed and reaches Google's servers (a malformed request fails differently, before quota enforcement) — the block is external and real, not an integration bug. Per the task's instruction to use the minimum number of Gemini calls possible, further live attempts were stopped at this point rather than retried blindly.
    - **What this means concretely**: the MCP bridge itself — real subprocess, real session, real `call_tool()`, real result, independently verified against `sqlite3` — is proven working end to end. What was **not** captured live today is a single request where Gemini itself chose to call `read_query` and it round-tripped successfully through the full `/api/chat` endpoint — that specific combined event is blocked until the daily quota resets. This entry does not claim that event happened; it did not.
    - Independently pre-computed, via direct `sqlite3` against the live database, the real answers the two blocked test prompts should produce once quota resets: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM documents` → `34`; the highest per-course resource count → course id 6 (Tangible Interfaces) with `52`.
    - Confirmed no regression: `/api/courses`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/documents` still `200`; empty chat message still `422`.
  - **Known limitations**: the free-tier daily quota (20 requests/day for `gemini-3.6-flash`) is a hard ceiling that will keep blocking live demonstration until it resets or the project is upgraded — this is the single biggest practical risk to reliably grading/demoing this feature. `mcp-server-sqlite` (2025.4.25) is not being actively updated for newer `mcp` SDK releases (the exact incompatibility found today); pinning `mcp==1.6.0` is a real, working combination but means this project can't freely upgrade the `mcp` package without re-testing this server. The read-only guarantee is enforced by this bridge (allow-list + a chmod'd throwaway snapshot), not by the third-party server itself, which is why both layers exist.
- **Recommended next step**: once the daily quota resets, run exactly one `curl -X POST /api/chat -d '{"message":"How many total documents are indexed across every course?"}'` and confirm `mcpCalls` is populated with a real `read_query` call and the reply states `34` — the specific missing piece of live evidence, with everything else already in place and verified.

## 2026-09-17 (found and fixed a real MCP dispatch bug; live confirmation still blocked by quota)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What happened, in order, with real evidence for each step**:

  1. **17:40 IST / restart** — restarted the backend cleanly (`uvicorn main:app --app-dir venv --port 8000`), confirmed `/health` returned 200.

  2. **Attempt 1 — 2026-09-17T12:10:22Z** — sent exactly the requested one targeted prompt:
     `"How many total documents are tracked across all courses? Use the SQLite MCP tool to verify the answer."`
     Real response: `"reply": "_(Gemini is temporarily unavailable — KeyError: 'read_query' — ... deterministic fallback instead.)_ ..."`, `"mcpCalls": []`.
     This was **not** a quota error — `KeyError: 'read_query'` is a Python dict lookup failure, and critically it names the *exact real tool* the MCP bridge exposes. That specific detail is what made this worth investigating rather than just recording as "MCP not called" and moving on, per the instruction to investigate rather than blindly retry.

  3. **Investigation (zero additional Gemini calls)** — read `google/genai/chats.py`, `_extra_utils.py`, and `_adapters.py` directly (all already installed locally) to trace the async `Chat.send_message()` AFC path:
     - `chats.py`'s `AsyncChat.send_message()` calls `parsed_config = method_config.model_copy(deep=True)` on the `GenerateContentConfig` — which includes our live `LoggingSqliteSession` object inside `tools`.
     - `_extra_utils.get_function_response_parts_async()` later does a bare `func = function_map[func_name]` (no `.get()`) when dispatching the model's chosen tool call — the exact line that would raise `KeyError('read_query')` if the map built after the deep-copy doesn't agree with the tool declarations built before it.
     - **Reproduced the failure locally, with zero Gemini API calls**: a standalone script built a real `GenerateContentConfig(tools=[live_session])` against our actual `mcp_bridge.sqlite_mcp_session()` and called `.model_copy(deep=True)` on it directly. It raised, verbatim: `TypeError: cannot pickle '_asyncio.Future' object`, several stack frames deep inside `copy.deepcopy()` recursing into the live session's internal anyio task-group state. This is a structural incompatibility between Pydantic's `model_copy(deep=True)` and any live, open `mcp.ClientSession` — not specific to our subclass, not a prompt/tool-selection issue, and not something `--help` or a package-listing check could ever have caught.
  4. **Fix (no architecture change)** — added `__deepcopy__(self, memo): memo[id(self)] = self; return self` to `LoggingSqliteSession` in `backend/api/mcp_bridge.py`. This is the standard Python idiom for a live resource object that must never be duplicated by a "copy" (the same pattern used for things like open file handles or `threading.Lock`). It makes Pydantic's deep-copy return the *same* live session instance instead of trying to clone its internals, which also guarantees the tool declarations and the dispatch table are built from the identical object.
  5. **Verified the fix locally, again with zero Gemini calls**: reran the same standalone repro script. `model_copy(deep=True)` no longer raised; `copied_tool is session` → `True`; `isinstance(copied_tool, ClientSession)` → `True`; and calling `list_tools()` on the "copied" (identical) object still correctly returned `['read_query', 'list_tables', 'describe_table']`.
  6. **Attempt 2 (confirmation, not a blind retry) — 2026-09-17T12:14:29Z** — restarted the backend with the fix and sent the same prompt once, specifically to confirm the fix live, not to re-probe blindly. Real response: `"reply": "_(Gemini is temporarily unavailable — ClientError: 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED ... GenerateRequestsPerDayPerProjectPerModel-FreeTier ... limit: 20 ... model: gemini-3.6-flash ..._)"`, `"mcpCalls": []`. This is a **real, confirmed daily-quota exhaustion** (same quota metric as previous sessions' entries), not a code failure — the two app-level calls made today (the KeyError one and this one) were apparently enough to close out the remaining daily allowance.
  7. **Stopped here.** Per the instruction not to repeatedly retry, no further live Gemini calls were made after the second 429.

- **mcpCalls evidence, both attempts**: `[]` in both cases — correctly and honestly empty in both. In attempt 1, the model selected the tool but the SDK bug meant `call_tool()` was never actually reached. In attempt 2, Gemini's API rejected the request before any tool-calling could occur. Neither attempt should show and neither did show a fabricated or partial `mcpCalls` entry.
- **Independent sqlite3 verification (ground truth, unchanged from the prior entry, bypassing MCP entirely)**: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM documents` on the live `storage/esmerelda.db` → `(34,)`. **Not claiming this number was returned by Gemini or by MCP today** — it wasn't observed live. This is only the independently pre-verified answer the feature should produce once it's actually exercised successfully.
- **Regression check**: `/api/courses`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/documents` still all `200` after the fix; no frontend changes were needed (the fix is entirely inside `mcp_bridge.py`).
- **Files changed**: `backend/api/mcp_bridge.py` only — added the `__deepcopy__` override to `LoggingSqliteSession`. No other files touched; architecture unchanged, as instructed.
- **Honest current status**: the MCP bridge itself is proven correct in isolation (prior entry) and the specific bug that broke the Gemini-driven path has been found, root-caused with a reproducible local test, fixed, and the fix has been verified locally — but **a live, successful "Gemini calls read_query and gets a real answer back through /api/chat" has still not been observed**, solely because of the real daily quota ceiling, confirmed twice today with matching error payloads. This entry does not claim that event happened.
- **Known limitation reconfirmed**: 20 requests/day on the free tier for `gemini-3.6-flash` is proving to be the single largest practical obstacle to finishing this demonstration, not the code.
- **Recommended next step**: once the daily quota resets, send exactly one more `curl -X POST /api/chat -d '{"message":"How many total documents are tracked across all courses? Use the SQLite MCP tool to verify the answer."}'` and check for a populated `mcpCalls` array containing a real `read_query` call. If it appears and the reply states `34`, this closes out the feature with full live evidence; if `mcpCalls` is still empty, capture the exact error again before any further changes.

## 2026-09-17 (integrated Groq as primary LLM provider, Gemini demoted to secondary)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: ~[fill in]
- **What shipped**: Added Groq as a real second LLM provider with a hand-rolled multi-step tool-calling loop, made it primary (Gemini is now the secondary fallback, not removed), and added a local test suite — all without a single live Groq or Gemini API call, per the explicit instruction to avoid unnecessary API usage during development.
  - **Inspected first, confirmed before writing anything**: re-read `chat_agent.py`, `routes.py`, `schemas.py`, `gemini_tools.py`, `mcp_bridge.py`, `gemini_agent.py` in full. Confirmed `groq` 1.7.0 was already installed in `backend/venv` and `backend/.env` already had `GROQ_API_KEY` and `GROQ_MODEL=openai/gpt-oss-20b` set (checked the model name only, never the key). Read the Groq SDK's own generated types (`chat_completion_tool_param.py`, `chat_completion_message_tool_call.py`, `chat_completion_tool_message_param.py`, `function_definition.py`) directly to confirm its OpenAI-compatible tool schema and response shape rather than assuming it matches Gemini's. Confirmed `mcp.types.Tool` already exposes `.name`/`.description`/`.inputSchema` as a real JSON Schema, so MCP→Groq tool conversion needed no guessing. Confirmed `pytest` isn't installed, so the new tests follow the project's existing plain-script convention (`backend/storage/test_storage.py`) instead of adding a new test-framework dependency.
  - **`backend/api/groq_tools.py`** (new) — pure schema/dispatch conversion, no tool logic duplicated:
    - `python_tool_defs()` — hand-written Groq `FunctionDefinition` schemas for the same four callables `gemini_tools.build_tools()` already returns, matched by function name (raises loudly if a name doesn't have a schema, rather than silently dropping a tool).
    - `mcp_tool_defs()` — converts real `mcp.types.Tool` objects (from a real `session.list_tools()` call) directly into the same schema shape — nothing hand-written here, always in sync with `mcp_bridge.py`'s read-only allow-list.
    - `ToolDispatch`/`build_dispatch()` — a name → `"python"` / `"mcp"` / `"unknown"` router built from the real tool lists, used by the loop to decide how to execute each `tool_call`.
  - **`backend/api/groq_agent.py`** (new) — `run_tool_loop()` is the hand-rolled perceive→reason→act→observe→iterate loop Groq requires (unlike `gemini_agent.py`, which delegates this to `google-genai`'s built-in automatic function calling): call Groq → if the response has no `tool_calls`, done → otherwise parse each `tool_call.function.arguments` (a JSON string — malformed JSON from the model is caught and fed back as an `{"error": ...}` tool result, not a crash) → dispatch Python-tool calls directly (the *same* closures Gemini uses, so they populate the *same* `ToolCollector` as a side effect — the assignment-action-planner Skill via `plan_assignment_action` is exercised identically) → dispatch MCP-tool calls through the *real*, already-existing `mcp_bridge.sqlite_mcp_session()` / `LoggingSqliteSession` (read-only allow-list and call logging reused as-is, not reimplemented) → append each result as a `{"role": "tool", ...}` message → repeat, capped at `MAX_ITERATIONS = 5` (returns an honest "couldn't finish" reply if hit, never loops forever or crashes). `run_tool_loop()` takes an injected `create_completion` callable specifically so it can be exercised in tests with zero network calls. `handle_chat_message_groq()` wires it to a real `AsyncGroq` client (`_get_client()`, `GROQ_API_KEY`/`GROQ_MODEL` read from `os.environ` only — no hardcoded model name, unlike `gemini_agent.py`'s literal `MODEL` constant) and returns the exact same `ChatResponseOut`/`McpCallOut` shape Gemini does. The MCP `ClientSession` object itself is never handed to Groq — only the plain JSON-schema dicts `groq_tools.py` builds from it; execution against the session happens entirely inside `run_tool_loop`.
  - **`backend/api/chat_agent.py`** — extended from a 2-tier to a 3-tier fallback chain: Groq (primary) → Gemini (secondary, only tried if Groq raises `GroqUnavailableError`) → deterministic agent (final safety net, only if both LLM providers fail). Every tier is labelled honestly in the reply.
  - **Real bug found and fixed while writing the fallback chain, caught by the new test suite before ever reaching a live endpoint**: the first version did `except GroqUnavailableError as groq_exc: pass`, then referenced `groq_exc` in an f-string several lines later. Python deletes `as`-bound exception variables at the end of their `except` block — this raised a real `UnboundLocalError` the first time the fallback-chain test ran (both Groq and Gemini faked as unavailable). Fixed by copying `str(exc)` into a plain local variable (`groq_reason`/`gemini_reason`) before the `except` block ends. Re-ran the test suite after the fix — clean.
  - **`backend/tests/test_groq_agent.py`** (new — first `tests/` directory in the backend). Plain asyncio/assert script, run via `venv/Scripts/python.exe tests/test_groq_agent.py`. Covers all 6 requested areas, **10/10 real checks passing**, zero Groq/Gemini network calls:
    1. Groq tool schema conversion — both the 4 Python-tool schemas and the 3 real MCP-tool schemas (from a real `list_tools()` call) come back with the right names and a `parameters` JSON Schema.
    2. Tool-call parsing — a faked Groq response with a real `tool_calls` entry gets executed and its real result correctly appended as a `role: "tool"` message; a second case confirms malformed JSON in `function.arguments` is caught and reported as an error, not a crash.
    3. MCP tool routing — a faked Groq response naming `read_query` gets routed through the *real* MCP session; the reply contains the real count (`34`, matching every independent `sqlite3` check earlier in this log) and `McpCallLog` recorded exactly one real call.
    4. Write operations blocked — `write_query` is confirmed `"unknown"` to the dispatcher (never even offered to Groq as a tool, since it was already filtered out by `LoggingSqliteSession.list_tools()`), and calling it directly through the real session still raises `PermissionError` as defense in depth.
    5. Maximum iteration protection — a fake Groq client that *always* returns a tool call, never a final answer, is confirmed to stop at exactly `max_iterations` real loop turns and return the safe fallback text, not hang or crash.
    6. assignment-action-planner Skill — `plan_for_assignment()` still runs correctly against a real DB row and still returns real grounding (the real `submission_url`).
    7. (bonus, not separately numbered in the requirement but explicitly requested) Fallback behavior — the real bug above was caught here.
  - **Verification beyond the test suite**: backend restarted cleanly (no import errors from the new modules); full regression sweep of every existing endpoint (`/api/courses`, `/api/courses/1`, `/api/courses/999` → 404, `/api/assignments`, `/api/documents`, `/api/documents/34/download`, `/api/sync-status`, `/api/dashboard/summary`, empty chat message → 422, `/health`) — all matched expected status codes; frontend `npx tsc -b` still clean — the `/api/chat` contract didn't change, so **no frontend files were touched**.
  - **Groq/Gemini API calls made during this entire implementation: zero**, by design — every test either exercises real local code (MCP subprocess, database, Skill) or fakes the LLM response/raises client-side before any HTTP request would be built.
  - **Known limitations**: none of this has been exercised against the *real* Groq API yet — the tool schemas, the loop mechanics, and the routing are all proven correct against faked responses shaped exactly like the real SDK's types, but a live call could still surface a real-world surprise (e.g. Groq's actual tool-calling behavior for this specific model, `openai/gpt-oss-20b`, choosing tools differently than a synthetic test expects). This entry does not claim a live Groq call has succeeded, because none was made.
- **Recommended next step**: with explicit approval, run exactly one live Groq smoke-test call (e.g. the same "how many total documents..." aggregate question used for the Gemini MCP demonstration) to get real end-to-end confirmation, same one-call discipline used throughout the Gemini/MCP work.

## 2026-09-17 (live Groq smoke test — real success, first live confirmation)
- **Time spent**: ~[fill in]
- **Rough tokens used**: not captured — see "Token usage" below.
- **What happened, exactly one real request, no retries**:
  1. Confirmed `GROQ_API_KEY` loads from `backend/.env` via `python-dotenv` without printing it: `GROQ_API_KEY loaded: True | length: 56 | prefix: gsk_...`.
  2. Restarted the backend fresh (`uvicorn main:app --app-dir venv --port 8000`) to rule out any state carried over from the earlier local test suite (which had monkeypatched `_get_client` in a separate, already-exited process — never the live server — but restarted anyway for a clean run). Confirmed `/health` → 200 first.
  3. Sent **exactly one** request, timestamp **2026-09-17T12:46:52Z**:
     ```
     POST /api/chat
     {"message":"How many total documents are tracked across all courses? Use the SQLite MCP tool and verify the result."}
     ```
  4. **Real response, HTTP 200, captured in full**:
     ```json
     {
       "reply": "There are **34** documents tracked across all of your courses.  \nI confirmed this by querying the underlying SQLite database: `SELECT COUNT(*) AS total_documents FROM documents;` returned 34.",
       "assignments": [],
       "courses": [],
       "documents": [],
       "sources": [],
       "followUps": [],
       "mcpCalls": [
         {"server": "sqlite", "tool": "list_tables", "arguments": {}, "isError": false},
         {"server": "sqlite", "tool": "read_query", "arguments": {"query": "SELECT COUNT(*) AS total_documents FROM documents;"}, "isError": false}
       ]
     }
     ```
  - **This is the first live confirmation in this project that an LLM (Groq, in this case) actually selected and successfully called the real MCP tool end to end.** Groq's own reasoning called `list_tables` first (checking the schema before querying — not instructed to do that specifically, a real, sensible tool-use decision) and then `read_query` with a real, correct `SELECT COUNT(*)` statement. No fallback text appears anywhere in the reply — Groq answered directly as the primary provider, on the first attempt, no retry needed.
  - **Backend log for this request**: `INFO: 127.0.0.1:51563 - "POST /api/chat HTTP/1.1" 200 OK` — one line, one request, no errors, no retries.
  - **Independent verification via `sqlite3`, bypassing MCP entirely**:
    ```
    conn.execute('SELECT COUNT(*) AS total_documents FROM documents').fetchone()
    → (34,)
    ```
    **Matches exactly.** The `34` in Groq's reply and in both `mcpCalls` arguments is the real, independently-confirmed number — not fabricated, not assumed.
  - **Fallback/error information**: none. Neither the Groq nor the Gemini fallback path was triggered; `chat_agent.py`'s primary tier (`handle_chat_message_groq`) succeeded outright.
  - **Token usage**: not available. The current `groq_agent.py` implementation does not read or log `response.usage` from the Groq completion object, so no real token count was captured for this call. Not fabricating a number — this is a genuine gap in the current implementation's observability, not a value that was measured and omitted.
- **Recommended next step**: if per-request token/cost visibility is wanted, add `response.usage` capture (Groq's `ChatCompletion.usage` — `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens`/`total_tokens`) to `groq_agent.py`'s `create_completion` closure and log or surface it, the same way `mcpCalls` is already surfaced.
