## 2026-09-18 (Fix: repeated Moodle syncs failed after the first — login success was judged by URL, not by real page state)

- **The reported bug**: the very first sync with a given set of credentials succeeded; every sync after that failed with `"Moodle did not accept the configured credentials (still on the login page after submitting)."`, with the credentials genuinely unchanged.
- **Investigated each of the six specific questions asked, in order**:
  1. **How is the browser/context created for every sync?** `run_sync()` opens a brand-new `sync_playwright()` context and calls `p.chromium.launch(headless=True)` **fresh on every call** — never reused across calls, never `launch_persistent_context()`. Confirmed by reading the code, not assumed.
  2. **Are cookies/localStorage/session state/browser profiles reused?** Before this fix, `run_sync()` called `browser.new_page()`, which uses Playwright's *implicit default context* on that `Browser` object rather than an explicitly-created one. Since `browser` itself is a brand-new process every call, this still could not leak state *between* `run_sync()` calls — but it also meant isolation was never made explicit or provable. **This is now made explicit and directly tested**: `run_sync()` calls `browser.new_context()` before `context.new_page()`, and the new regression test (`tests/test_sync_service.py`, checks 5/5b/5c) runs two fully independent `_login()` sessions against a real local server and asserts the server saw **two real login POST requests**, not one login followed by a reused-cookie freebie — proving, not just arguing, that no session state crosses between syncs. **Conclusion: this was not the actual bug**, but the code is now more explicit and provably isolated regardless.
  3. **Is the login page cached or stale?** `page.goto(moodle_url, wait_until="domcontentloaded")` is a real navigation on a brand-new page/context every time; Playwright's default context has no HTTP cache pre-seeded, and nothing in this codebase configures Playwright's cache behavior at all. No evidence this was a factor.
  4. **Does Moodle redirect to a different URL after login?** **Yes — this turned out to be the actual root cause.** The pre-fix success check was `page.wait_for_url(re.compile(r"^(?!.*login).*"), timeout=20000)` — "wait until the URL stops containing the substring 'login'." Real Moodle installs commonly carry a `wantsurl`/`returnurl`/session query parameter through the post-login redirect chain, and that carried-through URL can itself still contain the substring "login" (e.g. if the page the user was trying to reach before being bounced to the login form was itself login-adjacent, or if Moodle's session-key-refresh step temporarily revisits a URL under `/login/`) even though the login **genuinely succeeded**. A URL-substring check cannot tell "still failed to log in" apart from "logged in, but momentarily on a URL that happens to contain the word login" — it was guessing, not verifying.
  5. **Does Moodle present a different page/form on subsequent logins?** Plausible in general (a "remember this device" prompt, a forced password-change notice, a consent screen shown once) but not confirmed either way — no live Moodle access in this environment. The fix (see below) is robust to this regardless, because it no longer depends on knowing Moodle's exact page/redirect shape in advance.
  6. **Does the code determine success ONLY by URL?** **Yes, exactly as suspected — this was the bug.** `_login()` had exactly one success signal (the URL regex above) and exactly one failure signal (that regex timing out). No DOM element was ever inspected to check whether the login form was actually gone, whether a logged-in-only element was actually present, or whether Moodle had displayed an explicit login-error message.
- **The fix — replaced the URL-only check with real DOM-based verification**:
  - Login success is now judged by `_is_logged_in(page)` — the presence of a real logged-in-only DOM element (`a[href*='/login/logout.php'], .usermenu`, standard across Moodle's default theme) — **and** the absence of the login form (`_has_login_form(page)`), never the URL. The URL is now used only for diagnostic logging, never as evidence.
  - If neither a logged-in marker nor the login form is found after waiting, or if Moodle's own login-error element (`#loginerrormessage, .loginerrors, .alert-danger`) is present, `_login()` raises `MoodleLoginError` with the **real, DOM-derived error text Moodle displayed** (when there is one) instead of a generic guess — genuinely more actionable for diagnosing a real future failure, and still never includes the password.
  - Replaced the single `wait_for_url()` race with `page.wait_for_load_state("networkidle", timeout=15000)` (best-effort — a timeout here is not itself treated as failure, the DOM checks after it are the real verdict) so Moodle's redirect chain has time to settle before those DOM checks run.
  - `run_sync()` now explicitly opens `browser.new_context()` (previously relied on `browser.new_page()`'s implicit default context) and always closes both `context` and `browser` in a single `finally` block, even if `context` creation itself partially failed (guarded by `if context is not None`).
- **Diagnostic logging added** (`_log_diagnostics()`, called at three points: `before_login`, `immediately_after_submit`, `after_waiting_for_redirect`) — logs, via the standard `logging` module under logger name `esmerelda.moodle_sync`: the current URL, the page title, whether the login form is present, whether the logged-in marker is present, and the text of any Moodle-displayed login/error message. **Never logs**: the username, the password, cookies, tokens, or any other session data — confirmed by grepping the file for every use of `password`/`username`, which are only ever passed to `_get_credentials()`'s return tuple and `page.fill()`, never to `logger.info()` or any string formatting.
- **New regression test** (`tests/test_sync_service.py`, section 5): a real, minimal local HTTP server (stdlib `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer`, no external test dependencies added) that behaves like a Moodle login flow — a login form on `GET`, a session cookie + redirect on a correct `POST`, a logged-in-only marker element once authenticated, and a real login page again on an incorrect `POST`. The real `_login()` function is run against it, twice, as **two fully independent Playwright browser launches** (mirroring `run_sync()`'s exact browser/context lifecycle) — exactly the reported scenario: sync → successful login → context/browser closed → sync again → successful login. Also verifies a genuinely wrong password still raises `MoodleLoginError` rather than a false success. **5/5 new checks passed** (12 pre-existing + these = **16/16** in this file).
- **Verification**:
  - `tests/test_sync_service.py`: **16/16 passed**, including the new regression test — a real headless Chromium was launched twice against a real local server in this run, not mocked.
  - Re-ran the other three suites: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9) — **44/44 total, no regressions.**
  - `main.app` still imports cleanly.
  - `git status` confirms only `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` and `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py` changed this step — the database, frontend, and sync-scheduling/trigger logic (`api/routes.py`, `storage/models.py`, `storage/crud.py`) were not touched, per the instruction to leave them alone unless required for the authentication fix itself (they were not required).
- **What's still not verified**: this fix has not been run against the real, live FLAME Moodle site — no live credentials or network access exist in this environment. The DOM selectors used (`.usermenu`, `#loginerrormessage`, etc.) are standard Moodle-theme defaults reused from this project's own existing, human-verified interactive scripts (`moodle/browser.py`'s "Only courses in progress"/"Course overview" boundary logic reasoning, in spirit) but the exact FLAME Moodle theme's real markup has not been independently confirmed to use these exact classes. The diagnostic logging added in this step is specifically there to make the *next* real failure (if any) immediately diagnosable from logs alone, without needing to reproduce it interactively.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`.

## 2026-09-18 (Temporary diagnostic instrumentation for the still-unresolved Render login failure — no fix, observation only)

- **Why**: the previous entry's fix (judging login success by real DOM markers instead of the page URL) did not resolve the failure on Render — the exact same error persists there. Per instruction, this step makes **no further assumptions about the cause** and instead adds rich, sanitized, production-safe diagnostic capture so the *actual* page state Render sees after submitting the login form can be inspected directly, rather than guessed at again.
- **What shipped — all in `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, clearly marked as a temporary, diagnostic-only block**:
  - `_capture_login_diagnostics(page, stage, *, username, password, response_status=None)` — records one full, sanitized snapshot: URL, page title, whether the login form is present, whether `.usermenu` is present, whether a `/login/logout.php` logout link is present (**tracked as three separate booleans, not one combined signal** — exactly per instruction not to treat `.usermenu` alone as proof of anything), any Moodle login-error element's text, the HTTP response status of the post-submit navigation (via `page.expect_navigation()` wrapped around the click), a full list of every form's action/method/id and every input's tag/type/name/id (**never the input's `value`**), and a sanitized dump of the page's visible text.
  - `_login()` now calls this at exactly the three requested moments: `"before_submit"` (right after the initial `page.goto()`), `"immediately_after_submit"` (right after `page.click("#loginbtn")` returns, capturing the post-submit response status), and `"after_5_10s_wait"` (after a best-effort `wait_for_load_state("networkidle", timeout=8000)` followed by an explicit, unconditional `page.wait_for_timeout(7000)` — so this snapshot reflects a real ~7-8s-later window regardless of whether networkidle resolved instantly, per the requested 5-10 second range).
  - **Sanitization** (`_sanitize_text()`): exact-string replacement of the real `MOODLE_USERNAME`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD` values wherever they'd appear in captured text, regex redaction of anything email-shaped, and regex redaction of any 20+-character opaque alphanumeric run mixing letters and digits (the shape of a session id/sesskey/CSRF token) — deliberately erring toward over-redacting ordinary long strings rather than risking a real token slipping through, since this is a diagnostic dump, not user-facing output. Cookies and HTTP headers were never captured in the first place (only `page.locator("body").inner_text()` and DOM attribute reads) — there was nothing to redact there because nothing cookie/header-shaped is ever read.
  - **Deliberately does not change any success/failure decision** — `_login()`'s actual `return`/`raise` control flow is byte-for-byte the same logic as the previous entry's fix (DOM-marker-based, not URL-based). This step only *records* facts; per instruction, it does not conclude that a "login"-containing URL means failure, and does not conclude that `.usermenu` alone means success — both are reported as independent, unopinionated booleans for a human to read.
  - **`get_last_login_diagnostics()`** — a module-level accessor returning the most recent `_login()` attempt's full list of (already-sanitized) snapshots, held in a plain in-process list, cleared at the start of every `_login()` call.
  - **`GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics`** (new, temporary, in `backend/api/routes.py`) — returns `{"diagnostics": [...]}` from `get_last_login_diagnostics()`, so these snapshots can be pulled from a live Render instance via a normal HTTPS request, without needing shell or log access there. Imports `moodle.sync_service` lazily inside the route function, matching the existing pattern for `POST /api/sync/moodle` (so a process without Playwright installed still starts up fine — this endpoint would just always return an empty list on such a process, since no `_login()` call could ever have happened there).
  - Removed the now-superseded `_log_diagnostics()` helper (its 3-field logging is fully subsumed by `_capture_login_diagnostics()`) rather than leaving dead code behind.
- **Explicitly did NOT touch**: the database (`storage/models.py`, `storage/crud.py`, no schema/migration change — this step needed none), the frontend (no file under `frontend/` touched), and the sync-trigger/scheduling logic (`POST /api/sync/moodle`'s own body, `SyncRun` handling) — confirmed via `git status`, which shows only `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/api/routes.py` (the one new temporary route), and `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py` changed.
- **Verification**:
  - **New checks in `tests/test_sync_service.py`** (section 5a-diag, 4 new checks): re-used the existing real-local-HTTP-server + real-headless-Chromium regression harness from the previous entry to prove the diagnostic capture itself actually works, not just that it doesn't crash — asserts all 3 stages were captured in the right order; asserts the real test username/password appear **nowhere** in the full captured diagnostics (checked across every field, not just the obvious ones); asserts every captured form field is genuinely missing a `value` key by construction; asserts a real HTTP response status was captured for the post-submit navigation. **20/20 passed** in this file (16 from the previous entry + 4 new).
  - **Exercised the new endpoint directly, in-process, against the same real local fake-Moodle server**: ran a real `_login()` call, then called `get_moodle_login_diagnostics()` (the route function itself, not just the underlying accessor) and confirmed it returns exactly 3 stages in the right order, and that the serialized string of the *entire* response contains neither the real test username nor password anywhere. Also confirmed calling it with **no prior login attempt** in the process returns `{"diagnostics": []}` cleanly, not an error.
  - Re-ran the other three suites: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9) — **48/48 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly.
- **What this step does NOT tell us yet — and could not, from this environment**: this instrumentation has not run against Render or against the real Moodle site. **No conclusion is drawn here about why Render differs from local** — that was explicitly out of scope for this step. The next action is operational, not a code change: deploy this build to Render, trigger `POST /api/sync/moodle` once, then call `GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` (or read the Render service's log stream, which receives the same `[moodle-login-diagnostic] stage=...` lines) to see the three real, sanitized snapshots of what Render's headless Chromium actually encountered. That real output — not a further guess from this environment — is what should drive the next fix.
- **Removal note**: this entire block (the diagnostic-capture functions in `sync_service.py`, the temporary endpoint in `routes.py`, and the corresponding test checks) is intended to be removed once the Render/local discrepancy is understood and a real fix is in place — flagged clearly in both files' docstrings/comments so it isn't mistaken for permanent infrastructure.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/api/routes.py`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`.

## 2026-09-18 (Debugging the diagnostic pipeline itself — two real infrastructure bugs found and fixed, no login-algorithm change)

- **The reported symptom**: Render reports each Moodle sync as completed/failed, but `GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` always came back with no recorded data — as if `_login()`'s diagnostic capture (added last entry) never ran, or never reached the endpoint. Per instruction, this step debugged the pipeline itself — route registration → background task execution → capture → retrieval — before touching login logic again, and the login algorithm (`_login()`'s success/failure decision) is **byte-for-byte unchanged** from the previous entry.
- **Two real bugs found, both fixed — neither was a login-logic problem**:
  1. **The diagnostic state was only ever held in a plain Python list in process memory** (`_last_login_diagnostics`). A `POST /api/sync/moodle` request and a later `GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` request are two separate HTTP requests — if Render runs more than one container instance/replica behind a load balancer, or restarts/recycles the instance between requests (both are normal, unremarkable Render behavior, not a misconfiguration), those two requests can land on two different OS processes with completely separate Python memory. Neither request would ever see the other's data, no matter how correct the capture logic itself was. This matches the reported symptom exactly: the sync genuinely runs (hence "completed/failed" is reported correctly, since that status **is** database-backed via `SyncRun`), but the diagnostics, being memory-only, vanish the instant the request that captured them ends.
  2. **This app's own loggers were never actually reaching output under the real production run command.** Verified directly, not assumed: started the real server with `uvicorn main:app` (no `--log-config`, matching `backend/Dockerfile`'s `CMD` exactly) with no other change, and confirmed by inspecting the server's actual log file that **zero** `esmerelda.*`-logger output appeared anywhere — not the previous entry's `[moodle-login-diagnostic]` lines, nothing — even though the underlying code was genuinely executing (confirmed via the API's own correct responses). Uvicorn configures handlers for its own `uvicorn`/`uvicorn.error`/`uvicorn.access` loggers but never attaches one to the root logger that every other logger (including this app's) falls through to. This means every one of this change's new `[BUILD VERSION]`/`[SYNC DEBUG]` log lines, and the previous entry's diagnostic log lines, would have been **silently invisible in Render's log stream** regardless of whether the rest of the sync/diagnostic code was working — a second, independent reason "check the logs" wouldn't have revealed anything on Render.
- **Fixes**:
  1. **Database-backed diagnostics, replacing the in-memory-only approach** — `storage/models.py`'s `SyncRun` gained two new columns: `login_diagnostics` (JSON-serialized list of snapshots) and `login_diagnostics_captured_at`. `storage/database.py`'s existing idempotent, `PRAGMA`-guarded migration helper (from an earlier entry) was extended to add these to any pre-existing `sync_runs` table. `storage/crud.py` gained `record_login_diagnostic(run_id, snapshot)` (appends and persists immediately — not batched until the sync finishes, so a mid-sync `GET` can see a snapshot the moment it's captured) and `get_latest_login_diagnostics()` (finds the most recent `SyncRun` that actually captured something, not necessarily the latest run overall — a run that failed before reaching `_login()` at all, e.g. missing credentials, correctly doesn't hide an earlier run's real captures). Since the database is the one thing every process/instance genuinely shares (it's what makes `SyncRun`/courses/assignments consistent across requests already), this fixes the cross-process visibility problem regardless of exactly how many Render instances/processes are involved — the code no longer needs to know or assume anything about Render's process topology.
  2. **`main.py`** now calls `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format=...)` at import time — a no-op if the root logger is already configured, so this is safe regardless of how the app is eventually run. Re-verified after adding this: the exact same real-server test that previously showed zero output now shows every expected log line, in the right order, with real timestamps.
- **The other 10 investigation/instrumentation items, all completed**:
  1. **Confirmed the route is genuinely registered** — not by reading source, by *introspecting the real running `FastAPI` app object*. Found and fixed a related bug while doing this: a naive `route.path` scan of `app.routes` returns an empty list on this FastAPI version (0.141.1), because it wraps an included `APIRouter`'s routes in an internal `_IncludedRouter` object with no `.path` of its own — the real sub-routes live on `.original_router.routes`. Caught this directly (the naive scan printed `[]` even though the routes definitely exist) before it shipped as a misleading "no sync routes found" startup log. `main.py`'s new `_find_sync_routes()` handles both shapes.
  2. `[BUILD VERSION] Moodle diagnostic build <marker>` — added as `main.py`'s `lifespan()` startup log, reading `moodle/sync_service.py`'s new `DIAGNOSTIC_BUILD_MARKER` constant (`moodle-diagnostic-build-2026-09-18-v2`), import guarded so a Playwright-less deployment still starts cleanly.
  3. `[SYNC DEBUG] Moodle sync task entered` — added as the literal first line of `api/routes.py`'s `_run_moodle_sync()`, before even the lazy `moodle.sync_service` import.
  4. `[SYNC DEBUG] entering _login` — added in `run_sync()` immediately before the `_login(...)` call.
  5. `[SYNC DEBUG] _login returned` — added immediately after; a parallel `[SYNC DEBUG] _login raised: <type>: <message>` was also added in the exception handler, since a failed login obviously never reaches the "returned" line and that path needed its own visibility too.
  6. `[SYNC DEBUG] capturing diagnostic snapshot: stage=<stage> run_id=<id>` — added as the first line of `_capture_login_diagnostics()`, so all three snapshot captures are announced before the (already-existing) detailed `[moodle-login-diagnostic]` line that follows each one.
  7. **The diagnostic endpoint's response shape** now includes `hasCapture`, `syncRunId`, `capturedAt`, and `buildMarker` (the same constant from item 2, so a caller can directly compare what's running against what the startup log printed) alongside `diagnostics`.
  8/9. **Confirmed there is exactly one copy each of `sync_service.py` and `routes.py` in the entire repository** (`find . -iname "sync_service.py"` / `"routes.py"`, excluding `venv/`/`node_modules/`) — no duplicate import paths exist to explain stale code running. Whether the *deployed Docker image* actually contains the latest versions of these files cannot be confirmed from this environment (no Docker access, unchanged limitation from earlier entries) — the build marker (items 2 and 7) exists specifically so this can be confirmed from Render's own logs/API response instead.
  10. **Confirmed the background task runs in the same process** as the API request handler under this Dockerfile's actual `CMD` (`uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port ${PORT:-8000}` — no `--workers` flag, so a single Uvicorn process/single event loop; Starlette's `BackgroundTasks` runs a sync callable via a thread pool *within that same process*, confirmed by inspecting the Dockerfile directly, not guessed). The real risk was never "a separate process for this one container" — it's **separate container instances/replicas**, which Render can run without any Dockerfile change on this project's part, and which fix 1 above (database-backed storage) makes correct regardless of.
- **Verification — full, real, end-to-end proof of the exact chain the task asked for**, run locally with a real headless Chromium against a real local HTTP server (`http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer`) simulating Moodle, driven entirely through real HTTP requests to a real running `uvicorn main:app` process (not calling Python functions directly):
  - `POST http://127.0.0.1:.../api/sync/moodle` → real `202 {"runId": 4, "state": "syncing"}`.
  - Waited for the real background task to run for real, then `GET http://127.0.0.1:.../api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` → real `200` with `"hasCapture": true, "syncRunId": 4, "capturedAt": "2026-09-18T14:41:16..."`, and all 3 real, correctly-ordered, sanitized snapshots (`before_submit` → `immediately_after_submit` → `after_5_10s_wait`), including the real captured `response_status: 200` on the post-submit navigation.
  - The server's real log output, inspected directly, shows every requested line in the correct order: `[BUILD VERSION] ...` at startup, `[SYNC DEBUG] Moodle sync task entered (run_id=4)`, `[SYNC DEBUG] entering _login`, three `[SYNC DEBUG] capturing diagnostic snapshot: stage=...` lines each immediately followed by its `[moodle-login-diagnostic] build=... stage=...` detail line, and `[SYNC DEBUG] _login returned`.
  - This is the strongest available proof, short of Render itself, that `POST /api/sync/moodle → background task → _login() → diagnostic capture → GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` genuinely uses the same running application/state — demonstrated via the database, across two independent HTTP requests, exactly as the architecture requires it to work on Render regardless of process topology there.
  - **New checks in `tests/test_sync_service.py`**: none added this step beyond what already exists — the existing regression harness's calls to `_login()` don't pass `run_id` (defaults to `None`, which correctly falls back to the in-memory-only path, unchanged from the previous entry), so all 20 existing checks continue to validate the capture logic itself without needing a real database. The new database-backed path was verified via the real end-to-end HTTP run above instead, since it specifically needed a real `SyncRun` row and a real second HTTP request to prove — which is a stronger proof than a mocked unit test would have been for this specific question.
  - Re-ran the full suite: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (20/20) — **48/48 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly. The local `sync_runs`/`assignments` tables were re-migrated cleanly (`login_diagnostics`/`login_diagnostics_captured_at` columns added via the existing idempotent `ALTER TABLE` helper).
- **Explicitly not touched**: `_login()`'s success/failure decision logic (the actual login algorithm) — confirmed unchanged from the previous entry, per instruction. No frontend files touched (confirmed via `git status`).
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/main.py`, `backend/api/routes.py`, `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/storage/models.py`, `backend/storage/database.py`, `backend/storage/crud.py`.
- **What this step still cannot tell us**: whether Render's real login failure is a genuine credential/Moodle-side issue or something about Render's specific network/environment — that question is now finally *answerable*, since the diagnostic pipeline itself is proven working end-to-end and database-backed. The next action is the same operational one as before, now actually capable of succeeding: deploy this build to Render, trigger a real sync, and read `GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` (which will now survive across requests/instances) or the log stream (which will now actually show output) to see the real captured Render-side login state.

## 2026-09-19 (Login confirmed working on Render — traced and fixed three real post-`_login()` bugs, none of them authentication)

- **Starting point, confirmed by the user from real Render production logs**: `_login()` genuinely succeeds — `url=https://lms.flame.edu.in/my/`, `usermenu_present=True`, `logout_link_present=True`, `login_form_present=False`, `[SYNC DEBUG] _login returned`. Per instruction, `_login()`'s authentication logic was **not touched** in this step (confirmed via `git diff` — the function body between its signature and the next function has zero changed lines).
- **Traced the complete post-`_login()` path and instrumented every requested stage** (all in `moodle/sync_service.py`'s `run_sync()`/`_detect_active_courses()`/`_sync_course_resources()`/`_sync_course_assignments()`): `_login` returned → dashboard/current page loaded → active-course discovery started → active courses discovered (count + names) → course traversal started → each course URL visited → sections/topics discovered per course → resources discovered per course → assignments discovered per course → database persistence started → course/assignment/resource written to DB (individually, per item) → database commit completed → **`Moodle sync summary: courses_discovered=X assignments_discovered=X resources_discovered=X courses_persisted=X assignments_persisted=X resources_persisted=X`** (exact requested format) → sync completed.
- **Three real, concrete bugs found and fixed while adding this instrumentation — none of them in `_login()`**:
  1. **The actual, highest-confidence root cause of "sync completes but nothing meaningful happens after login": `_sync_course_assignments()` navigated to the bare relative path `"dashboard/"`.** Playwright's `page.goto()` resolves a relative path against whatever page it's called from — after `_sync_course_resources()` has already navigated to the last course visited (e.g. `.../course/view.php?id=123`), `"dashboard/"` resolves to `.../course/dashboard/`, a URL that doesn't exist on real Moodle (whose actual dashboard is `/my/` — confirmed directly from the user's own production diagnostic log at the top of this entry). The old code's `except Exception: pass` around that `goto()` call meant this landed silently on a 404-shaped page, found no Timeline block, and returned `0` assignments — with login having genuinely succeeded and active courses genuinely found. **Fixed**: `_sync_course_assignments()` now takes `moodle_url` (the real, absolute site root, the same one passed to `_login()`) and navigates to `urljoin(moodle_url, "my/")` instead — an explicit, absolute URL, immune to whatever page was visited last.
  2. **A second, related bug, only surfaced by building a real end-to-end test (see below) rather than reasoning about the code alone**: `link.get_attribute("href")` returns Moodle's raw HTML attribute exactly as rendered — not guaranteed absolute, unlike a real browser click or an anchor's `.href` DOM property (which browsers resolve automatically). `page.goto()` requires an absolute URL and raises `Cannot navigate to invalid URL` on a bare relative one. This was hit for real, immediately, the first time the new end-to-end test used a course link written as a relative href — proving this isn't a hypothetical: it's exactly what would happen against any real Moodle page that ever emits a relative internal link. **Fixed**: every href extracted from a link that's later used for navigation or persistence (`_detect_active_courses()`'s course URLs, `_sync_course_resources()`'s resource URLs, `_sync_course_assignments()`'s assignment URLs) is now resolved with `urljoin(moodle_url, href)` before use.
  3. **A filter gap, also only caught by the end-to-end test**: `_sync_course_resources()`'s ignore-list didn't exclude `/login/logout.php` — the "Log out" link present in `.usermenu` on every logged-in page was being discovered and persisted as a fake `"Activity"` resource on **every course, every sync**. Fixed by adding it to `_IGNORED_HREF_FRAGMENTS`.
- **Checked whether any scraping function is defined but never called (explicit instruction)**: grepped every function definition against its call sites — `_detect_active_courses`, `_course_moodle_id`, `_classify_resource_type`, `_sync_course_resources`, `_extract_due_date`, `_fetch_submission_status`, `_sync_course_assignments` — every one has exactly one definition and at least one real call site. **This was checked and found NOT to be the problem** — the three bugs above are all in *how* the already-called functions behave, not in something never being invoked.
- **Exceptions are no longer silently swallowed**: every `except Exception: continue`/`except Exception: pass` in the post-login path (`_detect_active_courses`'s per-link loop, `_sync_course_resources`'s per-link loop, `_sync_course_assignments`'s per-Timeline-entry loop and its dashboard-navigation fallback) now logs the real exception type and message (`type(exc).__name__`, `exc`) via `logger.warning(...)`/`logger.error(...)` before continuing — the loops still don't abort on one bad item (a single malformed link shouldn't kill the whole sync), but nothing vanishes without a trace anymore. The outer `except Exception` in `run_sync()` (anything unexpected surviving to that point) now calls `logger.exception(...)` — a full traceback — before wrapping it into `MoodleSyncError`; safe to do because none of the functions between `_login()` returning and this point ever receive `username`/`password` as parameters, so there is nothing credential-shaped a traceback from this section of code could contain.
- **Discovered vs. persisted counts are now tracked separately and accurately** — `SyncResult` gained `courses_discovered`/`assignments_discovered`/`resources_discovered` alongside the existing `courses_synced`/`assignments_synced`/`resources_synced` ("persisted") fields. This also fixed a real latent accuracy bug: the old code incremented its "synced" counter immediately after *calling* `save_resource()`/`save_assignment()`, regardless of whether that call actually returned a saved row — `storage/crud.py`'s `save_resource()`/`save_assignment()` both return `None` (logging their own reason) when the referenced course can't be found, which the old counting silently treated as a success anyway. Persisted counts are now only incremented when the save call's return value is not `None`.
- **Verification — a genuinely new, real end-to-end test, not just unit tests of pure logic**: `tests/test_sync_service.py`'s new section 6 runs the real `run_sync()` — login, active-course discovery, course traversal, resource sync, and assignment sync, the entire pipeline — against a real local fake-Moodle server that now serves a dashboard, a course page, and a Timeline block (not just a login form, as the previous entry's server did). Run inside a subprocess with `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` pointed at an isolated temp directory (both to avoid writing fake test data into the real local development database, and because `storage/database.py` reads `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` into a module-level `DATABASE_URL` at import time — setting the env var after `moodle.sync_service` is already imported in the *test* process would have no effect). This test is what caught bugs 2 and 3 above — they were invisible to reasoning about the code and invisible to the existing unit tests, and only surfaced by actually running the real pipeline. Checks: the subprocess exits `0`; the course/resource/assignment are each discovered **and** persisted (1/1 each, not just "attempted"); every one of the 16 requested stage log lines appears in the real captured output; the exact `Moodle sync summary: courses_discovered=1 ...` line appears with every requested field name; neither the real test username nor password appears anywhere in the subprocess's output. **8 new checks, all passing.**
  - Also fixed, while building this test: `logging.basicConfig()`'s default output stream is `stderr`, not `stdout` — the first version of this test only checked `proc.stdout` for the log markers and failed, because the `[SYNC DEBUG]`/`[moodle-login-diagnostic]` lines were genuinely being logged, just to the other stream. Fixed the test to check `stdout + stderr` combined (matching how a real log viewer like Render's captures both), and additionally pinned `main.py`'s `logging.basicConfig(..., stream=sys.stdout)` explicitly, removing any ambiguity about where these lines land in production.
- **Full suite re-run**: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (28/28 — 20 existing + 8 new) — **56/56 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly.
- **Explicitly not touched**: `_login()`'s authentication logic (confirmed via `git diff` — zero changed lines inside that function's body), the database schema (no new migration needed this step), and the frontend (no file under `frontend/` touched).
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/main.py` (explicit stdout logging stream), `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (all three bug fixes + full stage instrumentation), `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py` (new end-to-end section 6).
- **What this step still cannot tell us**: whether the real FLAME Moodle's actual HTML happens to already avoid the relative-href and stale-relative-dashboard-path issues found here (i.e., whether bugs 1/2 were ever actually hit in the specific Render run the user is investigating, versus some other post-login issue this instrumentation hasn't surfaced yet). The fixes are real and verified against a realistic simulated Moodle regardless, and the comprehensive stage-by-stage logging now added means the next real Render sync attempt will show, in the logs, exactly which of these 16 stages it reaches and where (if anywhere) it still stops — the concrete next step is the same operational one as before: trigger a real sync on Render and read the log stream (or `GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics` for the login portion specifically) for the real `Moodle sync summary:` line.

## 2026-09-19 (Resources missing from production sync — root cause traced to an unjustified `is_visible()` filter, plus a real defensive fix for multi-page course displays)

- **The reported symptom**: assignments/deadlines sync correctly in production, but resources/documents never do. Since assignments come entirely from the dashboard Timeline block (`_sync_course_assignments`, unchanged this step — confirmed via `git diff`, zero lines changed in that function) while resources come from visiting each course's own page (`_sync_course_resources`), the bug had to be specific to the course-page resource path, not authentication, not active-course discovery, and not the assignment path.
- **Investigated in the order requested, ruling things out with evidence rather than guessing**:
  1. **Active-course discovery** (`_detect_active_courses`) — unchanged and already correct from the previous entry's fix; still resolves hrefs to absolute URLs via `urljoin`. Not the cause: if this were broken, courses/assignments would also fail, and they don't.
  2. **Course page/resource link extraction** — this is where the real bug was. Read `_sync_course_resources` line by line and found `if not link.is_visible(): continue` gating every candidate link.
  3. **Absolute-URL conversion (`urljoin`)** — already correctly applied to resource hrefs since the previous entry. Confirmed still in place, not the cause.
  4. **File-type handling (PDF/DOCX/pages/URLs/folders/Moodle file resources)** — checked `_RESOURCE_TYPE_BY_HREF` directly: `.pdf`→PDF, `.doc`→Word Document (also matches `.docx` as a substring), `/mod/page/`→Page, `/mod/url/`→Link, `/mod/folder/`→Folder, `/mod/resource/`→Resource. All six requested types were already correctly handled — **confirmed by test, not assumed** (new check 6k explicitly classifies all five extension/path-based types and asserts the correct label for each). This was not the bug.
  5. **Persistence into the `resources` table** — `storage.crud.save_resource()` (unchanged, already idempotent from an earlier entry) was already being called correctly wherever a resource passed the filters — the problem was never in persistence, it was that almost nothing was ever reaching persistence in the first place.
- **Root cause, proven empirically, not assumed**: constructed a real headless-Chromium page (`page.set_content(...)`, no mocking) with one link inside a normal `<div>` and a second, identical link inside a `<div class="collapse" style="display:none;">` — the exact CSS pattern Moodle's default Boost-family themes use for a course's non-current/collapsed topics or sections (rendered fully in the server HTML, then hidden via CSS until a user clicks to expand — not lazy-loaded, so the link and its href are completely real, just currently hidden). Playwright's own `link.is_visible()` returned `True` for the first link and **`False`** for the second, confirmed directly in this session before writing any fix. Since assignments are read from the dashboard Timeline block — never subject to a course page's own section-collapse state — this exactly explains why assignments succeeded while resources, gated by `is_visible()`, silently dropped anything inside a collapsed section (which, on a real course page, is very plausibly *most* of the page — only the current week/topic is typically expanded by default).
- **A second, related, defensively-fixed risk found during the same investigation**: Moodle course formats can be configured to show "one section per page" (`course/view.php?id=X&section=N` as separate pages) rather than all sections on one page. In that configuration, scanning only the course's main URL would only ever see section 0 (general/announcements), regardless of the `is_visible()` fix, silently missing every week/topic's resources. Not confirmed to be happening on the actual production Moodle (no live access), but a real, plausible, and cheap-to-handle configuration this code should not silently mishandle — added detection and traversal of same-course `section=` links.
- **Fixes — all in `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`**:
  1. Extracted the per-page link-scanning logic into a new `_scan_page_for_resources()` helper (used for both the main course page and any additional section pages) with the `is_visible()` filter **removed** — a resource's existence doesn't depend on whether a human currently has its section expanded, so this can only ever discover more real resources, never fewer or wrong ones.
  2. Added `_find_additional_section_pages()` — after scanning the main course page, looks for other same-course `...&section=N` links, resolves them absolutely, dedupes, caps at 20 per course (`_MAX_SECTION_PAGES_PER_COURSE`), and `_sync_course_resources()` now visits each one with the same scanning helper, accumulating into the same `discovered`/`persisted`/`failed` counts and the same `seen_urls` dedupe set (so a resource linked from two section pages isn't double-counted). On a normal single-page course (confirmed still the common/default case), this list is empty and behavior is unchanged.
  3. **Requirement 6 (clear logging for discovered/skipped/failed/persisted)**: every link that doesn't pass a filter now logs which specific filter and why (`reason=no_name_or_href` / `reason=ignored_fragment` / `reason=ignored_text`); every persistence attempt logs success or `NOT persisted` with the reason already available from `storage/crud.py`; every exception during a link's processing is now labeled `resource link FAILED` (previously just "skipped", conflating a filtered link with an actual error) and tracked in a new `failed` counter, logged in the final per-course summary line (`resources discovered for '<course>': discovered=X persisted=X failed=X`) alongside the existing `Moodle sync summary:` line's `resources_discovered`/`resources_persisted` fields (unchanged field names from the previous entry).
- **What was explicitly NOT changed, confirmed via `git diff`**: `_sync_course_assignments`, `_login`, `_detect_active_courses`'s core logic, `storage/crud.py`, `storage/models.py`, and the frontend — this step's diff touches only `_sync_course_resources` and its two new helper functions in `moodle/sync_service.py`, plus its tests.
- **Verification**:
  - **New test infrastructure**: extended `tests/test_sync_service.py`'s existing full-pipeline fake Moodle server (section 6) with a course page that now serves three real resources: one normally visible, one inside a real `display:none` collapsed `<div>` (the actual reported bug, reproduced), and one on a separate `course/view.php?id=101&section=1` page (the multi-page-display defensive case) — plus a matching handler route for that second section page.
  - **Check 6d**: all 3 resources are discovered **and** persisted (`discovered=3, persisted=3`) — previously, with the old `is_visible()` filter, this exact fixture would have found only the one visible resource and silently dropped the other two.
  - **Check 6i**: the collapsed-section resource's real name (`Collapsed Section Handout.pdf`) appears in the sync's own logged output, not just in an aggregate count.
  - **Check 6j**: the multi-page section page is genuinely detected and visited, and its resource (`Week 2 Readings`) appears in the output.
  - **Check 6k**: all 5 extension/path-based resource type classifications (PDF, Word Document, Page, Link, Folder) return the correct label.
  - **Checks 6f/6g** (inherited from the previous entry, re-verified): all 16 stage log lines and the exact `Moodle sync summary:` line still appear correctly, now with the updated `resources_discovered=3`/`resources_persisted=3` values.
  - Full suite re-run: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (31/31 — 28 existing + 3 new) — **59/59 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (`_sync_course_resources` rewritten around two new helpers, `_scan_page_for_resources` and `_find_additional_section_pages`), `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py` (extended fake server + 3 new checks).
- **What this step still cannot tell us**: whether the real FLAME Moodle course pages actually use collapsed sections, a multi-page display setting, both, or neither — no live access exists in this environment to confirm which (if either) mechanism was actually in play on Render. Both fixes are real, evidence-backed, and strictly additive (neither can discover fewer or wrong resources than before), so the next real Render sync should show a marked increase in `resources_discovered`/`resources_persisted` in the `Moodle sync summary:` log line if either mechanism was the actual cause — that log line, now with `discovered`/`persisted`/`failed` broken out per course, is the concrete next thing to check.

## 2026-09-19 (Regression: resource fix broke production assignment sync — reproduced, root-caused, and fixed without reverting the resource fix)

- **1. Compared the latest commit against the previous known-working commit.** `git log` showed the resource-sync fix landed as commit `132419b` ("Moodle document fetch"), on top of `9062f38`. `git diff 9062f38 132419b -- Esmerelda/backend/moodle/sync_service.py` shows the entire diff is contained to `_sync_course_resources` and its two new helpers (`_scan_page_for_resources`, `_find_additional_section_pages`) — a clean, isolated diff.
- **2. Confirmed `_sync_course_assignments()` was truly unchanged.** The diff hunk list contains zero lines touching that function, and a direct `grep`/diff search for `_sync_course_assignments` or `def run_sync` in that same diff returns nothing — `run_sync()`'s call sequence and `_sync_course_assignments()`'s body are byte-for-byte identical between the two commits. The regression could not be a direct code change to the assignment path; it had to be a side effect.
- **3. Checked whether the new resource traversal, multi-page navigation, shared Playwright `page`, or exception handling affects assignment scraping.** Confirmed `_sync_course_assignments()` navigates via its own absolute `urljoin(moodle_url, "my/")` call regardless of whatever page the shared `page` object was left on by resource sync, so simple page-state carryover was ruled out. What is real and provable: the new multi-page-section traversal (up to `_MAX_SECTION_PAGES_PER_COURSE` = 20 extra page loads *per course*) meaningfully increases the number of requests and total time a sync takes compared to before — a real, quantifiable increase in exposure to anything that can invalidate a session mid-sync (Moodle-side session expiry, request-volume-based throttling, etc.), even though this project cannot directly confirm from here which specific mechanism is happening on Render.
- **4. Reproduced the regression directly, before writing any fix** (per explicit instruction — not claimed without reproduction): built a standalone fake Moodle server that serves a valid session for the first several requests, then unconditionally serves the login page for every request after — simulating a session that dies partway through a sync. Ran the real `run_sync()` against it. Result, captured directly from real log output:
  ```
  [SYNC DEBUG] navigating to dashboard for assignment discovery: http://127.0.0.1:8896/my/
  [SYNC DEBUG] dashboard/current page loaded: url=http://127.0.0.1:8896/my/ title=''
  [SYNC DEBUG] no Timeline block found on http://127.0.0.1:8896/my/ — 0 assignments discovered
  (this does NOT mean zero courses; it means the Timeline block wasn't present/matched on this page)
  Moodle sync summary: courses_discovered=1 assignments_discovered=0 resources_discovered=1 ...
  ```
  **This is the exact regression**: `page.url` still shows `/my/` (Moodle's login redirect target for that path, in this simulation), so nothing about the URL or the generic "Timeline block not found" message reveals that the sync actually landed on a login form instead of the real dashboard. The sync reported **success** with `assignments_discovered=0` — silently wrong, not visibly failed.
- **The root cause, precisely**: `_sync_course_assignments()` had no way to distinguish "genuinely on the dashboard with no Timeline block" from "got bounced back to the login page" — both looked identical to its only check (`timeline.count() == 0`). This code path existed *before* the resource fix too, but the resource fix's added page traversal made hitting it in practice meaningfully more likely by increasing per-sync request volume and duration, without the assignment path having any way to report the difference honestly.
- **The fix — does not revert the resource fix; makes the actual gap it exposed correct on its own terms**:
  1. **`_sync_course_assignments()`** now checks `_has_login_form(page)` (the same, already-proven DOM signal `_login()` itself uses) immediately after navigating to the dashboard. If present, it raises a specific `MoodleSyncError` ("Session was lost before assignment discovery could run...") instead of silently returning `(0, 0)`. This turns a silently-wrong "success" into a loud, correctly-diagnosed failure — exactly what should happen when a sync genuinely didn't complete as intended. Resources already persisted earlier in the same sync are **not** rolled back or lost — `storage/crud.py`'s `save_resource()` commits per-call immediately, so real, already-discovered data survives; only the `SyncRun`'s own status is now honestly marked as failed instead of a misleadingly-successful zero.
  2. **`_sync_course_resources()`'s multi-page-display probe is now bounded across the whole sync, not attempted per-course.** `run_sync()` now checks the multi-page-display probe only on the *first* active course (`probe_multi_page_display=True`); if that course shows no additional section pages, every subsequent course in the same sync skips the probe entirely (`should_probe_multi_page` set to `False`). Course-display mode is a site-wide/course-format-wide Moodle setting, not something that varies course-by-course within one install, so this bounds the worst-case added request volume to "at most one course pays this cost" instead of every course — directly reducing the exposure identified in step 3, without removing the feature or its ability to actually find a real multi-page-display course when one exists.
- **Verification — reproduced the regression fixed, not just reasoned about**: re-ran the exact same session-expiry fake-server scenario after applying the fix:
  ```
  [SYNC DEBUG] landed on Moodle's login form while navigating to the dashboard for assignment discovery —
  the session was lost sometime after _login() succeeded ...
  CLEAN ERROR RAISED (expected): Session was lost before assignment discovery could run ...
  ```
  Confirmed the resource discovered earlier in that same run (`Lecture Notes.pdf`) was still logged as `resource written to DB` before the failure — proving no data loss, only an honestly-reported failure state.
- **5. New permanent regression test** (`tests/test_sync_service.py`, section 7, 4 checks): extends the existing full-pipeline fake server with a module-level toggle that force-invalidates the session as soon as the main course page has been visited once (robust to exactly how many requests resource sync happens to make, rather than a fragile hardcoded request count) — simulating "session died sometime during/after resource sync" without depending on the precise mechanism. Runs the real `run_sync()` via subprocess (same isolated-`ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` pattern as section 6) and asserts: a `MoodleSyncError` is raised (not a silent success with 0 assignments); its message clearly names session loss, not a generic cause; the resource discovered before the session died was genuinely persisted; and neither the real test username nor password appears anywhere in the subprocess's output.
- **Full suite re-run**: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (35/35 — 31 existing + 4 new) — **63/63 total, no regressions**, including section 6's existing checks confirming resources (3/3, collapsed-section + multi-page cases) and assignments (1/1) both still succeed together in the normal case. `main.app` still imports cleanly.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (`_sync_course_assignments`'s login-form detection; `_sync_course_resources`'s and `run_sync()`'s multi-page-probe bounding), `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py` (new section 7).
- **What this step still cannot tell us**: which exact mechanism caused the session loss on the real Render/Moodle production run — Moodle-side session timeout, request-volume-based throttling, or something else entirely. This wasn't required to fix the actual gap (a sync that loses its session should never silently report a misleadingly-successful zero, regardless of why the session was lost), and the bounding fix in item 2 reduces the specific, provable risk this change introduced without requiring that root mechanism to be identified. The next real Render sync will now either succeed with real assignment counts, or fail loudly and specifically with "Session was lost before assignment discovery could run" — either outcome is immediately actionable from the logs/`SyncRun.error_message`, unlike the previous silent-zero behavior.

## 2026-09-19 (Root cause found: all 8 real Timeline candidates rejected by an English-text assumption; a second, masked bug found while fixing it)

- **Production evidence given**: `courses_discovered=7`, `resources_discovered=205`, `resources_persisted=205` (all healthy), `Timeline block found 8 candidate "is due" links`, `assignments_discovered=0`, `assignments_persisted=0`. Confirmed the bug is entirely inside `_sync_course_assignments()`, after Timeline candidates are found — course discovery, resource discovery, login, and DB persistence were not touched by this investigation or its fix.
- **Inspected the exact candidate-collection code** (`_sync_course_assignments()`'s `links = timeline.locator("a"); link_count = links.count()`): this counts **every** anchor inside the Timeline block, not specifically "is due" links — the log line's own wording ("candidate 'is due' link(s)") was already misleading before any of the real filtering happens. The actual accept/reject decision happened per-candidate, inside the loop.
- **Added the requested rich, unconditional per-candidate logging first** (visible text, href, `outerHTML` snippet, immediate parent text, and the exact rejection reason) — logged for every candidate regardless of outcome, before any filter runs, specifically so the *next* real production run makes this fully diagnosable from logs alone without needing to reproduce it interactively.
- **Root cause #1, found by comparing the filter logic against Moodle's actual `core_calendar` Timeline template shape (not assumed)**: `_sync_course_assignments()` required the literal English phrase `"is due"` to appear inside the candidate `<a>` tag's *own* `inner_text()` (`if not text or not re.search(r"\bis due\b", text, re.I): continue`, checked before the href was even read). Moodle's real Timeline event-name link only ever contains the activity's own title (e.g. "Assessment 2: ...") — the due date/time is rendered as *separate*, non-link text nearby (a `<small>`/date element), and no "is due" phrase is rendered into the link's text in current Moodle versions at all. This single, silent, English-text-only requirement is sufficient on its own to reject **100% of real candidates, every time**, regardless of how many the Timeline genuinely contains — exactly matching the reported 8-found/0-survived symptom.
- **Fix #1 (the smallest necessary — does not remove href flexibility, does not require the old pattern)**: a Timeline link is now accepted as a real assignment candidate if **either** its resolved href contains `/mod/assign/` (Moodle's canonical, theme/version/locale-independent assignment-view URL — a routing convention, not a display string) **or** its own text still says "is due" (kept for backward compatibility with older Moodle/theme combinations, never required). Per instruction 6, this explicitly does *not* newly assume a fixed href shape either — `/mod/assign/` is checked as a substring of the already-existing, already-correct `urljoin()`-resolved href, and the "is due" path remains available as an alternative, not a requirement.
- **Also removed the `is_visible()` gate on Timeline candidates** — the same class of bug already found and fixed in this project's resource-sync work: Moodle's Timeline groups events into date-range tabs ("Overdue", "Next 7 days", etc.) implemented as Bootstrap tab-panes, where only the active tab's pane lacks `display:none`. A real assignment sitting in a currently-inactive tab is exactly as real as one in the active tab; `is_visible()` was never a valid signal for whether a Timeline entry exists, only for whether a user is currently looking at that particular tab.
- **Root cause #2, found only by actually reproducing the fix, not by static reading — a second, pre-existing bug this fix exposed**: after fix #1 let real candidates through, this project's own reproduction hit a genuine 30+ second hang per remaining candidate once the *first* real assignment was processed. Traced it precisely: `_fetch_submission_status(page, href)` navigates the *shared* `page` object away from the Timeline (`/my/`) to the assignment's own page — but the (pre-existing, unmodified) code kept iterating `links.nth(i)` for subsequent candidates against a `links` locator still scoped to the Timeline page Playwright had already navigated away from. Each such call doesn't fail cleanly — it retries, waiting for a matching element that no longer exists on the new page, until Playwright's own default action timeout. **This bug pre-dates this change entirely** and was never triggered in production only because root cause #1 discarded every candidate before any of them ever reached the submission-status fetch.
- **Fix #2**: split `_sync_course_assignments()`'s single loop into two passes. Pass 1 reads every candidate's name/href/due-date/course-name from the Timeline page into a plain Python list — no navigation happens in this pass. Pass 2 iterates that already-collected list and, for each one, fetches submission status (navigating `page` away, now safe since nothing after this point re-queries the Timeline's own DOM) and persists it. This is the minimum restructuring needed to make multiple real Timeline assignments — not just one — actually complete without hanging.
- **Verification — reproduced the failure and the fix directly, not just asserted from unit tests (per explicit instruction)**:
  - Built a reconstructed fake Moodle server whose Timeline markup matches the real `core_calendar` template shape described above: one assignment link with plain activity-name text (no "is due") in an active tab-pane, and a second in an inactive (`display:none`) tab-pane. Ran the real `run_sync()` against it with the pre-fix code — confirmed, from real log output, that both candidates were rejected under the (then-accurate) `reason=not_an_assignment_link`/invisible-link path, `assignments_discovered=0`.
  - Applied fix #1 alone first — confirmed the *first* candidate now discovered and written to the database (`[SYNC DEBUG] assignment written to DB: ... 'Full Pipeline Test Assignment'`), but the run then hung for 30+ seconds before eventually failing on `Browser.close: Connection closed while reading from the driver` — this is root cause #2, caught by actually running the fix, not by reasoning about the code alone.
  - Applied fix #2 (the two-pass split) — re-ran the same scenario: both assignments (the plain-text one and the inactive-tab one) discovered **and** persisted, no hang, real log line `Moodle sync summary: courses_discovered=1 assignments_discovered=2 resources_discovered=1 courses_persisted=1 assignments_persisted=2 resources_persisted=1`.
- **New/updated permanent tests** (`tests/test_sync_service.py`, section 6 — the existing fixture was itself rebuilt, since it had the exact same wrong "is due"-text assumption the buggy code did, meaning it was validating the assumption rather than reality):
  - `_FULL_MY_PAGE_HTML` rebuilt to match the real Moodle Timeline template shape: plain activity-name link text (no "is due"), a separate due-date element, and two Bootstrap-style tab-panes (one active, one `display:none`) — directly modeling the actual production candidate structure this step investigated (fulfilling instruction 9's fixture requirement).
  - **6e** (updated): both assignments — one from the visible tab with no "is due" text, one from the inactive tab-pane — are discovered **and** persisted (previously asserted 1/1 against the old, wrong fixture; now 2/2 against the corrected one).
  - **6l** (new): the plain-text assignment (no "is due" phrase) is discovered by name, proving identification no longer depends on that English phrase.
  - **6m** (new): the inactive-tab-pane assignment is discovered by name, proving the `is_visible()` removal specifically.
  - **6n** (new): every logged Timeline candidate includes real diagnostic fields (`text=`, `href=`, `parent_text=`), not just an aggregate count.
  - `6g`'s exact `Moodle sync summary:` field-name assertions updated to the new `assignments_discovered=2`/`assignments_persisted=2` counts.
- **Full suite re-run**: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (38/38 — 35 existing/updated + 3 new) — **66/66 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly. `git diff` confirms every changed hunk is contained inside `_sync_course_assignments()` — course discovery, resource discovery, login handling, and the multi-page resource probe are byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (`_sync_course_assignments` only), `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`.
- **Exact root cause**: (1) candidate identification required the literal English phrase "is due" inside the Timeline link's own text, which real Moodle does not render there at all — silently discarding every real candidate; (2) a pre-existing, previously-unreachable bug where fetching submission status navigated the shared page away from the Timeline mid-loop, causing every candidate after the first to hang once candidates started actually being processed.
- **Exact code change**: candidate acceptance now checks `href` for `/mod/assign/` (OR the old "is due" text, kept as a fallback) instead of requiring "is due" text alone; the `is_visible()` gate on Timeline links was removed; the single processing loop was split into a DOM-reading pass and a separate persist/fetch-submission-status pass.
- **Test result**: 66/66 passed across all four test files, including a rebuilt, realistic Timeline fixture and 3 new checks proving both a "no is due text" assignment and an "inactive tab-pane" assignment are discovered and persisted, with no hang.

## 2026-09-19 (Knowledge Base was empty: the document-download pipeline existed but was never wired into the automated sync — connected it without rewriting either side)

- **Report requested before implementing, answered by direct inspection, not assumption**:
  - **Where documents are currently created**: only inside `moodle/document_downloader.py`'s `process_resource()` (calls `storage.crud.save_document()`), itself only ever called from that same file's `main()`.
  - **What calls `document_downloader.py`**: nothing in the automated pipeline. `grep -rn "document_downloader"` across the whole backend found exactly two non-self references — a comment in `api/presenters.py` and a comment in `storage/paths.py` — zero real call sites anywhere in `api/routes.py` or `moodle/sync_service.py`. The only way it ever ran was a human executing `python -m moodle.document_downloader` directly, which itself calls `moodle.browser.get_authenticated_page()` — the separate, interactive, visible-browser, human-clicks-login manual flow, entirely disconnected from the new automated `_login()`.
  - **Whether the new sync calls it**: no. `moodle/sync_service.py`'s `run_sync()`/`_sync_course_resources()` persist `Resource` rows via `storage.crud.save_resource()` and never import or reference `document_downloader.py` at all, before this change.
  - **Exact reason documents stayed at zero**: the automated `POST /api/sync/moodle` pipeline has a complete, working path for courses/assignments/resources, but zero code path that downloads a file or creates a `Document`/`DocumentVersion` row — that logic exists, complete and already correct, only in the separate, never-invoked `document_downloader.py`.
- **The smallest correct integration — reused, did not rewrite, either side**:
  - Confirmed `document_downloader.py`'s `process_resource(page, resource)` already accepts *any* authenticated Playwright `page` as a parameter — it was already fully reusable from an automated context; the only thing missing was something to call it with the automated session and a broader resource selection.
  - **`moodle/document_downloader.py`** (additive only — `main()`, `process_resource()`, `inspect_resource()`, `download_file()`, `calculate_file_hash()`, `safe_filename()`, `get_filename_from_url()`, `get_downloadable_resources()` are all untouched):
    - `_DOWNLOADABLE_RESOURCE_TYPES = {"PDF", "Word Document", "Spreadsheet", "PowerPoint", "Resource"}` and an extension-based fallback (`.pdf`, `.doc`, `.docx`, `.ppt`, `.pptx`, `.xls`, `.xlsx`, `.zip`) checked directly against `resource.url` — covers every file type instruction 3 named, including ZIP (which `moodle/sync_service.py`'s own, untouched `_classify_resource_type()` doesn't have a distinct label for) without touching that classifier at all. Excludes "Page", "Link", "Folder", "Quiz", "Activity" — ordinary navigation/activity links are never treated as documents, per instruction 4.
    - `get_resources_eligible_for_document_sync()` — a new, broader query (the old `get_downloadable_resources()` only matched a `/mod/resource/view.php` URL substring, missing direct file links and other mod types the new resource classifier already recognizes; left that function itself unchanged for `main()`'s own use).
    - `sync_resource_documents(page)` — the new automated entry point. For each eligible resource without an existing `Document` (idempotency, reusing the exact same `Document.resource_id` check `process_resource()` already does — no new idempotency mechanism invented), calls the existing, unmodified `process_resource(page, resource)`, and logs every one of the requested stages (eligible, attempted, succeeded, skipped, failed, documents created, versions created) plus a final summary line.
    - Moved `from moodle.browser import get_authenticated_page` from module level into `main()` itself (the only place that ever used it) — this was a real, separate blocker found during implementation: `backend/Dockerfile`'s `.dockerignore` deliberately excludes `moodle/browser.py` from the production image (it's the interactive-login-only script's own helper), so importing `document_downloader.py` at module level from `sync_service.py` while `moodle.browser` still imported `get_authenticated_page` at ITS OWN module level would have raised `ModuleNotFoundError` on every single automated sync in production. Verified directly: simulated the real `/app` layout (only `main.py`/`api/`/`storage/`/`moodle/document_downloader.py`/`moodle/sync_service.py`, no `moodle/browser.py`) and confirmed both `moodle.document_downloader` and `main.app` import cleanly.
    - **`backend/.dockerignore`** updated to stop excluding `moodle/document_downloader.py` itself (it was previously excluded entirely, a second real blocker — even after fixing the import issue, the file wouldn't have been in the deployed image at all) — `moodle/browser.py`/`moodle/browser_profile/` remain excluded, unaffected.
  - **`moodle/sync_service.py`**: `SyncResult` gained `documents_eligible`/`documents_downloaded` fields. `run_sync()` calls `sync_resource_documents(page)` once, using the exact same authenticated `page`/context already open for the whole sync, positioned after assignment sync and before the existing "database commit completed"/`Moodle sync summary:` log lines — wrapped in its own `try/except` so a document-download problem can never abort the already-working course/assignment/resource sync above it. The existing `Moodle sync summary:` log line's fields (courses/assignments/resources) are completely unchanged, to avoid touching anything the previous entries' tests already assert against; document counts get their own separate summary line instead.
  - **Authentication (instruction 6) and the `page.goto()`-triggers-a-download problem (instruction 5)**: both already solved by the pre-existing, unmodified code, not new work — `inspect_resource()`/`download_file()` use `page.context.request.get(url)` (Playwright's `APIRequestContext`), which reuses the browser context's session cookies for a real authenticated HTTP request without ever calling `page.goto()` on the file itself — so a file response never becomes a browser "download" event needing separate interception at all.
- **Verification — real downloads, not just DB row counts (per explicit instruction not to declare success from unit tests alone)**:
  - Extended `tests/test_sync_service.py`'s existing full-pipeline fake Moodle server with two resources serving **real binary content with real, non-HTML content types**: a PDF at the existing `/mod/resource/view.php?id=999` route, and a direct `.docx` file link (`/files/course-syllabus.docx`) — plus the existing collapsed-section PDF (which still resolves to HTML on this fake server, deliberately exercising the "failed" path) and the existing Folder resource (deliberately ineligible).
  - **12 new checks (6o–6v)**, all against a real subprocess run of the real `run_sync()`: 3 resources correctly identified as eligible (Folder correctly excluded); exactly 2 real downloads succeed; exactly 2 `Document` rows are created, each correctly linked to its real `Resource` via `resource_id`, each with a file genuinely present on disk at the exact downloaded byte size, each with a real SHA-256 hash; exactly 2 `DocumentVersion` rows created; **a second `run_sync()` call in the same process/database downloads 0 new documents** (idempotency, instruction 8) with Document/DocumentVersion counts unchanged; every requested log stage (eligible/attempted/succeeded/skipped/failed/documents_created/versions_created) appears in real captured output; and the pre-existing course/assignment/resource summary counts are unchanged by this step running (instruction 12).
  - **Beyond the unit/subprocess tests — a full, real, live-server proof of the complete requested chain**: started the real `uvicorn main:app` process pointed at a fresh, empty `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` and the same fake Moodle server, called the real `POST /api/sync/moodle` endpoint over real HTTP, then called the real `GET /api/documents` endpoint — it returned the real, newly-created document, correctly joined to its course. Then called the real `GET /api/documents/1/download` endpoint — it returned **HTTP 200 with the exact downloaded PDF bytes** (`%PDF-1.4 FAKE-CONTENT-...`), byte-for-byte. This proves the full chain — Moodle Resource → download → Document → DocumentVersion → `/api/documents` → real download — works end-to-end through the real, unmodified API layer, not just at the database-row level.
  - Full suite re-run: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (46/46 — 34 existing + 12 new) — **74/74 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly, confirmed both normally and in the simulated no-`moodle/browser.py` production layout.
- **A known, honest test-fixture limitation, not a code bug**: the live-server verification's document was named "view.php" rather than "Lecture Notes.pdf" — an artifact of the fake test server responding directly at `/mod/resource/view.php?id=999` with no redirect, whereas real Moodle redirects a resource-wrapper URL to a `pluginfile.php/.../<real filename>` path that `get_filename_from_url()` (pre-existing, unmodified) correctly extracts a real filename from. Not fixed, since the underlying, unmodified logic is already correct for Moodle's real behavior — noted here for transparency rather than silently accepted or over-engineered around.
- **What was explicitly NOT touched, confirmed via `git status`**: course discovery, assignment discovery, and resource discovery (`_detect_active_courses`, `_sync_course_assignments`, `_sync_course_resources`, `_classify_resource_type`) are byte-for-byte unchanged — only `moodle/document_downloader.py` (additive), `moodle/sync_service.py` (one new call site + two new `SyncResult` fields), `.dockerignore`, and the test file changed.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py`, `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/.dockerignore`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`.
- **Known remaining limitation**: matching `process_resource()`'s own pre-existing behavior exactly (not a new limitation introduced here), a resource whose `Document` already exists is skipped outright on every future sync — if the underlying file changes on Moodle after its first successful download, this pipeline won't detect or re-download it (no periodic re-check/update path exists yet for already-downloaded resources, only for entirely new ones).
