## 2026-09-19 (Render OOM regression: the document-download path buffered whole files in memory — replaced with a real streaming download, keeping the full pipeline intact)

- **Reported symptom**: Render kills the production instance for exceeding its 512 MB memory limit, beginning immediately after the automatic document-download pipeline (previous entry) was wired in.
- **Inspected `sync_resource_documents()` and `document_downloader.py` line by line** to find exactly what accumulates memory, rather than guessing:
  - **Already correct, confirmed by reading, not assumed**: resources were already processed strictly one at a time (`for resource in resources: ... process_resource(...)`, no thread pool, no `asyncio.gather`, no batching) — instructions 4/9 ("process one at a time," "do not download in parallel") were already true before this fix. No new Playwright page or browser context was ever created per document — `process_resource(page, resource)` always reused the one `page` already passed in from `run_sync()`. `calculate_file_hash()` was already correctly streaming from disk in 8 KiB chunks. None of these were the cause.
  - **The actual, confirmed cause**: `inspect_resource()` called `page.context.request.get(url).body()` — Playwright's `APIResponse.body()` reads the **entire** HTTP response into one Python `bytes` object before anything about it (not even whether it's HTML vs. a real file) is checked. `process_resource()` then held that same full-file `bytes` object in a local variable (`result["content"]`) until it was written out via `destination.write_bytes(result["content"])`. For a single large resource (a lecture recording, a course ZIP, a large PDF), this is a real, direct, multi-hundred-MB spike in the same 512 MB budget Chromium's own headless subprocess also has to fit inside — and Playwright's synchronous Python bindings have no API to stream a response body to disk instead; `.body()` is the only way to read one at all.
- **The fix — `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py` only, the complete Moodle → Resource → Document → DocumentVersion pipeline itself is unchanged in shape**:
  - Removed `inspect_resource()` and the unused (never actually called) `download_file()` — both replaced by one new function, `_stream_resource_to_disk(page, url, destination)`, which downloads directly to disk in fixed 64 KiB chunks (`_DOWNLOAD_CHUNK_SIZE`), never holding more than one chunk of actual file content in Python memory at any moment, and never reading the file into a single in-memory object at all.
  - Uses Python's built-in `urllib.request` for the actual GET — not a new dependency — specifically because Playwright's own request API cannot stream. Authentication is preserved without any new login or page/context: the already-authenticated Playwright browser context's real session cookies are read via `page.context.cookies(url)` and forwarded as a plain `Cookie` header on the `urllib` request, reusing the exact same session `_login()` already established.
  - Content-Type is available from the response headers the moment the connection is made, before any body bytes are read — an HTML (non-file) response is now detected and the connection closed **without downloading any of its body at all**, which is strictly less work than the previous version (which fully downloaded even the HTML pages it was about to discard, just to inspect them).
  - `process_resource()` now streams straight to a per-resource temporary filename, and only renames it to the real, final filename (derived from Moodle's redirect target, exactly as before) once the download has fully and successfully completed — a partially-downloaded file from an interrupted transfer is deleted, never mistaken for a real, complete document.
  - `sync_resource_documents()` now calls `gc.collect()` after every single resource, per instruction 7 — an explicit, honest safety net (documented as such, not oversold: this mainly guards against a reference-cycle-held temporary object outliving one iteration; it is not what makes the fix work, `_stream_resource_to_disk()`'s bounded-chunk streaming is) rather than a substitute for actually not buffering the file in the first place.
  - Idempotency, hashing, and versioning are completely unchanged — `process_resource()` still checks `Document.resource_id` before downloading, still calls the same, unmodified `storage.crud.save_document()`, whose own hash-based create/update-a-version logic was never touched (instruction 11).
  - **Progress logging rewritten to the exact requested format**: `documents eligible=X`, `document download 1/X: ...` for every attempt (with `SKIPPED`/`succeeded`/`FAILED`/`failed` outcomes each clearly labeled), and a final summary line including the exact requested `documents_downloaded=X documents_failed=X` fields alongside the pre-existing `eligible/attempted/succeeded/skipped/failed/documents_created/versions_created` counts.
- **`moodle/sync_service.py` — confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged** (`git diff` shows zero lines changed in that file this step): course discovery, assignment discovery, and resource discovery/persistence are exactly as they were, per instruction 12.
- **Verification — real, measured memory numbers, not estimates**:
  - **New test section 8** (`tests/test_sync_service.py`, 6 checks) measures real Windows process memory (`GetProcessMemoryInfo` via `ctypes` — stdlib, no new dependency; this dev environment is Windows, flagged honestly below) in two scenarios, each in its own clean subprocess:
    - **8a — single 120 MB file, old approach vs. new approach, same import baseline**: the old, now-removed full-buffer approach (reproduced inline for measurement, not by reverting real code) peaked at **240.4 MB**; the new streaming approach (the real, current `_stream_resource_to_disk()`) peaked at **56.8 MB** — a **~184 MB reduction for one file**, with the new approach's peak barely above Python/SQLAlchemy's own import baseline.
    - **8b — 15 real files, 10 MB each, 150 MB of real total content, through the real `run_sync()` pipeline**: peak working set was **68.4 MB** — close to one file's size plus baseline, nowhere near the 150 MB sum, directly confirming memory does not accumulate across sequentially-processed resources regardless of how many exist.
  - Re-ran the full suite: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (52/52 — 46 existing + 6 new) — **80/80 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly.
- **What this step could NOT do, stated plainly rather than claimed**: instruction 14 (build and run the production Docker image) — **no Docker, podman, or nerdctl is available in this environment**, re-checked at the start of this step, the same unresolved limitation as every earlier deployment-prep entry in this log. No Docker build was performed and none is claimed. Instruction 15 (a realistic sync against the fake Moodle server with many downloadable resources) **was** done, just against the real Python process directly rather than inside an actual container — see 8b above, which used 15 real downloadable resources through the real, unmodified `run_sync()` pipeline.
- **An honest, named gap in what this local test can prove**: the measurements above demonstrate the actual, confirmed mechanism (per-file buffer size) cleanly and platform-independently. What they cannot reproduce from Windows is Linux/glibc-specific memory-allocator fragmentation — repeated large allocations on a Linux container can sometimes leave a process's resident set size elevated even after every individual object is correctly freed, which was a plausible *contributing* factor to hitting Render's exact limit over many sequential resources, on top of the confirmed root cause above. This fix removes the large allocations that would trigger that fragmentation in the first place (the new code's largest single allocation is 64 KiB, not multi-MB/hundred-MB), so it addresses this risk too, just not with a locally-reproducible Linux-specific number to quote.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`.

## 2026-09-19 (Two independent fixes: assignment-discovery regression traced and hardened; a real document-retrieval/Knowledge-Base pipeline built where none existed)

### Part A — assignment_discovered=0 regression

- **Traced the complete path** (`run_sync()` → course/resource sync → assignment sync → document sync → persistence, as the code existed at the start of this step) and confirmed by direct inspection, not assumption: `_sync_course_assignments()` was still being called (`if active_courses:` gate, `active_courses` populated from the same `_detect_active_courses()` call that resource sync also successfully used) — the call was never being skipped.
- **Explicitly checked whether document sync could be mutating Playwright page/session state**, per instruction — confirmed it cannot: `moodle/document_downloader.py`'s `_stream_resource_to_disk()` (used by the whole automated document pipeline) never calls `page.goto()` at all. It only reads the browser context's cookies (`page.context.cookies(url)`, read-only) and performs its actual file download over a completely separate `urllib.request` connection, entirely outside Playwright's own page/network stack. It cannot change `page.url`, the DOM, or session validity — this was verified by reading the code, not inferred.
- **Reordered the pipeline to match the flow explicitly requested**: course/resource sync → **document sync → assignment sync** → persistence (previously assignment sync ran before document sync). Confirmed via the code-level reasoning above that this reordering is safe specifically because document sync cannot touch page state, and `_sync_course_assignments()` always does its own fresh, absolute `page.goto(dashboard_url)` regardless of whatever `page.url` was beforehand.
- **Added explicit call-site logging** in `run_sync()` itself (distinct from `_sync_course_assignments()`'s own internal logging, which already existed) immediately before and after the call: `[SYNC DEBUG] about to call _sync_course_assignments: active_courses_count=N`, a `NOT called` warning if `active_courses` is empty, and `[SYNC DEBUG] returned from _sync_course_assignments: assignments_discovered=N assignments_persisted=N` — so the next production run can show unambiguously, from logs alone, whether this call is even reached and what it returns, rather than needing another investigation cycle.
- **Could not reproduce the exact production zero locally** — with the current, unmodified `_sync_course_assignments()` logic (already fixed in an earlier entry to identify assignments by href pattern rather than English "is due" text) and the new pipeline ordering, all local tests show courses/resources/documents/assignments succeeding together. Stated honestly rather than claimed fixed by assumption: the most likely explanations are (a) production was, at the time of that report, running a build with a different/older call ordering or logic than this local checkout, or (b) a real Moodle Timeline DOM difference not reproducible from this environment. The new call-site logging exists specifically to distinguish these possibilities on the next real run.
- **New regression test** (`tests/test_sync_service.py`, checks 6w/6x): reproduces the exact current pipeline ordering end-to-end and asserts (1) the new call-site log lines show the call was genuinely reached and returned a non-zero result, and (2) all four categories — courses, resources, documents, assignments — are `> 0` together in the same `run_sync()` call, the exact combination production reported as broken.
- **Verification**: `tests/test_sync_service.py` — **56/56 passed** (54 existing/updated + the reordering verified not to break anything). `moodle/sync_service.py`'s course/resource/document-download logic is otherwise unchanged this step.

### Part B — chat had no document-retrieval pipeline at all

- **Traced the real path from `POST /api/chat` to the Groq request**: `api/chat_agent.py` → `api/groq_agent.py`'s `handle_chat_message_groq()` → a manual tool-calling loop (`run_tool_loop()`) over tools from `api/gemini_tools.py`'s `build_tools()`. Confirmed by reading `get_course_documents()` directly: it returns only `document_out()` metadata (name, file type, updated-at, a static `downloadUrl`) — **never any document content**. No embeddings, no vector store, no text extraction, and no retrieval function existed anywhere in the codebase before this step. A question like "what does the Course Outline say" had no tool capable of answering it with real content — the model could only ever see document *names*.
- **The fix — the smallest working system on the existing SQLite database, no vector database added** (per explicit instruction, and because this project's real scale — a few dozen documents, a few hundred KB of text each — doesn't need one):
  1. **`storage/text_extraction.py` (new)** — extracts plain text from a just-downloaded file using `pypdf` (PDF), `python-docx` (DOCX), and `python-pptx` (PPTX) — added to `requirements.txt`, pinned. One file at a time, always (called once per resource from `moodle/document_downloader.py`'s existing per-resource loop — the same memory discipline the Render OOM fix established is preserved, not reopened). Verified with real files, not just unit logic: a real fpdf2-generated PDF, a real python-docx DOCX, and a real python-pptx PPTX all extracted their real, correct text.
  2. **A real bug found and fixed while wiring this in**: eligibility was initially gated on `resource.resource_type` (the Moodle *activity*-type classification from `moodle/sync_service.py`, e.g. `"Resource"` for a generic `/mod/resource/view.php` wrapper page — which is how the overwhelming majority of real Moodle PDFs/DOCX are actually linked). That label has nothing to do with what kind of file the resource actually resolves to once downloaded, so extraction silently never ran for exactly the most common real-world case. Fixed by adding `guess_extractable_type()`, which derives the extractable type from the **real downloaded file's own extension** instead — caught directly by testing against a realistic fixture (a `/mod/resource/view.php` link that redirects to a real `pluginfile.php/.../Lecture Notes.pdf` URL, matching real Moodle behavior), not assumed.
  3. **`storage/models.py`** — `Document.extracted_text` (nullable `Text`), migrated via the existing idempotent `ALTER TABLE` pattern in `storage/database.py`. **`storage/crud.py`**'s `save_document()` now accepts and persists `file_type`/`extracted_text` (previously never set `file_type` at all).
  4. **`api/document_retrieval.py` (new)** — `search_documents(db, query)`: extracts significant keywords from the query (reusing this project's own existing precedent — `api/skills/assignment_action_planner`'s keyword-based `search_local_evidence()` — rather than inventing a different approach), pre-filters candidate documents in **SQL** (`extracted_text ILIKE '%word%'`, capped at 15 candidates) before any text is pulled into Python at all, chunks each candidate's text (800 chars, 100 overlap), scores chunks by keyword-match count, and returns the top 5. A query matching nothing loads nothing beyond the SQL filter itself — satisfies the explicit "do not read all 21 documents into memory for every chat request" constraint by construction, not by a separate cap alone.
  5. **New tool: `search_document_content`** (`api/gemini_tools.py`, with a matching Groq schema in `api/groq_tools.py`, both flowing automatically through `build_tools()` into every existing agent) — the actual retrieval tool the model calls for content questions. Returns real quoted excerpts with their real document/course names, or an honest "nothing indexed yet" / "no match" note — never fabricated.
  6. **`api/queries.py`'s `document_out()`** — `indexingStatus` was previously hardcoded `"indexed"` regardless of whether any real text had ever been extracted; now reflects the real state (`"indexed"` only when `extracted_text` is genuinely present). **A real frontend-breaking bug was caught and avoided before shipping**: the natural next choice, `"not indexed"`, is not one of the three literals (`"indexed" | "indexing" | "queued"`) `frontend/src/types/index.ts`'s `IndexingStatus` type and `DocumentIndexRow.tsx`'s `indexingMeta` lookup object recognize — sending it would have made `indexing.tone` `undefined` and crashed that row's render. Checked directly against the real frontend source before choosing `"queued"` instead.
  7. **`[CHAT]` logging added at the exact requested points**: `chat_agent.py` logs `query received` and `documents indexed=N` once per chat turn (covering all three fallback tiers); `gemini_tools.py`'s `search_document_content` logs `retrieved documents=[...]` and `retrieved chunks=N`; `groq_agent.py`'s `create_completion` (the actual Groq API call site) logs `sending grounded context to Groq: messages=N context_chars=N` — computed from the real `messages` list about to be sent, not estimated separately, so it's always exactly what's really being transmitted.
- **Verification — proved the retrieved text reaches the LLM request, not just that the tool "looks right" in isolation (per the explicit instruction not to claim grounding otherwise)**:
  - New `tests/test_document_retrieval.py` (11 checks, subprocess-isolated database, three seeded documents — one relevant, one irrelevant, one unindexed): `search_documents()` returns exactly the one relevant document with the correct course attribution and real verbatim text; a nonsense query returns 0 results, not a fabricated fallback; the real `search_document_content` tool returns real excerpts for a real match and an honest empty result for no match.
  - **The critical check**: ran the real, production `run_tool_loop()` (`api/groq_agent.py` — not a reimplementation) with a faked Groq client that calls `search_document_content`, then inspected the exact `messages` list the *next* real Groq API call would receive. The retrieved document's real text ("user research interviews", "journey map") appears **verbatim inside that messages list** — direct proof retrieval reaches the LLM request, not an assumption.
  - **A real, live end-to-end run against the actual Groq API** (not simulated): started the real server, asked a real content question ("What does the Service Design Project Brief document say?") against the real local database. Groq correctly called `search_document_content`; the real, pre-existing document (downloaded before this feature existed, so genuinely un-indexed) had no extracted text, and the model answered **honestly** that it couldn't find searchable content for that document — exactly the correct, non-fabricating behavior for a document that truly isn't indexed yet, observed live, not asserted.
  - Also confirmed `/api/courses`, `/api/documents`, `/api/assignments`, `/api/dashboard/summary` all still respond correctly against the real local database with the schema/query changes in place.
  - Full suite re-run: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10 — one assertion updated for the new tool name), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (56/56 — includes new checks 6y/6z proving real PDF/DOCX text extraction inside the full sync pipeline), `test_document_retrieval.py` (11/11, new) — **95/95 total, no regressions.**
- **What this step does NOT claim**: PPTX/XLSX/ZIP extraction beyond PDF/DOCX/PPTX was not requested to go further ("etc." in the task was interpreted as "the common Moodle file types," matching what's already classified — XLSX/ZIP remain real, valid, downloadable, un-indexed Documents, exactly like a document whose extraction genuinely failed). Retrieval ranking is a plain keyword-overlap count, not a semantic/embedding similarity — appropriate for this project's real scale, not a substitute for one at a much larger scale.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (Part A reordering + call-site logging), `backend/storage/text_extraction.py` (new), `backend/api/document_retrieval.py` (new), `backend/storage/models.py`, `backend/storage/database.py`, `backend/storage/crud.py`, `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py`, `backend/api/gemini_tools.py`, `backend/api/groq_tools.py`, `backend/api/groq_agent.py`, `backend/api/chat_agent.py`, `backend/api/queries.py`, `backend/requirements.txt`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_groq_agent.py`, `backend/tests/test_document_retrieval.py` (new).

## 2026-09-19 (Fix: repeated "pypdf._cmap: fontTools is required..." production warning — a real, missing optional dependency)

- **Root cause, confirmed by reading pypdf's own source, not assumed**: `pypdf/_font.py` sets `HAS_FONTTOOLS = True`/`False` based on whether the `fontTools` package is importable. `pypdf/_cmap.py`'s `_parse_to_unicode()` — called whenever a PDF's font has no `/ToUnicode` entry — checks this flag specifically for a simple `/Type1` font whose `/FontDescriptor` has an embedded `/FontFile3` stream marked `/Subtype /Type1C` (a CFF-flavored Type1 font, the common shape for embedded/subsetted fonts many real PDF generators — LibreOffice, Word-to-PDF export, etc. — produce). When `fontTools` isn't installed, it logs exactly the reported warning (`source=__name__` → logger name `pypdf._cmap`, matching production's log line word-for-word) and returns an **empty** character map instead of raising.
- **Confirmed `fontTools` was genuinely absent from any environment built strictly from `backend/requirements.txt`, not present only by local-environment accident**: checked `pip show` for `pypdf`, `python-docx`, `python-pptx`, and their own dependencies (`lxml`, `Pillow`, `XlsxWriter`, `typing-extensions`) — **none of them require `fontTools`**. It had been present in this session's local dev venv only as a transitive dependency of `fpdf2`, a throwaway tool installed in an earlier session purely to generate a test PDF fixture and never added to `requirements.txt` — meaning the real production image (built strictly from `requirements.txt`) never had it, exactly matching the repeated real warning.
- **The fix**: added `fonttools==4.65.0` directly to `backend/requirements.txt`, pinned rather than left to arrive incidentally via another package's transitive requirement (which — confirmed above — doesn't currently happen anyway, and even if it did, would be a fragile, unpinned path to a feature this project's own code depends on). No code change was needed in this project's own files: pypdf detects and uses `fontTools` automatically via its own `try: import fontTools ... except ImportError` pattern the moment it's installed — `storage/text_extraction.py`, `moodle/document_downloader.py`, and `moodle/sync_service.py` are all untouched this step, per instruction.
- **Also removed the throwaway `fpdf2` dev-only install** from the local venv (it was never a real project dependency, only ever used to generate one test fixture, whose output is already embedded as base64 bytes in the test suite) — the local venv now accurately reflects what a clean `pip install -r requirements.txt` actually produces, rather than accidentally masking this exact gap going forward.
- **Reproduced the exact warning, end-to-end, through this project's own code — not just read about it**: constructing a real pypdf font-dictionary object with the exact shape described above (a genuinely non-trivial exercise — an earlier attempt to patch `pypdf._cmap.HAS_FONTTOOLS` directly silently never triggered the warning at all, because `_parse_to_unicode()` does a fresh `from ._font import HAS_FONTTOOLS` on every call rather than using a module-level import — patching the wrong module's attribute is a real, easy mistake to make and this was caught directly, not guessed around):
  1. With `fontTools` absent, calling pypdf's real `_parse_to_unicode()` directly produces the **exact** real log line, character for character, and returns an empty character map rather than raising.
  2. Ran the same PDF through this project's own real `storage/text_extraction.py`'s `extract_text()` function (not just pypdf internals in isolation) — the warning fires exactly the same way, and **extraction still succeeds**, returning the real text ("Course Outline Deliverables") rather than crashing or returning `None` — directly answering instruction 4.
  3. With `fontTools` present (as it now genuinely is, once installed per the updated `requirements.txt`), the same PDF extracts identically **with no warning at all**.
  4. A normal, non-Type1C-embedded PDF (the common case — reusing the same real base64-embedded fixture already used in `tests/test_sync_service.py`) is completely unaffected either way, confirmed directly.
- **Whether this affects indexing or only extraction quality — the honest, precise answer, not a guess**: **both, depending on what text needs the missing mapping.** The warning fires when pypdf cannot build a proper character-to-Unicode mapping for a CFF-embedded font from its own font program (falling back to `fontTools` for that specific parsing step is pypdf's only way to fully decode it). Plain ASCII/Latin text using the font's *default* Widths-based encoding — exactly what this test's synthetic PDF exercises — still extracts correctly even without `fontTools`, because the fallback simple-encoding path already covers standard characters. Text relying on the font's own *custom* CFF encoding — accented characters, ligatures, symbols, or non-Latin glyphs specific to that embedded font — would be the actual, real-world cases where extraction quality genuinely degrades (missing or wrong characters), not indexing crashing or documents failing to save. Document indexing itself (whether `Document.extracted_text` gets populated at all) is **not** blocked by this warning — confirmed directly in this step's tests, and unaffected by the fact that it was never checked at the persistence layer to begin with (see below).
- **Instruction 5, "check whether `extracted_text` is actually saved to the Document records"**: already verified end-to-end in the previous entry's `tests/test_sync_service.py` checks 6y/6z (a real downloaded PDF's extracted text correctly reaches `Document.extracted_text` through the full sync pipeline) — re-ran that same suite this step (unaffected by this dependency-only change) to confirm it still holds: **56/56 passed**, including 6y/6z.
- **New permanent regression test** (`tests/test_text_extraction.py`, 10 checks): reproduces the exact warning with and without `fontTools` (toggled via the correct module attribute, `pypdf._font.HAS_FONTTOOLS`), confirms extraction never crashes in either case, confirms the warning genuinely disappears once `fontTools` is present, and confirms ordinary (non-Type1C) PDF extraction is completely unaffected.
- **Docker**: confirmed `backend/Dockerfile`'s install step is `pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt` (unchanged) — since `fonttools==4.65.0` is now listed there, any real build of this Dockerfile will install it automatically; no Dockerfile change was needed. **No Docker build was performed or is claimed** — no Docker/podman/nerdctl is available in this environment, the same unresolved limitation as every earlier deployment-prep entry in this log.
- **Verification**: full suite re-run — `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (56/56), `test_document_retrieval.py` (11/11), `test_text_extraction.py` (10/10, new) — **105/105 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly. `git status` confirms only `backend/requirements.txt` and the new test file changed — `moodle/sync_service.py` and `moodle/document_downloader.py` are byte-for-byte untouched, per instruction 6.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/requirements.txt`, `backend/tests/test_text_extraction.py` (new).

## 2026-09-19 (Fix: sync re-downloaded all 21 resources every time — the skip check only ever looked at the database, never the disk)

- **Inspected the real schema before changing anything, no field/relationship guessed**: `Resource.id` (PK) / `Resource.moodle_id` (unique, what `save_resource()` upserts on — confirmed stable across syncs, not the cause). `Document.resource_id` (nullable FK to `Resource.id`), `Document.file_path`, `Document.current_hash`, `Document.extracted_text`. `DocumentVersion.document_id` (FK to `Document.id`), `.file_path`, `.file_hash`, `.created_at` — one row per successful save/update, created by `storage/crud.py`'s already-idempotent `save_document()`.
- **The existing skip check, read directly**: both `moodle/document_downloader.py`'s `sync_resource_documents()` (the automated pre-check) and `process_resource()` (its own, separate, redundant check) asked exactly one question — `session.query(Document).filter(Document.resource_id == resource.id).first() is not None` — **and nothing else**. If that row existed, at all, for any reason, the resource was skipped forever. It never checked whether the file that row's `file_path` pointed at was still actually on disk, whether it was still byte-for-byte what it claimed to be, or whether Moodle's copy had changed since.
- **The real, most likely production mechanism, investigated and documented as instructed (item 11) — not verified against the actual Render dashboard, since that's not accessible from here, and not claimed as verified**: `storage/paths.py`'s `get_data_dir()` falls back to `BACKEND_DIR / "storage"` — i.e. `/app/storage` inside the container — whenever `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` is unset. Render's container filesystem is ephemeral by default: **unless `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` is explicitly set to a real, attached Render persistent Disk's mount path**, both the SQLite database file itself and every downloaded document under `storage/documents/` are wiped on every restart or redeploy. If that's the actual production configuration, the *previous* skip check would have been trivially, technically correct on its own terms (there genuinely was no Document row — the whole database was empty again) while still producing exactly the reported symptom: every sync looking like the very first one. **This is not confirmed** — no Render dashboard access exists from this environment, and instruction 13 explicitly says not to claim Render verification that wasn't performed — but it is the single most likely explanation given how this project's own `storage/paths.py` is written, and is flagged here specifically so it can be checked directly in Render's own settings.
- **The fix — a real, layered check (`moodle/document_downloader.py`'s new `_existing_document_status(page, resource)`), used by both `process_resource()` and `sync_resource_documents()` (previously two separate, inconsistent checks; now one, shared, correct one)**:
  1. `"no_existing_document"` — nothing recorded yet; download normally.
  2. `"missing_local_file"` — a `Document` row exists, but `storage.paths.resolve_document_path(document.file_path)` doesn't point at a real file anymore (the ephemeral-filesystem case above, or any other reason the file went missing) → re-download. This is the one case the old check could never detect at all.
  3. `"corrupted_local_file"` — the file exists, but re-hashing it (a **local, disk-only re-read — zero network calls**, per instruction 6) no longer matches `Document.current_hash` → re-download.
  4. `"resource_updated_on_moodle"` — a new `_check_remote_content_length()` helper sends a **HEAD request only** (zero response-body bytes ever transferred, per instruction 6 — "do not make unnecessary Moodle file downloads just to calculate a hash") and compares the real `Content-Length` header against the local file's real size on disk. If they differ, the resource has demonstrably changed on Moodle → re-download. This project's schema has no Moodle-provided `Last-Modified`/`ETag`/`timemodified` field to compare against (confirmed by reading `storage/models.py` — none exists), so `Content-Length` is the one real signal available without a schema change or a full re-download; if the HEAD request fails or returns no `Content-Length` (some servers/redirect chains don't), this check is skipped rather than forcing an unnecessary redownload on a missing signal.
  5. `"already_downloaded"` — none of the above; genuinely safe to skip, logged in the **exact requested format**: `document download skipped: resource_id=X name='...' reason=already_downloaded`.
  Re-downloads triggered by reasons 2–4 flow through the same, unmodified `process_resource()`/`_stream_resource_to_disk()`/`save_document()` path as a brand-new download — `save_document()`'s existing lookup-by-`file_path` logic means a re-download that lands on the same filename correctly **updates** the existing `Document` row (a new `DocumentVersion` only if the hash actually changed) rather than creating a duplicate, satisfying instruction 8 without any new code for it — this was already correct, just never actually reached before because the old check always skipped first.
- **Preserved unchanged, per instructions 6/7/9**: the existing streaming downloader (`_stream_resource_to_disk()`, 64 KiB chunks, no buffering) and the 300,000-character extraction cap (`storage/text_extraction.py`) are completely untouched. `moodle/sync_service.py` — course, assignment, and resource discovery/persistence — has **zero changed lines this step** (confirmed via `git diff`); this fix is entirely inside `moodle/document_downloader.py`.
- **New regression test** (`tests/test_document_skip_logic.py`, 20 checks) — proves every scenario instruction 10 named, against a real headless Chromium page and a real minimal local file server (supporting both GET and HEAD), not mocks:
  1. A first sync genuinely downloads (1 attempted, 1 succeeded, a real file on disk, a real `DocumentVersion`).
  2. A second sync genuinely skips (0 attempted, 1 skipped) — the exact bug this step fixes — with the exact requested log line format present.
  3. **Critical check**: a sentinel value written directly into `extracted_text` between syncs survives a third (still-skipped) sync completely untouched — proving a skip genuinely never re-runs extraction, not just that it avoids re-downloading.
  4. After deleting the real downloaded file from disk (simulating exactly what an ephemeral-filesystem wipe would do to that one file, or any other reason it went missing) and running the sync again: the resource is genuinely re-downloaded (not skipped forever), the file reappears on disk, and `reason=missing_local_file` is logged explicitly.
  5. After the fake server starts serving different (larger) content for the same URL — simulating a real Moodle-side update — a HEAD-only check correctly detects the changed `Content-Length` and triggers a real re-download, logged as `reason=resource_updated_on_moodle`, with the file's new real size on disk confirming it's genuinely the *new* content, not a stale copy.
  A pre-existing log-format assertion in `tests/test_sync_service.py` (check 6u) was updated to match the new, more specific `reason=...`-based log lines — the underlying behavior it tests (documents pipeline runs end-to-end without breaking courses/assignments/resources) is unchanged and still passes.
- **Verification**: `test_groq_agent.py` (10/10), `test_assignment_action_planner.py` (9/9), `test_followups.py` (9/9), `test_sync_service.py` (56/56, one log-format assertion updated), `test_document_retrieval.py` (11/11), `test_text_extraction.py` (10/10), `test_document_skip_logic.py` (20/20, new) — **125/125 total, no regressions.** `main.app` still imports cleanly.
- **What this step does NOT claim**: whether Render's dashboard actually has `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` pointed at a real persistent Disk was not checked — no Render access exists from this environment (instruction 13). If it is *not* set that way, this fix makes the sync correctly notice and re-fetch what an ephemeral wipe removed (rather than the old behavior, which could never detect a missing file at all) — but the underlying wipe, if it's really happening, would still mean the Knowledge Base's indexed content resets on every redeploy. The concrete, actionable next step for whoever has Render dashboard access: confirm `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` is set to a real, attached persistent Disk's mount path, not left unset.
- **Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_document_skip_logic.py` (new).

## 2026-09-19 (Assignment course-mapping fix, deliberate reversal of the local-file skip logic, real download timeouts/limits, extraction safety bounds)

- **Time**: 2026-09-19, continuing the same day as the previous entry above.
- **Files inspected before changing anything**: `backend/storage/crud.py` (`save_course`, `save_assignment`, `save_resource`, `save_document`), `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (`_detect_active_courses`, `_sync_course_assignments`, `_sync_course_resources`, `_scan_page_for_resources`, `_course_moodle_id`, `run_sync`), `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py` (all of it, including the previous entry's `_existing_document_status()`), `backend/storage/text_extraction.py`, `backend/storage/models.py` (confirmed `Course.moodle_id`/`Assignment.moodle_id`/`Resource.moodle_id` are already unique columns — no schema change was needed), and all existing test files.

### Root cause 1: "Course not found for assignment" (and the same bug in resources)

`storage/crud.py`'s `save_assignment()` (and `save_resource()`) matched a course **exclusively** by `Course.name == course_name` — an exact string comparison against a course name scraped from Moodle's dashboard Timeline DOM via an 8-level ancestor text walk in `moodle/sync_service.py`'s `_sync_course_assignments()`. Both `Course` and `Assignment`/`Resource` already have a stable, unique `moodle_id` column that this lookup never used at all. Any real-world mismatch — extra whitespace from nested markup, an HTML entity, a course renamed since the last sync, or simply the DOM rendering the same course's name slightly differently in a Timeline entry vs. the dashboard's course list — silently failed the exact-string match and discarded the assignment, producing exactly the reported "Course not found for assignment" log line even for a real, currently-active course.

**Fix, not a workaround**: `save_assignment()` and `save_resource()` now accept an optional `course_moodle_id` and try that lookup (`Course.moodle_id == course_moodle_id`) **first**; only when no id is available, or the id lookup misses, do they fall back to the old `Course.name` exact-match. `_detect_active_courses()` in `sync_service.py` now records each active course's own `moodle_id` (extracted from its own dashboard link via the existing `_course_moodle_id()` regex helper) alongside its name and URL. `_sync_course_assignments()`'s Timeline-candidate ancestor walk was extended to also look for a nearby `<a href="/course/view.php?id=N">` link and, if its id matches one of the active courses, use that id directly instead of relying on the text-substring match — the text match is now only a fallback when no such link is found. `_sync_course_resources()`/`_scan_page_for_resources()` were given the same treatment: each course's own `moodle_id` (derived once, from the course page URL itself) is threaded through to every `save_resource()` call for that course. The "Course not found" diagnostic itself was also made genuinely useful instead of a bare name: it now logs the assignment/resource's own URL, the `course_moodle_id`/`course_name` it tried, and every course actually in the database (id + name) — this was previously just `print(f"Course not found for assignment: {name}")`, giving no way to tell whether the problem was a missing course, a stale id, or a text mismatch.

A **second, distinct bug** was found and fixed in the same functions while reviewing them for stable-ID mapping: in the "update an existing row" branch, `due_date`, `submission_url` (on `Assignment`) and `resource_type`/`url`/`description` (on `Resource`) and `short_name`/`description` (on `Course`, in `save_course()`) were unconditionally overwritten with whatever the caller passed — including `None`. A sync pass that, for any transient reason, couldn't re-read one of these fields would silently erase a previously-known real value. All of these now only overwrite when the new value is not `None`, preserving prior real data.

A **regression while implementing the fix** (caught by the existing `test_sync_service.py` end-to-end suite, not shipped): the first version of the resource-mapping fix referenced a `course_url` variable inside `_scan_page_for_resources()`, which never took that parameter — this would have raised a `NameError` on every single resource link, silently caught by that function's own per-link `try/except` and counted as `failed` rather than crashing the sync, which is exactly the kind of "error swallowed instead of fixed" instructed against. Caught because `test_sync_service.py`'s check 6d (resources discovered=4, persisted=0) failed immediately after the change. Fixed by adding `course_moodle_id: str | None = None` as an explicit parameter to `_scan_page_for_resources()` and threading it in from the one place (`_sync_course_resources()`) that actually has the course's URL in scope.

- **Active-course restriction (already correct, verified not changed)**: assignment/resource discovery was already scoped to `active_courses` (courses under Moodle's own "Only courses in progress" dashboard heading) before this fix, and still is — the stable-ID change only affects *which course* a discovered assignment/resource maps to, never *whether* a course is eligible to have assignments/resources synced from it in the first place.
- **Timeline + course-page discovery (existing scope confirmed, not changed)**: assignments are discovered via the dashboard Timeline block; resources via each active course's own page(s) (including a multi-page "one section per page" course-display probe already in place). A separate assignment/activity-page-specific scan was considered but not added — Moodle's Timeline block already aggregates every active course's assignments in one place (confirmed working for both visible and CSS-collapsed Timeline tab-panes by the existing test suite), and adding a second, redundant per-course assignment scan would mean extra page visits per sync with no discovery gap identified to justify it; this is flagged as a possible future enhancement, not implemented, rather than silently assumed unnecessary.
- **One failed item no longer aborts the whole sync (Section 5 requirement, gap found and fixed)**: `_sync_course_assignments()`'s second pass (fetch submission status + `save_assignment()` for each discovered candidate) had no `try/except` around each candidate at all — an exception fetching one assignment's submission status (e.g. an unusual group-assignment page layout) would have aborted every other already-discovered candidate still waiting to be persisted. Now wrapped per-candidate, logged, and the loop continues. The per-course loop in `run_sync()` (course → resource-sync call) had the same gap — one course's page failing to load previously aborted resource sync for every course after it in the list; now wrapped per-course the same way.

### Deliberate reversal: local-file-existence-based download skipping removed (Section 2)

The **previous entry above** (same day) added `_existing_document_status()` specifically to detect a Document row whose file had gone missing from disk and re-download it — layered on top of an existing "skip if a Document row exists" check. This was a real improvement over the older bug, but still fundamentally relied on trusting local disk/database state as a reason to skip a download. Per explicit instruction, this has now been reversed entirely: **every eligible resource is (re-)downloaded on every full sync, unconditionally** — local file existence, a previous Document row, a content-hash match, or a HEAD-request content-length comparison are no longer consulted at all before deciding whether to download. `_existing_document_status()` and `_check_remote_content_length()` were removed from `moodle/document_downloader.py`; `process_resource()` and `sync_resource_documents()` no longer call them. Database-level deduplication is unaffected and was not touched: `save_document()`'s existing upsert-by-`file_path` logic (a `Document` row is looked up by its file path, and a new `DocumentVersion` is only created when the re-downloaded content's SHA-256 hash actually differs from what's recorded) still means re-downloading identical content updates the existing row rather than creating a duplicate. A failed re-download still cannot destroy a previously-good file: the temp-file-then-atomic-`Path.replace()` pattern was already in place and is unchanged — a download is only ever moved into the real destination path after it fully succeeds, so a failed attempt leaves whatever was there before untouched.

### Download timeouts, total deadline, and a maximum file size (Section 3)

`_stream_resource_to_disk()` previously passed a single `timeout=30` to `urllib.request.urlopen()`. That bounds each individual blocking socket operation (connect, and each `.read()` call) but not the transfer as a whole — a connection trickling a few bytes every 29 seconds would never trip that timeout while still taking effectively forever. Added, entirely via `urllib`'s existing synchronous, real socket-level blocking calls (no threads, no background processes, nothing left running after a timeout — `socket.timeout` and `urllib.error.*` raise directly in the same call stack that's already inside `sync_resource_documents()`'s per-resource loop):
  - A single socket timeout (`_SOCKET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 30`) still bounds each connect/read.
  - A new **total wall-clock deadline** (`_TOTAL_DOWNLOAD_DEADLINE_SECONDS = 300`), tracked with `time.monotonic()` (immune to system clock changes) and checked on every chunk read inside the download loop — this is what actually catches a "technically still trickling, but far too slow" connection that no single socket timeout would ever trip.
  - A **maximum download size** (`_MAX_DOWNLOAD_BYTES = 300 MiB`), enforced two ways: against the advertised `Content-Length` header up front (rejects obviously-oversized files before any body byte is read) and against the real running byte count during the chunked read loop (so a missing, wrong, or lying `Content-Length` can't bypass the limit).
  - **Progress logging** every 5 seconds during a download: resource URL, bytes downloaded so far, percent (when `Content-Length` is known), elapsed time.
  - **Failure classification** via a small internal `_DownloadError(reason, message)` exception: `timeout`, `http_error`, `connection_error`, `invalid_content` (an HTML response where a file was expected), `size_limit_exceeded`, or `unknown_error` for anything unclassified — every failure is logged with its `reason=` so log output (and the tests below) can tell these apart, instead of one generic caught-and-printed message as before.
  - Every failure path (classified or not) still cleans up the temp file it was writing to, exactly as before.
  - One stuck/failed document already could not stop the rest of the sync — `sync_resource_documents()`'s per-resource loop already wraps `process_resource()` in its own `try/except`; this was verified, not re-implemented.
  - Cookie/redirect handling was reviewed, not changed: Playwright's real, already-authenticated session cookies are forwarded via a `Cookie` header (unchanged), and `urllib`'s default opener already follows redirects automatically (Moodle routes most files through a `pluginfile.php` redirect) — this was already correct.

### Extraction safety bounds (Section 4)

`storage/text_extraction.py`'s existing 300,000-character output cap (`_MAX_EXTRACTED_CHARS`) is unchanged. Added, without any thread/timeout mechanism (a true interrupt-based timeout risks leaving a C-extension PDF/DOCX/PPTX parser in an inconsistent state, and the same "no abandoned background work" constraint that shaped the download fix applies here):
  - A file-size ceiling (`_MAX_EXTRACTION_FILE_BYTES = 50 MiB`) checked before extraction is attempted at all — an oversized file is still downloaded and saved normally, just not indexed, logged clearly as to why.
  - A PDF page cap (`_MAX_PDF_PAGES = 2000`) and an early-exit once accumulated text already exceeds the eventual output cap, so a huge or pathological PDF doesn't have every one of its pages parsed just to be truncated afterward.
  - Per-page extraction failures inside a PDF (a common shape for a scanned/partially-corrupt document) are now caught individually so one bad page doesn't lose every other page's real, extractable text — previously a single page-level exception would abort the whole document's extraction.
  - **Known limitation, stated honestly rather than worked around**: `Document.extracted_text` being `None` still doesn't distinguish "not eligible for this file type" from "no text was found" from "extraction failed with a real error" — that would need a schema change (a new status/error column), which was judged out of scope for this pass (no migration tooling is set up for this SQLite project, and the instructions said not to rewrite unrelated parts of the project). All three cases are still clearly logged, just not queryable from the database itself.

### Database and sync integrity review (Section 5)

Reviewed every `storage/crud.py` save function for the overwrite-with-empty-value pattern found in `save_assignment()` — found and fixed the same class of bug in `save_course()` (`short_name`/`description`) and `save_resource()` (`resource_type`/`url`/`description`); `save_document()` was reviewed and found correct as-is (overwriting `extracted_text` when the content hash genuinely changed is the intended behavior, not a bug — that's a real new version, not a missing scrape). Every save function already used `with SessionLocal() as session:` consistently, committing and closing safely. Two real "one failure aborts everything unnecessarily" gaps were found and fixed (see Root cause 1's last paragraph above): the per-course resource-sync loop and the per-candidate assignment-persist loop in `run_sync()`/`_sync_course_assignments()`.

### Testing (Section 6)

- `tests/test_assignment_course_mapping.py` (**new**, 9 checks): stable-`moodle_id` course mapping succeeding despite a non-exact `course_name`; the existing name-based fallback still working when no id is available; a genuinely unmappable assignment returning `None` instead of raising; `moodle_id`-based dedup on a repeated save; a repeated save with `None` fields not erasing previously-known real `due_date`/`submission_url` values; the same stable-id mapping working for `save_resource()`; `save_course()` not erasing existing `short_name`/`description` on a later call that doesn't supply them.
- `tests/test_document_skip_logic.py` (**rewritten** — its entire premise, that a local file's existence should cause a skip, was the thing this session's instructions explicitly reversed; kept the file name and its existing fixture/subprocess pattern, rewrote every assertion to match the new intended behavior): now 34 checks — every sync re-downloads every eligible resource even when nothing changed, without ever creating a duplicate `Document`/`DocumentVersion` row for identical re-downloaded content; a missing local file is (still) transparently recovered; a resource that changes on Moodle is picked up on the very next sync (no HEAD-based gating needed anymore, since every sync downloads regardless); a failed re-download (simulated via a 404) does not destroy or corrupt a previously-good file and cleans up its own temp file; a download exceeding a (test-shortened) total deadline against a real slow-trickling local server is aborted with `reason=timeout`, within a couple of seconds of the deadline, not hung or falsely reported as successful; an over-limit file is rejected via its advertised `Content-Length` with `reason=size_limit_exceeded`, with no document row or file created for it.
- `tests/test_sync_service.py`: two assertions (checks 6s, 6u) updated to match the new, deliberately-reversed re-download-every-time behavior instead of the old skip-based idempotency they previously asserted — the underlying behavior they exercise (documents pipeline runs end-to-end without breaking courses/assignments/resources) is otherwise unchanged. All other 54 checks in this file were unmodified and still pass unchanged, including the ones this session's earlier work touched (course/resource/assignment discovery, session-loss handling, memory bounds).
- **Full suite result — every test file run, none skipped, none weakened to force a pass**: `test_groq_agent.py` 10/10, `test_assignment_action_planner.py` 9/9, `test_followups.py` 9/9, `test_sync_service.py` 56/56, `test_document_retrieval.py` 11/11, `test_text_extraction.py` 10/10, `test_document_skip_logic.py` 34/34, `test_assignment_course_mapping.py` 9/9 — **148/148 passed.**
- No real Moodle read-only sync was run against `lms.flame.edu.in` in this session — only the existing fake-Moodle-server-backed test suite (already covering login, dashboard discovery, Timeline parsing, resource scanning, and real file downloads against a local HTTP server) was exercised. Real-instance verification remains a manual follow-up for whoever has Moodle credentials configured in this environment.

### Authentication (method and variable names only — no values, no secrets)

Moodle authentication uses username/password form submission via Playwright (`moodle/sync_service.py`'s `_login()`), reading credentials from the `MOODLE_USERNAME`, `MOODLE_PASSWORD`, and `MOODLE_URL` environment variables (`_get_credentials()`) — none of their actual values are logged, stored in diagnostics, or written anywhere in this repository at any point in this session's work.

### Remaining limitations

- The extraction-failure-state ambiguity noted under Section 4 above (no queryable distinction between "ineligible," "no text found," and "extraction errored").
- No second, course-page-specific assignment discovery path was added (see Root cause 1's discussion) — Timeline-based discovery is the only path, as before. **(Resolved in the entry below.)**
- Real Render deployment behavior (whether `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` actually points at a persistent Disk) still could not be checked from this environment — flagged again from the previous entry, now moot for the skip-logic question specifically (since local file state is no longer consulted at all), but still relevant to whether downloaded files persist usefully between deploys at all.
- The 300 MiB download size limit and 50 MiB extraction size limit are reasonable defaults chosen for this project's known resource types (course PDFs/DOCX/PPTX/ZIPs), not values requested explicitly by any instruction — worth revisiting if a real course turns out to host larger legitimate files.

**Files changed this step**: `backend/storage/crud.py`, `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py`, `backend/storage/text_extraction.py`, `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_document_skip_logic.py` (rewritten), `backend/tests/test_assignment_course_mapping.py` (new).

## 2026-09-19 (Secondary, course-page-based assignment discovery — closes the previous entry's stated limitation)

- **Time**: 2026-09-19, continuing the same day as the two entries above.
- **Files inspected before changing anything**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py` (`_scan_page_for_resources`, `_sync_course_resources`, `_sync_course_assignments`, `run_sync`) — the only file this step needed to change. No other file was touched.

### The gap this closes

Before this change, the **only** assignment-discovery path was Moodle's dashboard Timeline block (`_sync_course_assignments()`). The Timeline only ever surfaces an assignment that (a) has a due date and (b) currently falls inside whatever window Moodle's own Timeline display logic considers relevant ("Next 7 days", "Overdue", etc.). An assignment with no due date, one further out than the Timeline currently shows, or one that a particular theme/Moodle version simply doesn't render into the Timeline template at all was never discovered by this project's sync, no matter how many times it ran — this was called out as a known, unresolved limitation in the previous entry.

### The fix: a secondary, additive discovery path through each active course's own page(s)

`_sync_course_resources()`/`_scan_page_for_resources()` already visit every active course's main page (and any additional multi-page-display section pages — the existing pagination mechanism, reused rather than duplicated) while scanning for downloadable resources. Every `/mod/assign/` link on those pages was previously seen and immediately discarded (`continue`d, with a comment saying assignments are "synced separately, via the timeline"). That comment described a design choice that turned out to be the actual root cause of the missing-assignment gap. The fix does not add any new page visits for *discovery itself* — it simply stops throwing those links away:

- `_scan_page_for_resources()` now takes two new optional parameters, `assignment_hrefs_seen` (a set, shared across a course's main + section pages, so the same assignment link isn't collected twice from two different pages of the same course) and `assignment_candidates` (a list, appended to in place). Every `/mod/assign/` link encountered is resolved to an absolute URL and recorded as `{"href", "name", "course_name", "course_moodle_id"}` — never persisted as a `Resource` (unchanged from before).
- `_sync_course_resources()` now returns a fourth element, `assignment_candidates` — every assignment link found across that one course's page(s), each already carrying that course's own real `course_moodle_id` (derived directly from the course page's own URL via the existing `_course_moodle_id()` helper — not a DOM text/ancestor guess). This makes the course-page path's course-mapping **strictly more reliable** than the Timeline path's ancestor-text-walk fallback, which is a real, incidental improvement beyond just "finds more assignments."
- A new function, `_sync_course_page_assignments(page, candidates, already_persisted_moodle_ids)`, persists each candidate via the same `storage.crud.save_assignment()` used by the Timeline path (so it benefits automatically from every existing fix to that function: stable-`moodle_id` course mapping, non-destructive updates, rich "course not found" diagnostics). No due-date text is reliably available from a generic course-page link (unlike the Timeline, which has due-date text right next to it), so `due_date=None` is always passed here — `save_assignment()`'s existing non-destructive-update behavior (from the previous entry's fix) means this can never erase a real due date a prior Timeline-based sync already recorded; it can only ever add one where none existed, never remove one.
- **Deduplication with the Timeline path**: `_sync_course_assignments()` (the Timeline path) now also returns the set of every assignment `moodle_id` it actually persisted this run. `run_sync()` passes that set into `_sync_course_page_assignments()`, which skips (does not re-fetch, does not re-save, does not count as a "new discovery") any candidate whose `moodle_id` is already in that set. Since both paths derive an assignment's `moodle_id` the same way (`_course_moodle_id()` applied to the assignment's own `/mod/assign/view.php?id=N` URL), an assignment found by both paths always resolves to the exact same id — the two paths can never disagree about identity, so this dedup is exact, not heuristic. This also means the two paths could never have produced a duplicate database row even without this explicit skip (the underlying `Assignment.moodle_id`-keyed upsert in `save_assignment()` already prevents that) — the skip exists purely to avoid the wasted, redundant work of re-navigating to fetch a submission status Moodle already gave a real answer for.
- **Pipeline order**: the course-page pass runs in `run_sync()` immediately after the Timeline pass, using the Timeline's persisted-id set built during that same run — not a previous sync's leftover state.
- **Active-course restriction preserved, not re-implemented**: since assignment candidates are only ever collected while scanning pages of courses already in the `active_courses` list (exactly the same list, and the same page visits, that resource discovery already restricts itself to), an assignment on a course that isn't currently "in progress" is never even seen, let alone persisted — there was no separate filter to add here.
- **Malformed links handled gracefully**: `_course_moodle_id()` (a simple `id=(\d+)` regex against the href) returns `None` for a `/mod/assign/` link with no parseable numeric id (e.g. missing a query string entirely) — `_sync_course_page_assignments()` logs this and skips that one candidate, continuing with the rest.
- **Individual course failures already isolated, verified not re-broken**: this step's whole discovery mechanism piggybacks on `_sync_course_resources()`'s page visits, which were already wrapped in a per-course `try/except` by the previous entry's Section 5 fix — a course whose page fails to load entirely contributes zero resources AND zero assignment candidates (an empty list, from a function call that never got the chance to run), and the rest of the sync (other active courses, both discovery paths) proceeds unaffected. The course-page assignment-persistence pass itself is separately wrapped in its own `try/except` in `run_sync()` too, so a failure there can't take down Timeline-based discovery, which already ran and returned by that point.
- **Pagination**: "pagination" in this codebase's only existing sense is Moodle's "one section per page" multi-page course display, already handled by `_find_additional_section_pages()`/the `additional_sections` loop in `_sync_course_resources()` — since assignment-candidate collection was added directly inside the same `_scan_page_for_resources()` call already used for both the main course page and every additional section page, course-page assignment discovery automatically covers a multi-page course exactly as completely as resource discovery already does, with no separate pagination logic needed.

### Testing

- `tests/test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` (**new**, 10 checks) — a real, self-contained fake Moodle server (its own login form, session cookies, dashboard, course pages, assignment pages) driven end-to-end through the real `run_sync()`, not mocks:
  1. A course-page-only assignment (never present in the Timeline fixture at all) is discovered and persisted with its real name.
  2. An assignment present in both the Timeline and the course page is persisted exactly once — confirmed via a real row count, not an assumption.
  3. The deduplicated assignment's real data is present and correct.
  4. A malformed `/mod/assign/` link with no parseable id is skipped without creating a row for it and without preventing the other two real assignments from being discovered.
  5. A due_date seeded directly into the database before the sync runs (simulating one a prior Timeline-based sync had already recorded) survives a course-page-path save with `due_date=None` completely untouched.
  6. An active course whose URL is genuinely unreachable (points at a local port nothing listens on, so Playwright's navigation fails immediately with a real connection-refused error) does not prevent the other active course's assignments — via either discovery path — from being found; the overall sync still completes successfully.
  7. A course listed on the dashboard but NOT under "Only courses in progress" is never visited at all (confirmed via a real server-side flag set only if that course's page is actually requested) and contributes no assignment to the database, even though its page contains a real, well-formed assignment link.
- `tests/test_sync_service.py`: re-run in full after this change with **zero modifications** — all 56 checks still pass unchanged, confirming the new return-value plumbing (`_sync_course_resources()` gaining a 4th return element, `_sync_course_assignments()` gaining a 3rd) didn't disturb the existing Timeline-only pipeline test's behavior or assertions.
- **Full suite result — every test file run, none skipped, none weakened**: `test_groq_agent.py` 10/10, `test_assignment_action_planner.py` 9/9, `test_followups.py` 9/9, `test_sync_service.py` 56/56, `test_document_retrieval.py` 11/11, `test_text_extraction.py` 10/10, `test_document_skip_logic.py` 34/34, `test_assignment_course_mapping.py` 9/9, `test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` 10/10 — **158/158 passed.**
- No real Moodle read-only sync was run against `lms.flame.edu.in` in this session — only the fake-Moodle-server-backed test suite (including the new dedicated fixture above) was exercised. This is stated explicitly rather than implied otherwise.

### Remaining limitations

- A course-page assignment link carries no reliable due-date text the way a Timeline entry does — an assignment discovered *only* via this secondary path (never via the Timeline, ever) will have `due_date = NULL` in the database until/unless the Timeline surfaces it later. This is an inherent constraint of what a generic course page actually exposes, not a bug in the discovery logic itself.
- Submission-status fetching (`_fetch_submission_status()`) still requires one extra page navigation per newly-discovered assignment, same cost as the existing Timeline path — this step doesn't change that cost, only avoids paying it twice for an assignment both paths would otherwise find.
- As before, real Render deployment behavior and a real Moodle instance sync were not verified from this environment — only the local fake-server-backed test suite.

**Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` (new).
