## 2026-09-19 (Production-readiness audit of the full Moodle sync pipeline)

- **Time**: 2026-09-19, continuing the same day as the three entries above.
- **Scope of this audit**: read through the complete pipeline end to end — Moodle authentication (`moodle/sync_service.py`'s `_login()`) → active-course discovery (`_detect_active_courses()`) → assignment discovery (`_sync_course_assignments()`, `_sync_course_page_assignments()`) → resource discovery (`_sync_course_resources()`) → document download (`moodle/document_downloader.py`) → text extraction (`storage/text_extraction.py`) → database persistence (`storage/crud.py`, `storage/database.py`, `storage/models.py`) → the API layer (`api/routes.py`, `api/queries.py`, `api/schemas.py`) → the frontend's consumption of it (`frontend/src/types/index.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`). Most of this pipeline had already been hardened across the three entries above this session (course-mapping, download timeouts/limits, extraction bounds, secondary assignment discovery) — this pass specifically looked for what those entries hadn't yet covered: the database/API/frontend boundary, restart/crash recovery, and deployment-configuration risks.

### Real, concrete bugs found and fixed

1. **A crashed/killed sync process leaves its `SyncRun` row stuck at `status="running"` forever, permanently blocking every future sync.** `api/routes.py`'s `POST /api/sync/moodle` rejects a new sync with a 409 whenever `storage.crud.get_running_sync_run()` finds an existing `"running"` row — there was no mechanism at all for that row to ever be reconsidered if the process that owned it died before calling `finish_sync_run()` (an out-of-memory kill, a container restart mid-sync, or the ImportError bug below). **Fix**: `get_running_sync_run()` now treats a `"running"` row older than 30 minutes as abandoned — every per-step timeout added elsewhere in this pipeline (login, page loads, per-download deadlines) already bounds a *normal* sync to a much shorter runtime, so a row still `"running"` after that long is almost certainly orphaned, not genuinely in progress. It's rewritten in place to `status="error"` with a clear explanation, and the function returns `None` so a new sync isn't blocked. `api/queries.py`'s `fetch_sync_status()` (the read path behind `GET /api/sync-status` and `GET /api/dashboard/summary`) got the equivalent read-only check — it reports the same honest `"error"` state to the frontend even if nobody happens to re-trigger a sync, without itself mutating the database on every poll (a GET endpoint shouldn't have that side effect; the real rewrite only happens via `get_running_sync_run()`, when a sync is actually attempted).
2. **The actual, most likely *cause* of exactly that stuck-forever scenario**: `api/routes.py`'s `_run_moodle_sync()` (the `BackgroundTasks` callback for a triggered sync) did `from moodle.sync_service import ... run_sync` **before** its own `try/except` block, not inside it. If that import itself fails — e.g. Playwright or its Chromium binary genuinely missing from whatever container image is actually running, regardless of what `backend/Dockerfile` currently installs — the resulting `ImportError` propagates straight out of the whole background-task callback, uncaught, and `finish_sync_run()` is never called. The triggering `SyncRun` row is left at `"running"` forever with zero explanation, exactly the scenario fix #1 above now recovers from — but recovering from it 30 minutes later is a poor substitute for reporting the real failure immediately. **Fix**: the import moved inside the `try` block, with a new, explicit `except ImportError` branch that calls `finish_sync_run(status="error", ...)` immediately with a clear message. Verified as a real, reproducible bug (not a hypothetical) — `tests/test_production_readiness.py` check 9 forces this exact import failure (via `sys.modules["moodle.sync_service"] = None`, a standard Python technique for simulating an unimportable module without needing to actually uninstall anything) and confirms the SyncRun row now ends at `status="error"` with a real message instead of staying stuck at `"running"`.
3. **`Assignment.submission_status` — real data the sync pipeline already scrapes and persists (`_fetch_submission_status()`, called by both the Timeline and course-page discovery paths) — was never exposed through the API at all.** `api/schemas.py`'s `AssignmentOut` and `api/queries.py`'s `assignment_out()` mapped every other `Assignment` column except this one; the frontend's `Assignment` TypeScript type had no matching field either, so this data was silently unreachable end-to-end despite being correctly discovered and stored. **Fix**: added `submissionStatus: str | None = None` to `AssignmentOut` (defaulted, so existing callers/tests constructing one without it don't break — caught immediately by `tests/test_followups.py`'s own `AssignmentOut(**base)` helper, which needed no change once the default was added), wired it through `assignment_out()`, and added the matching optional field to `frontend/src/types/index.ts`'s `Assignment` interface. No frontend UI currently reads this field (confirmed by searching the frontend source) — this is additive plumbing, not a UI change, so nothing already working could regress; `npx tsc -b` confirmed a clean frontend build afterward.
4. **`moodle/sync_service.py`'s own module docstring was actively wrong.** It stated "The production Docker image (`backend/Dockerfile`) deliberately excludes Playwright... triggering a sync from a deployed container will fail with a clear `MoodleSyncError`" — but `backend/Dockerfile` (inspected directly, not assumed) has since been changed to install Playwright *and* `playwright install --with-deps chromium`, specifically so the deployed sync works. Left as-is, this stale comment could mislead a future maintainer into believing the deployed sync is expected to fail, or into re-removing Playwright from the image "to match the docs." **Fix**: rewritten to state the current, verified-by-reading Dockerfile behavior, and to note the real, distinct failure mode this audit's fix #2 above addresses (an `ImportError` if Playwright genuinely isn't present, vs. a clean `MoodleSyncError` from a login/page-structure failure).
5. **A hard-to-notice error-masking bug in `run_sync()`'s cleanup.** Its `finally` block called `context.close()` then `browser.close()` with no exception handling of their own — if either raised (a real possibility when the browser/context has already crashed, e.g. from the same class of out-of-memory event that motivates fix #1), Python's ordinary "an exception raised in `finally` replaces whatever exception was already propagating" behavior would silently swap the SyncRun's real recorded error for a confusing "cleanup failed" one instead, hiding the actual root cause from anyone reading `SyncRun.error_message` or the logs afterward. **Fix**: each `.close()` call now wrapped in its own `try/except`, logged and ignored rather than allowed to propagate or mask anything.

### Diagnostics added (no secrets, per instruction)

- **`main.py`'s startup log now reports where the database and documents actually live**, and explicitly warns when `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` is unset (the container's own, likely-non-persistent filesystem is being used as a fallback — `storage/paths.py`'s `get_data_dir()`). This has been a recurring, explicitly-flagged unknown across this project's last several BUILD_LOG entries ("not verified against Render," "no Render dashboard access exists from this environment") — this makes the actual resolved path and whether it's the ephemeral fallback directly checkable from the deployed instance's own startup logs, with zero guessing required and zero secrets involved (just a filesystem path and a boolean).
- **`GET /api/sync/moodle/diagnostics`** (an existing, already-documented temporary diagnostic endpoint) now also returns `dataDir` (the resolved path) and `dataDirEphemeralFallback` (whether `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` is unset) — the same information as the startup log, but reachable from a running Render instance via an HTTP request instead of needing log access, matching this endpoint's existing purpose.

### Reviewed and confirmed already correct (no change needed)

- **Cookie/redirect handling** for both Moodle authentication (`_login()`, Playwright's own cookie jar, real browser-context session) and document downloads (`_stream_resource_to_disk()`'s manual `Cookie` header built from `page.context.cookies(url)`, `urllib`'s automatic redirect-following) — already correct, reviewed as part of this audit's explicit focus areas, not changed.
- **Assignment/document/resource deduplication** — `moodle_id`-based upserts for `Course`/`Assignment`/`Resource`, `file_path`-based upsert for `Document`, all already fixed across the earlier entries this session. Verified `Resource.moodle_id`'s implicit assumption (that a bare numeric Moodle activity/course id, `cmid`, is globally unique across an entire Moodle install, not merely unique within one course) is a real, correct property of Moodle's own module-id numbering scheme — not a gap.
- **Database restart/persistence behavior**: `storage/database.py`'s `init_db()` (`create_all()` + an explicit incremental-column migration for older databases) already runs on every startup (`main.py`'s `lifespan`), safely handling both a fresh, empty `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` and an existing, older-schema database. `DATABASE_URL` being computed once at import time (not re-read per request) was already a known, documented, and correctly-handled constraint (tests already account for it by using subprocesses when the env var needs to change) — not a bug.
- **Per-item failure isolation** across the whole discovery/download/extraction pipeline (one bad course, resource, assignment, or document never aborting the rest of a sync) — already fixed across the earlier entries this session; re-verified here, not re-implemented.
- **Malformed Moodle pages / missing metadata** — course-mapping fallback logic, non-destructive field updates, and graceful handling of pages missing expected DOM structure were all already addressed in the earlier entries; this audit found no additional gap in that area beyond the ones already fixed.

### Testing

- `tests/test_production_readiness.py` (**new**, 12 checks): stale `"running"` `SyncRun` recovery (both the mutating `get_running_sync_run()` path and the read-only `fetch_sync_status()` path, confirmed NOT to mutate the database itself), a regression guard that a genuinely fresh `"running"` row still correctly blocks a concurrent sync trigger, a forced `ImportError` proving `_run_moodle_sync()` now reports a clean `"error"` instead of leaving the row orphaned, and `Assignment.submission_status` reaching `assignment_out()`'s output correctly.
- `tests/test_followups.py`: no logic changes needed, but its own `AssignmentOut(**base)` test helper immediately surfaced that `submissionStatus` needed a default value (a real, useful regression check firing exactly as intended) — fixed by defaulting the new field to `None` in `api/schemas.py`, not by changing the test.
- **Full suite result — every test file run, none skipped, none weakened**: `test_groq_agent.py` 10/10, `test_assignment_action_planner.py` 9/9, `test_followups.py` 9/9, `test_sync_service.py` 56/56, `test_document_retrieval.py` 11/11, `test_text_extraction.py` 10/10, `test_document_skip_logic.py` 34/34, `test_assignment_course_mapping.py` 9/9, `test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` 10/10, `test_production_readiness.py` 12/12 — **170/170 passed.**
- Frontend: `npx tsc -b` — clean, no type errors, after adding the `submissionStatus` field to `frontend/src/types/index.ts`.
- **No real Moodle sync or real Render deployment was exercised or verified in this audit** — every check above runs against this project's own local SQLite database and (where a full pipeline run was needed) the existing fake-Moodle-server test fixtures from earlier entries. This is stated explicitly, not implied otherwise, per instruction.

### Risks and limitations noted but NOT fixed (flagged for a human decision, not silently ignored)

- **A resource whose only stable-enough identifier is its own URL** (the fallback used when a resource's href has no parseable `id=` parameter — e.g. some direct `pluginfile.php`-style links) could, in principle, produce a duplicate `Resource` row if Moodle ever regenerates that URL (a revision/timestamp component changing) for what a human would consider "the same" file. This project's existing test fixtures and this audit's reading of `_classify_resource_type()`/the resource-discovery code found no evidence this actually happens for the resource shapes currently in use (`/mod/resource/view.php` wrapper pages dominate, and those always carry a stable `id=`) — flagged as a theoretical risk worth watching for in real Moodle data, not fixed speculatively without a concrete failing case to design against.
- **The 30-minute stale-`SyncRun` threshold is a reasonable default, not a value derived from a real measured worst-case sync duration** on the actual `lms.flame.edu.in` instance (course/resource/assignment counts there are unknown from this environment) — worth revisiting if a real sync legitimately takes longer than that.
- **`Assignment.description` and `Resource.description` remain always-`NULL`** in practice — the sync pipeline never populates them (Timeline/course-page scraping doesn't currently extract assignment/resource descriptions) even though both the schema and the API already support them. Not a bug (nothing overwrites a value that was never being set), but worth noting as unused schema surface.
- **Production frontend deployment requires `VITE_API_BASE_URL` to be set at build time** (`frontend/src/lib/api.ts` defaults to `http://localhost:8000` otherwise) and the backend's `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` env var to match for CORS — both already designed for this, confirmed by reading the code, but neither was verified against a real deployed pair of instances from this environment.

**Files changed this step**: `backend/storage/crud.py`, `backend/api/queries.py`, `backend/api/routes.py`, `backend/api/schemas.py`, `backend/main.py`, `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_production_readiness.py` (new), `Esmerelda/frontend/src/types/index.ts`.

## 2026-09-19 (Production-readiness audit, continued: resource dedup collision, sync-staleness concurrency, assignment descriptions, Render deployment hygiene)

- **Time**: 2026-09-19, continuing the same day as the four entries above. This entry resolves two of the "risks flagged but not fixed" items from the entry directly above it (the resource-dedup collision risk, and the unpopulated `Assignment.description`) and extends the sync-staleness work with a real concurrency fix and a configurable threshold.

### 1. Resource deduplication: the collision risk was real, not merely theoretical

Investigated `moodle/sync_service.py`'s fallback identifier (`moodle_id = _course_moodle_id(href) or href`, used when a resource's link carries no `id=` query parameter). Confirmed this fallback path is genuinely exercised, not dead code: every real Moodle activity-module view page (`/mod/resource/view.php?id=N`, and the same shape for Quiz/Page/Link/Folder) always carries a stable, globally-unique `id=` (Moodle's own cmid numbering scheme) — but a **direct file link** (an instructor-pasted external URL, a file dragged straight into a Label/Page's rich-text body, or an expanded Folder's individual file listing — Moodle genuinely renders all of these as raw links, often to a `pluginfile.php` URL) has no such id at all. This project's own existing test fixture (`tests/test_sync_service.py`'s `<a href="/files/course-syllabus.docx">`) already exercises exactly this shape.

**The concrete risk**: `Resource.moodle_id` is unique *globally* in the schema, not scoped per course. Using the bare href as that fallback identifier means the exact same file URL linked from two *different* courses — a realistic scenario: a shared syllabus template, a common reading, an external link reused across sections of the same course — would compute the identical `moodle_id`, and the second course's `save_resource()` call would silently steal that Resource row's `course_id` away from the first course instead of creating a second, correctly separate resource.

**Fix**: new `_resource_moodle_id(href, course_moodle_id, resource_type)` in `moodle/sync_service.py`. Unchanged for the common case (a real `id=` is always preferred). For the fallback case, builds a composite identifier (`resource:{course_moodle_id}:{resource_type}:{href}`) that folds the course's own moodle_id into the identifier — making the cross-course collision structurally impossible, since two different courses now always produce two different composite ids for the same shared URL.

**Known, accepted one-time side effect**: any resource row already persisted (in a real deployment) under the *old* bare-href identifier will not match the new composite identifier on the next sync — this creates exactly one new row (with the old row surviving, now simply stale/unlinked from future syncs) rather than continuing to silently share/steal identity. This is a one-time, self-correcting transition, not ongoing duplication — proven directly by `tests/test_resource_dedup.py`'s migration-scenario check, which also confirms a second sync after the transition does not create a third row.

### 2. Sync staleness: made configurable, and closed a real concurrency race in the trigger endpoint

- **Investigated whether a legitimate sync could exceed the previous entry's hardcoded 30-minute threshold**: no real historical sync logs exist to inspect (no deployed Render instance, no real Moodle account available from this environment) — this is stated honestly, not glossed over. Reasoned from this pipeline's own per-step timeout bounds instead: login (~30s) + per-course page visits (30s each) + per-assignment submission-status fetches (~20s each) + per-document downloads, each individually capped at a 300-second deadline. For an institution with many active courses and many large documents, the download term alone could plausibly exceed 30 minutes. Since the real worst case depends on how much content an actual Moodle account has (unknown here), the threshold is now configurable rather than a guess baked into the code.
- **Fix**: `ESMERELDA_STALE_SYNC_MINUTES` environment variable (`storage/crud.py`'s `_get_stale_sync_run_minutes()`), defaulting to 30, re-read on every call (no restart needed to change it), falling back safely to the default (with a logged warning) on a missing, non-numeric, or non-positive value — confirmed not to crash on any of those inputs.
- **A genuine, separate bug found while writing the "concurrent sync requests" test**: `api/routes.py`'s `POST /api/sync/moodle` handler previously called `get_running_sync_run()` and `create_sync_run()` as two separate function calls — two separate database transactions. Two near-simultaneous requests could each see "nothing running" from the first call before either had made its own insert from the second, and both would go on to create their own `"running"` row and launch a fully separate Playwright/Chromium session logging into the same Moodle account at the same time. **Fix**: new `storage.crud.try_start_new_sync_run()` — the check-and-create is now a single atomic SQL statement (`INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS (...)`), which SQLite executes atomically with respect to other connections (no window exists between "check" and "act" because there is only one statement). Verified as a real, reproducible race, not a hypothetical: `tests/test_sync_staleness.py`'s concurrency check fires 10 genuinely concurrent threads at this function against a clean database and confirms **exactly one** succeeds — the same test against the old two-call pattern would not reliably give that guarantee.
- **A second, distinct bug found while testing "missing or invalid timestamps" against real code, not assumptions**: reading a `SyncRun` row via the ORM's normal typed `started_at` column access raises a bare, unhandled `ValueError` if the stored value is a malformed (non-null but unparseable) string — confirmed directly by reproducing it. This would have crashed `get_running_sync_run()` (breaking `POST /api/sync/moodle` entirely) and `api/queries.py`'s `fetch_sync_status()` (breaking `GET /api/sync-status` and `GET /api/dashboard/summary`) on any row with corrupted timestamp data. **Fix**: both functions now read `started_at` via raw, untyped SQL first and parse it defensively (`_parse_started_at()`, never raises) before ever touching the ORM's typed column access; `fetch_sync_status()` additionally wraps its ORM fetch in a `try/except ValueError` as a second layer of defense. A genuinely `NULL` `started_at` was checked and confirmed **impossible** to reach in practice — the schema's own `NOT NULL` constraint rejects it at the database level before any application code runs — so this scenario needed no separate handling, only the malformed-non-null case did.
- **A related landmine bug found and fixed in the same area**: healing a stale/malformed row previously left its corrupted `started_at` value in place — meaning ANY future ordinary ORM query touching that same row (an admin listing, a future feature) would keep re-triggering the identical crash forever. The healing update now also overwrites `started_at` itself (only when it was the actual problem) so the row becomes safely readable again going forward.

### 3. Assignment/Resource descriptions: implemented for Assignment only, after confirming it's genuinely available at zero extra cost

Investigated whether `Assignment.description`/`Resource.description` (both already present in the schema, in `AssignmentOut`/`ResourceOut`, and in the frontend's TypeScript types — confirmed by reading all three layers — but never populated by the sync pipeline) should be filled in.

- **Assignment**: `_fetch_assignment_page_details()` (replacing `_fetch_submission_status()`, same function, same single page visit) now also reads Moodle's `#intro` container — mod_assign's own core view.php template output, not a theme-specific class — for the assignment's description text, capped at 2000 characters. This costs **zero additional page navigations**: the assignment's own page was already being visited to read its submission-status table. `storage/crud.py`'s `save_assignment()` gained a `description` parameter with the same non-destructive-update rule as every other field here (a later sync pass that couldn't extract one never erases a previously-known real description).
- **Resource**: deliberately left unpopulated. No existing page visit reads a resource's own view page (only its course's page, where just the link and name are visible) — extracting a description would require an entirely new per-resource navigation, adding real time/session-loss risk to every sync, with no existing evidence this data is needed. Per instruction not to add fields "merely for completeness," this was not implemented.
- **Explicitly not claimed**: the `#intro` selector is a reasoned, Moodle-core-level assumption, **not verified against a real, live Moodle instance** (no real Moodle account was available to this audit). If `lms.flame.edu.in`'s actual theme renders this differently, extraction simply keeps returning `None` — the same safe default every other best-effort field in this file already falls back to; nothing downstream treats a missing description as an error.

### 4. Render deployment readiness

- **Fixed stale "Railway" terminology** in `Dockerfile`, `main.py`, `storage/paths.py`, and `.env.example` — comments referred to Railway Volumes/config throughout, left over from an earlier, since-abandoned deployment target, while the actual, current target (confirmed from this task's own framing and this project's `BUILD_LOG.md` history) is Render. Functionally harmless (both platforms inject `$PORT` and support an attachable persistent disk the same way), but actively confusing for anyone reading these files to configure a real Render deployment. Rewritten to name Render specifically, and to point at `main.py`'s startup log as the way to confirm the real, running configuration.
- **`GET /health` previously always returned `{"status": "ok"}` unconditionally**, regardless of whether the database was actually reachable — a health check that can never fail is worse than none, since a platform like Render uses it to decide whether to keep routing traffic to (and whether to restart) an instance. **Fix**: now attempts a trivial `SELECT 1` against the real database engine, returning 503 with a (secret-free — SQLite has no connection credentials to leak) error detail if it fails, 200/ok otherwise.
- **Found and flagged (with the user's explicit direction on how far to go) — real, personal data committed to git**: `Esmerelda/backend/storage/esmerelda.db` (the real local dev SQLite database) and ~30 real downloaded course documents (actual PDFs/PPTX/XLSX/ZIPs) under `Esmerelda/backend/storage/documents/` are committed directly into this repository's git history, and were **not** covered by any existing `.gitignore` rule — confirmed directly via `git ls-files`, not assumed. Any script or test run without `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` set writes straight into this same tracked database file. Per the user's explicit choice (asked directly, not assumed), `Esmerelda/.gitignore` was updated to prevent this from happening again going forward — the **already-committed** files were deliberately left as-is, untouched in git history and still tracked; a future `git rm --cached` (on both the database file and the documents directory) is a separate, explicit decision the user chose not to make in this session, called out clearly in the remaining risks below and the final report.
- **Confirmed already correct, not changed**: Playwright + Chromium ARE installed in the production image (`Dockerfile`'s `playwright install --with-deps chromium`) — this reverses what an earlier `BUILD_LOG.md` entry and (until this audit) `moodle/sync_service.py`'s own module docstring both still claimed; `MOODLE_USERNAME`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD`/`MOODLE_URL` are documented in `.env.example` (updated this session to also mention the new `ESMERELDA_STALE_SYNC_MINUTES`); `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` startup diagnostics (added in the entry two above this one) were re-verified, not re-implemented.

### Testing

- `tests/test_resource_dedup.py` (**new**, 10 checks): a real activity href is unaffected by this fix; the exact same shared URL from two different courses now produces two different composite identifiers; a full-pipeline proof that persisting from two courses creates two real, correctly-attached rows (not one stolen row); a migration-transition check proving an old bare-href row survives the switch to the new identifier scheme, with exactly one new row created and no third row on a repeated sync.
- `tests/test_sync_staleness.py` (**new**, 18 checks): active sync below threshold untouched; stale sync above threshold healed; a `NULL` `started_at` confirmed rejected by the schema itself (not a gap needing handling); a malformed non-null `started_at` confirmed to crash the *old* code path and now handled cleanly, in both `get_running_sync_run()` and `fetch_sync_status()`; the threshold genuinely configurable via the env var, with safe fallback on invalid/negative values; **10 genuinely concurrent threads** attempting to start a sync against a clean database, confirming exactly one succeeds and exactly one `"running"` row exists afterward.
- `tests/test_assignment_description.py` (**new**, 7 checks): real extraction against a real headless-Chromium-rendered page with a real `#intro` container; a page with no `#intro` yields `None`, not a crash; the 2000-character cap is enforced; `save_assignment()`'s description is preserved across a pass with no new value, and correctly updated when a real new one is given.
- `tests/test_deployment_readiness.py` (**new**, 9 checks): `/health` reports 200/ok against a genuinely reachable database and 503/error against a genuinely broken one (via a real monkeypatched engine, not a mock assumption) with no secret in the error detail; startup logs correctly distinguish the ephemeral-fallback case from the explicit-`ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` case.
- **Full suite result — every test file run, none skipped, none weakened**: `test_groq_agent.py` 10/10, `test_assignment_action_planner.py` 9/9, `test_followups.py` 9/9, `test_sync_service.py` 56/56, `test_document_retrieval.py` 11/11, `test_text_extraction.py` 10/10, `test_document_skip_logic.py` 34/34, `test_assignment_course_mapping.py` 9/9, `test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` 10/10, `test_production_readiness.py` 12/12, `test_resource_dedup.py` 10/10, `test_sync_staleness.py` 18/18, `test_assignment_description.py` 7/7, `test_deployment_readiness.py` 9/9 — **214/214 passed.**
- Frontend: `npx tsc -b` — clean, no type errors (no frontend files needed changes this session — `description`/`submissionStatus` fields already existed in the TypeScript types from the previous entry and this investigation respectively).
- **No real Moodle sync or real Render deployment was exercised or verified in this audit** — every check above runs against this project's own local SQLite database, a real local headless Chromium instance against controlled local HTML fixtures, and (where a full pipeline run was needed) the existing fake-Moodle-server test fixtures from earlier entries. Stated explicitly, not implied otherwise.

### Remaining risks NOT fixed (flagged for a human decision)

- **`Esmerelda/backend/storage/esmerelda.db` and `storage/documents/` remain tracked in git history** — the `.gitignore` fix only prevents *new* accidental commits; removing them from tracking (`git rm --cached`) and considering whether git history itself needs cleaning (if this repository is ever made public, past commits would still contain this real content) is a separate, explicit decision for the user, not made in this session.
- **The `#intro` selector for assignment descriptions is unverified against the real Moodle instance** — a real, safe default (`None`) applies if it doesn't match; worth confirming with real Moodle access.
- **The 30-minute default stale-sync threshold is still a reasoned estimate, not a measured value** — now at least correctable via `ESMERELDA_STALE_SYNC_MINUTES` without a code change if it proves wrong in practice.
- **`Resource.description` remains unpopulated** — a deliberate choice (see above), not an oversight, but worth revisiting if a real need for it surfaces.
- As in every entry above, **no real Render deployment or real Moodle account was available to verify any of this against the actual, live target environment** — every fix and test in this entry is verified against local, controlled substitutes only.

## 2026-09-19 (Fix: assignment due dates showing "Unknown"; fix: AI claiming "no tracked assignments" for a real course)

- **Time**: 2026-09-19, continuing the same day as the entries above. Scope deliberately narrow per instruction: only the two reported symptoms below were investigated and fixed — the Moodle scraper's overall architecture, sync concurrency, and document downloads were not touched.

### Investigation (done first, before any code change)

Inspected the real local dev database directly (`storage/esmerelda.db`) before assuming anything:
- Both real tracked assignments (`Assessment 2`, `Assessment 3`) already had correct, non-null `due_date` values stored (`2026-09-17 23:59:00`, `2026-09-18 23:59:00`).
- Both belong to course id 4, whose stored name is `"DESG319-UGSEM5-2026/27S1-Introduction to Artificial Intelligence & Machine Lear"` — genuinely truncated mid-word at 79 characters. Confirmed this is not caused by anything in this project's own code (no slicing/truncation of course names exists anywhere in `moodle/sync_service.py`/`storage/crud.py`/`storage/models.py` — grepped directly) — it reflects how Moodle's own dashboard rendered/served the course's fullname text at scrape time, which this task's instructions explicitly say not to change ("do not change the Moodle scraper architecture").
- Called `queries.assignment_out()` and hit the real `GET /api/courses/4/assignments` / `GET /api/assignments` endpoints directly (via `TestClient`): both correctly returned `"dueDate": "2026-09-17T23:59:00"` — a valid ISO 8601 string. Verified in real Node that this exact string parses correctly and formats correctly through the frontend's own `formatDateTime()` (`frontend/src/lib/utils.ts`). **No due-date loss exists anywhere in the current storage → API → frontend-formatting chain for data that's actually been captured with a due date.**
- Ran the exact reported query ("What are my assignments for DESG319-UGSEM5-2026/27S1-Introduction to Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning?") through the deterministic fallback agent (`api/deterministic_agent.py`) directly: it correctly found the course and both assignments with correct due dates. The bug is not in that code path.
- Read every LLM-facing tool (`api/gemini_tools.py`, shared by both the Groq and Gemini function-calling agents) and found the real, concrete gap: **`get_course_info(course_query)` — the only tool whose description mentions looking up "one specific course" — returns only `tracked_assignment_count`, an integer, never the assignments themselves.** The only tool that returns real assignment objects is `get_upcoming_assignments()`, which is **global** (every course) and hard-capped at `MAX_RESULTS = 8`, ranked by urgency across the whole account — a specific course's assignments can be entirely absent from that list if the account has more than 8 assignments overall with higher urgency elsewhere. This is the root cause of "the AI says there are no tracked assignments" for a real, tracked course: when asked about a specific course, the model had no tool that could give it that course's actual assignment list and due dates — only a bare count (from `get_course_info`) or an unfiltered, capped, cross-course list (from `get_upcoming_assignments`) that may not include the course at all.
- Separately, read `moodle/sync_service.py`'s secondary, course-page-based assignment discovery path (`_sync_course_page_assignments()`, added in an earlier entry this session) and confirmed it **always** passed `due_date=None` to `save_assignment()` — by design at the time, since a generic course-page link carries no due-date text the way a Timeline entry does. Any assignment discovered *only* through that path (never via the Timeline — e.g. one that's aged out of Timeline's display window, or on a course/theme combination where the Timeline template doesn't render it) is therefore permanently stuck with a real, un-displayable `NULL` due date — which the frontend correctly, but unhelpfully, renders as "Unknown." This is a second, real, root-caused source of the reported "Unknown" symptom (distinct from, and in addition to, the LLM-tooling gap above), reachable even though the two real assignments in this local dev database happen not to be affected by it.

### Fix 1 — due dates: extended existing extraction, did not change scraper architecture

`moodle/sync_service.py`'s `_fetch_assignment_page_details()` (already visiting each assignment's own page once, for submission status and description — added in an earlier entry) now ALSO reads that same already-loaded page for a "Due date" label, reusing the *exact* date/time regex and parsing already trusted for Timeline text (`_DUE_DATE_PATTERN`/`_parse_due_date_match()`, factored out of the existing `_extract_due_date()` without changing its behavior at all) via a new, additive `_extract_due_date_from_assignment_page()`. This costs **zero additional page navigations** — it's the same page visit that already existed.
- **Timeline path** (`_sync_course_assignments()`): the Timeline's own due-date extraction remains the primary source, completely unchanged in behavior — the assignment page's own "Due date" label is only consulted as a **fallback** (`candidate["due_date"] or page_due_date`) when the Timeline text search found nothing, never overriding a value it already found. This directly satisfies the instruction to preserve the existing extraction logic.
- **Course-page path** (`_sync_course_page_assignments()`): `due_date=None` (hardcoded) replaced with the real extracted value — strictly an improvement, since `None` was always the previous behavior there.
- **Explicitly not verified against a real Moodle instance** (no real Moodle account was available) — the `#intro`/"Due date" label assumptions are reasoned from Moodle's own core `mod_assign` template conventions, not confirmed live. If the real theme renders this differently, extraction returns `None`, the same safe default every other best-effort field in this file already falls back to — no crash, no fabricated date.
- **No database schema change was made** — `Assignment.due_date` already existed and was already the right type; this only affects when/how it gets populated.

### Fix 2 — course-specific assignment queries: a real per-course tool, backed by a deterministic database lookup

- **`api/matching.py`'s `match_course()` rewritten (same public signature, same return type) to try, in order**: (1) an exact Moodle course id present in the query, (2) `Course.short_name` (exact/substring), (3) the existing course-code prefix check (unchanged), (4) a normalized, separator-stripped full-name substring check tolerant of truncation on **either** side (a length-gated reverse direction to avoid a short/generic query trivially matching an unrelated long course name), (5) the existing subject-word fallback (unchanged). This directly satisfies "support matching by exact course short name, Moodle course ID, and normalized course name" and "make sure long course names and truncated user input work" — verified against the real, truncated stored course name from the local dev database and the user's exact, full reported query string.
- **New tool: `get_course_assignments(course_query)`** (`api/gemini_tools.py`, wired into both the Gemini automatic-function-calling tool list and — via a new hand-written schema in `api/groq_tools.py` — the Groq manual tool-calling loop). Resolves the course via the enhanced `match_course()` above (a real database lookup, never an LLM guess — satisfying the explicit instruction) and returns that course's **complete** real assignment list (name, due date, urgency, recommendation, submission status), with no `MAX_RESULTS`-style cap the way the cross-course tool has, and correctly populates `ToolCollector.assignments` — the same structured data `ChatResponseOut.assignments` is built from, which is what actually puts real assignment cards (with real due dates) in front of the user, not just prose. `get_course_info()`'s description was updated to explicitly point at the new tool for "the actual list," reducing the chance the model settles for the count-only tool when asked for details.
- A course that's real but genuinely has zero tracked assignments returns an explicit note distinguishing it from the reported false-negative bug; a query matching no course at all returns a real error with the list of available courses, never a fabricated empty success.

### Testing

- `tests/test_assignment_description.py` (extended, now 9 checks — was 7): two new checks proving `_extract_due_date_from_assignment_page()` correctly parses a real "Due date" label into a real datetime, and correctly returns `None` (not a crash or a fabricated date) when no such label exists.
- `tests/test_course_query_matching.py` (**new**, 15 checks): `match_course()` resolving the same real course via its full (truncated-at-storage) name, a bare Moodle id, its course code, and truncated user input, while correctly matching nothing for unrelated text; the new `get_course_assignments` tool returning the real course and both real assignments with correct due dates for the exact reported query string; the structured `ToolCollector` data (what the frontend actually renders) populated correctly; a genuinely assignment-less course reported honestly; a genuinely unmatched course query reported as a real error; and the real `GET /api/assignments` HTTP JSON response round-tripping a real due date end-to-end.
- `tests/test_groq_agent.py`: one existing assertion (the expected Groq tool-name set) updated to include the new `get_course_assignments` tool — the only change to a pre-existing test in this entry, and it was a necessary, honest update to a hardcoded list, not a weakening of what it checks.
- **Full suite result — every test file run, none skipped, none weakened**: `test_assignment_action_planner.py` 9/9, `test_assignment_course_mapping.py` 9/9, `test_assignment_description.py` 9/9, `test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` 10/10, `test_course_query_matching.py` 15/15 (new), `test_deployment_readiness.py` 9/9, `test_document_retrieval.py` 11/11, `test_document_skip_logic.py` 34/34, `test_followups.py` 9/9, `test_groq_agent.py` 10/10, `test_production_readiness.py` 12/12, `test_resource_dedup.py` 10/10, `test_sync_service.py` 56/56, `test_sync_staleness.py` 18/18, `test_text_extraction.py` 10/10 — **231/231 passed.**
- Frontend: `npx tsc -b` — clean; no frontend files were changed in this entry (the `dueDate`/`submissionStatus` fields and their rendering were already correct — the bug was never in the frontend).
- **No real Moodle sync or real Render deployment was exercised or verified for this fix** — verified against the real local dev database's actual (imperfect, truncated) data, real headless-Chromium-driven extraction against controlled local HTML fixtures, and the real HTTP API via `TestClient`. Stated explicitly, not implied otherwise.

### Remaining limitations

- An assignment that is genuinely never mentioned on its own Moodle page with a recognizable "Due date" label (a theme/version difference this audit couldn't verify against a real instance) will still have `due_date = NULL` if it was also never in the Timeline — this fix meaningfully narrows, but does not structurally eliminate, every path to a null due date, since it depends on best-effort text extraction rather than a guaranteed Moodle API field.
- `get_upcoming_assignments()`'s cross-course `MAX_RESULTS = 8` cap is unchanged — still fine for "what's due soon" style queries (its intended purpose), but a reminder that any *new* cross-course tool added in the future should consider the same per-course-omission risk this entry's fix specifically worked around for the single-course case.

**Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, `backend/api/matching.py`, `backend/api/gemini_tools.py`, `backend/api/groq_tools.py`, `backend/tests/test_assignment_description.py` (extended), `backend/tests/test_course_query_matching.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_groq_agent.py` (one assertion updated).

## 2026-09-19 (Temporary safety limit: cap document downloads at 3 per sync)

- **Time**: 2026-09-19, continuing the same day as the entries above. Deliberately the smallest possible change — one file (`moodle/document_downloader.py`) does the actual work; everything else touched is test/doc/env-example updates needed to keep the existing suite honest about the new behavior.

### What changed

`moodle/document_downloader.py`'s `sync_resource_documents()` — the automated document-download stage called from `moodle/sync_service.py`'s `run_sync()` — now caps how many eligible resources are actually downloaded/processed in one sync run, via a new `ESMERELDA_MAX_DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADS_PER_SYNC` environment variable (`_get_max_document_downloads_per_sync()`), defaulting to **3**, following the exact same "re-read every call, safe fallback on an invalid value" pattern already established for `ESMERELDA_STALE_SYNC_MINUTES` in `storage/crud.py`.

- **Scope, exactly as instructed**: only this one function, only the document-*download* stage. `moodle/sync_service.py`'s course discovery, resource-*metadata* discovery/persistence, and assignment discovery (Timeline and course-page paths) were not touched at all — every real course/resource/assignment is still discovered and persisted, in full, on every sync; only the *number of resources actually downloaded* is bounded. Verified directly, not assumed: `tests/test_document_download_limit.py`'s check 2 confirms all 6 test resources are persisted as `Resource` rows even though only 3 are downloaded.
- **`0` explicitly means unlimited** (every eligible resource attempted — the pre-existing behavior), never "download nothing." An invalid or negative value falls back to the default of 3 with a logged warning, the same defensive pattern already used elsewhere in this project for its other configurable env vars.
- **Logging, exactly as instructed**: the resolved limit is logged at the start of every sync (`"document download limit=3"` or `"document download limit=unlimited"`); the resources actually selected for download are logged with their real resource ids; when the limit truncates the eligible list, a distinct log line names how many were skipped *specifically because the limit was reached* (`"document download limit reached: N eligible, M selected for this sync, K skipped (resource_ids=[...])"`) — a different, more specific message than an ordinary per-item failure or ineligibility skip, so a real download failure is never confused with an intentional, limit-driven skip in the logs.
- **All existing safeguards are completely unchanged**: streaming download (`_stream_resource_to_disk()`), the per-download timeout/total-deadline/max-file-size limits, and the extraction size/page-count bounds (`storage/text_extraction.py`) were not touched — every resource that IS selected for download still goes through the exact same, already-hardened path as before. No resource content is ever loaded into memory to decide the limit — the limit is applied by slicing the existing lightweight ORM-row list (`resources[:limit]`), the same list `sync_resource_documents()` already built up front (a handful of short strings per row, not file content — unchanged from before).
- **`DocumentSyncCounts.skipped`** — a pre-existing field that was always 0 in practice before this change (nothing set it) — is now used to report the count of resources skipped due to this limit, requiring no new field and no change to any code that reads `DocumentSyncCounts`.
- **No database schema change.**

### Testing

- `tests/test_document_download_limit.py` (**new**, 13 checks, against a real headless-Chromium-driven `run_sync()` over a real local fake Moodle server with 6 eligible resources — deliberately more than the default limit): the default limit downloads exactly 3 of 6 eligible resources while all 6 are still discovered/persisted as `Resource` rows; the limit value, the selected resources, and the limit-driven skip are all logged in the exact form described above; the limit is genuinely configurable (set to 2, confirmed exactly 2 downloaded); `0` is confirmed to mean unlimited (all 6 downloaded); an invalid env value is confirmed to fall back to the default of 3 without crashing the sync.
- `tests/test_sync_service.py`: one existing test (`8b`, the "15 real files, memory doesn't accumulate" test) needed its own environment updated to explicitly set `ESMERELDA_MAX_DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADS_PER_SYNC=0` — its real purpose (proving memory stays bounded across many sequentially-processed resources) is orthogonal to this new limit, and without the override the new default-3 behavior would have silently changed what that test was actually exercising (down to 3 files instead of 15) rather than the test's assertions being wrong. This is the only pre-existing test that needed any change; every other test file already uses 4 or fewer eligible resources per run and was completely unaffected.
- **Full suite result — every test file run, none skipped, none weakened**: `test_assignment_action_planner.py` 9/9, `test_assignment_course_mapping.py` 9/9, `test_assignment_description.py` 9/9, `test_course_page_assignment_discovery.py` 10/10, `test_course_query_matching.py` 15/15, `test_deployment_readiness.py` 9/9, `test_document_download_limit.py` 13/13 (new), `test_document_retrieval.py` 11/11, `test_document_skip_logic.py` 34/34, `test_followups.py` 9/9, `test_groq_agent.py` 10/10, `test_production_readiness.py` 12/12, `test_resource_dedup.py` 10/10, `test_sync_service.py` 56/56, `test_sync_staleness.py` 18/18, `test_text_extraction.py` 10/10 — **244/244 passed.**
- `.env.example` updated to document the new variable, its default, and its `0`-means-unlimited behavior, matching the existing documentation style for `ESMERELDA_STALE_SYNC_MINUTES`.

### Remaining notes

- This is explicitly a **temporary** limit (per the request and the code's own comments) — intended to bound a single sync's download volume/time while the rest of the pipeline is exercised, not a permanent architectural constraint. Removing it later is a one-line revert of the default (or setting the env var to `0` in the deployment environment) — no other code depends on the limited count.
- As with every other entry in this log, this was verified only against a local fake-Moodle-server test fixture — not a real Moodle account or a real Render deployment.

**Files changed this step**: `backend/moodle/document_downloader.py`, `backend/.env.example`, `backend/tests/test_document_download_limit.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_sync_service.py` (one test's environment updated).

## 2026-09-22 (Voice — Step 1: Esmerelda can speak, via a manual test button)

- **Time**: 2026-09-22. Deliberately small, per instruction: only text-to-speech generation and a manual test control were added. The orb/circular animation, audio visualization, Moodle sync, assignments, document downloading, and the existing chat-agent logic were **not touched** — `AiCore.tsx`, `moodle/`, `chat_agent.py`, and `routes.py`'s existing endpoints are unchanged except for one new route appended at the end of the file. Esmerelda does not speak her chat replies automatically yet — this step only wires up a one-off "Test Esmerelda Voice" button.

### Backend

- **New module: `backend/api/tts.py`** — `synthesize_speech(text, voice="af_sarah") -> bytes`, returning raw WAV audio, and a single `TtsUnavailableError` that collapses every possible failure (network error, Space asleep, API shape change, empty text) into one clean, backend-detail-free message. The provider is isolated behind two module-level constants (`_SPACE_ID`, `_API_NAME`) plus this one function's body — replacing Kokoro with a different TTS provider later touches only this file, not `routes.py` or the frontend, since both only ever deal in raw audio bytes.
- **New route: `POST /api/speech`** (`backend/api/routes.py`) — takes `{"text": "..."}` (validated 1–2000 chars via a new `SpeechRequestIn` schema, same pattern as the existing `ChatRequestIn`), returns the generated audio as `audio/wav`, or a 502 with `TtsUnavailableError`'s message on failure. No new database access, no change to any existing route.
- **Provider used: `Remsky/Kokoro-TTS-Zero`, a public Hugging Face Space, called via `gradio_client` (not webpage scraping).** The task named `hexgrad/Kokoro-TTS` specifically, but that Space's own `/config` reports `"show_api": false` (confirmed directly by fetching it, not assumed) — its Gradio API is disabled by its owner, so `gradio_client` has nothing to call (`view_api()` returns zero named/unnamed endpoints against it). `Remsky/Kokoro-TTS-Zero` runs the same underlying Kokoro model and exposes the same voice set (including `af_sarah`) through a real, callable endpoint, `/generate_speech_from_ui(text, voice_names, speed)`, confirmed by inspecting its `/config` dependencies and by an actual end-to-end call that returned a valid WAV file. No API key is required — the Space is public.
- **Voice**: `af_sarah` (a stable Kokoro English female voice, present in the Space's own `voice_names` choice list) is the module's default, passed as a single-item list per the endpoint's own signature. No pitch/filter effects are applied — the raw WAV bytes returned by the Space are passed straight through.
- **Configuration**: none required — no API key, no new environment variable. `gradio_client==2.7.1` added to `backend/requirements.txt`.

### Frontend

- **New API function: `synthesizeSpeech(text)` in `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`** — `POST`s to `/api/speech` and returns a `URL.createObjectURL` for the returned audio blob (caller owns revoking it), plus a `SpeechUnavailableError` mirroring the existing `ChatUnavailableError` pattern for a reachability failure.
- **New control: "Test Esmerelda Voice" on `frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx`** (a new "Voice (test)" section, in the same style as the existing Profile section) — on click, sends the fixed test sentence "Good evening, Vedant. How may I assist you?" to the backend, receives the generated audio, and plays it via an `HTMLAudioElement`. Shows "Generating…" while pending and a plain error message inline on failure. Nothing elsewhere in the app calls this automatically — `Chat.tsx` and every other page are unchanged.

### Testing

- Directly exercised `api.tts.synthesize_speech()` in a real Python REPL against the real local venv: returned 162,044 bytes of genuine RIFF/WAVE audio (16-bit PCM mono, 24kHz) for the exact test sentence.
- Started the real FastAPI app (`uvicorn main:app`) locally and `curl`'d `POST /api/speech` directly: `200 audio/wav`, 162,044 bytes, confirmed as a valid WAVE file — the full **frontend-shaped request → FastAPI → Kokoro Space → audio** path was exercised for real, end to end (browser playback itself was not separately screenshotted, but the same `Audio.play()` API is standard and the blob is a genuine playable WAV).
- Backend suite: `python -m pytest tests -q` — 12 passed, 5 pre-existing failures in `test_groq_agent.py`, all failing with `"async def functions are not natively supported"` (no `pytest-asyncio` plugin installed in this venv) — unrelated to this change, present before it, and not touched by it.
- Frontend: `npx tsc -b` — clean, no errors.

### Limitations of the public Hugging Face Space

- **Free-tier Space, not a dedicated API** — no uptime, latency, or rate-limit guarantee. A Space that has gone to sleep (Spaces on the free tier sleep after inactivity) adds a real cold-start delay to the first request, and could time out; `TtsUnavailableError` surfaces this as a generic "voice provider unavailable" rather than a crash, but doesn't retry or warm it up.
- **Depends on a third party's Space staying up and API-enabled** at all — `hexgrad/Kokoro-TTS` itself already changed (API disabled) between when the task was written and now, which is exactly the kind of drift this module's isolated, easily-swappable provider boundary is meant to absorb, not prevent.
- **No authentication** — this is what makes it free and key-less, but also means no SLA and no guarantee the Space owner won't disable its API next, same as happened to the originally-named Space.
- Output is a single, non-streaming WAV per request — fine for a short test sentence or a chat reply, but not suited to long-form narration without chunking (out of scope for this step).

**Files changed this step**: `backend/api/tts.py` (new), `backend/api/routes.py` (one new route), `backend/api/schemas.py` (one new schema), `backend/requirements.txt` (one new dependency), `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` (one new function + error class), `frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx` (one new section).

## 2026-09-22 (Voice — Step 2: Esmerelda's chat replies are spoken automatically)

- **Time**: 2026-09-22, continuing the same day as Step 1 above. Scope deliberately narrow per instruction: only wiring the existing `POST /api/speech` (from Step 1) into the chat flow so the final assistant reply is spoken automatically. **No backend files were touched at all this step** — `synthesizeSpeech()` and `POST /api/speech` are reused exactly as they were. Moodle sync, assignment logic, document downloading, the orb/circular animation (`AiCore.tsx`, untouched), and the AI response generation logic (`chat_agent.py`, `groq_agent.py`, etc., untouched) were not modified.

### What changed

- **New hook: `frontend/src/lib/useEsmereldaSpeech.ts`** — `speak(text)`, `stop()`, and an `isSpeaking` boolean, built entirely on top of the existing `synthesizeSpeech()` from `lib/api.ts` (no second TTS implementation). `speak()`:
  - strips markdown via a new `stripMarkdownForSpeech()` (`lib/utils.ts`) before sending text to the backend — removes fenced/inline code, `**bold**`/`*italics*`, `#` headings, `- `/`* ` list markers, and `[label](url)` links (kept as just the label), collapsing blank-line paragraph breaks into a period-and-space so multi-paragraph replies don't run on;
  - always stops whatever is currently playing (or still being fetched) **first**, using a monotonically-incrementing request id to invalidate any in-flight `synthesizeSpeech()` call that a newer `speak()` call has superseded — so a slow first request can never start playing audio after a second, faster one already has (verified directly — see Testing);
  - swallows every possible failure (`synthesizeSpeech()` throwing, or `HTMLAudioElement.play()` rejecting) without re-throwing, so a voice-provider outage can never surface as a chat-breaking error;
  - revokes each blob URL it's done with (`URL.revokeObjectURL`), and stops/cleans up on unmount.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Chat.tsx`**: after a successful `sendChatMessage()` call appends the assistant's reply bubble to `messages`, `speak(res.reply)` is called on that same reply text — nothing else. It is never called for: the user's own message, the "Thinking…" pending state, the `catch` branch's `unavailable` error bubble, or any of `res.assignments`/`res.sources`/`res.followUps` (all structured data, never sent to TTS). `isSpeaking` is returned by the hook but intentionally unused by any UI yet — no visual indicator or animation was added, per instruction.
- **`frontend/src/lib/utils.ts`**: added `stripMarkdownForSpeech()`, a small regex-based cleaner (not a full markdown parser) scoped to exactly the markdown syntax `ChatMarkdown.tsx` actually renders.

### Testing

- `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` (Vite production build) — both clean.
- **Real browser, real backend, real Kokoro Space** (Playwright-driven Chromium against `vite` dev server + `uvicorn`, not a mock): sent a real chat message through the real Groq-backed `/api/chat`; the reply rendered, and immediately afterward the browser made a real `POST /api/speech` request on its own, with no manual trigger — confirmed from the backend's own request log. On this run the Kokoro Space itself returned a real "ZeroGPU runs limit exceeded" error (the public Space's own shared-quota limit, hit from repeated testing) — the chat reply stayed on screen and fully usable throughout, since the failure happened after the chat response had already succeeded and rendered. This was a genuine, unplanned instance of requirement 5 ("if TTS fails, chat must still work"), not a simulated one.
- **Real browser, mocked `/api/chat` and `/api/speech`** (Playwright, to get deterministic timing/failure control that a live third-party Space and a live LLM API can't reliably offer test-to-test):
  - Instrumented `HTMLMediaElement.prototype.play`/`.pause` at the page level to log every real call the app makes. Sent one message, then — while its (deliberately slowed) `/api/speech` response was still in flight — sent a second message whose `/api/speech` response resolved first. Captured log, in order: `play` (reply 1's audio) → `pause` (reply 1's audio, stopped by the second `speak()` call) → `play` (reply 2's audio). This directly confirms requirement 4 ("if a new response starts while audio is playing, stop the previous audio") and that the interrupted first request never played after being superseded, using the app's real runtime calls, not an assumption about the code.
  - Forced `/api/speech` to return `502` for every request: the first chat reply rendered normally, a second message was then sent and its reply also rendered normally — chat remained fully functional across two full turns despite 100% TTS failure, with only a same-origin "Failed to load resource: 502" console entry (an expected network log, not a thrown/uncaught error — no `pageerror` events were recorded).
- No frontend automated test runner exists in this project yet (`package.json` has no test script) — this step didn't add one; the browser-driven checks above were run manually via a scratch Playwright script (not committed) rather than as a permanent test file, consistent with the frontend project's existing test posture.
- Backend: unaffected — no backend files changed this step. Step 1's `pytest` result stands (12 passed; 5 pre-existing failures in `test_groq_agent.py`, unrelated, caused by a missing `pytest-asyncio` plugin in the local venv, present before this project's changes).

### Remaining limitations

- Still depends on the same free-tier `Remsky/Kokoro-TTS-Zero` Space as Step 1, including its shared `ZeroGPU` quota — observed directly during this step's own testing to be a real, hit-able limit under repeated use, not just a theoretical one. `speak()` handles this the same way it handles any other TTS failure: silently, without affecting the chat reply already on screen.
- `isSpeaking` exists on the hook but drives no UI yet (no speaking indicator, no stop button) — intentionally deferred, per instruction, to a future step alongside the orb/audio-visualization work.
- `stripMarkdownForSpeech()` is scoped to the markdown syntax this project's own `ChatMarkdown.tsx` renders, not general-purpose markdown — sufficient for this project's replies, not a drop-in for arbitrary markdown text.

**Files changed this step**: `frontend/src/lib/useEsmereldaSpeech.ts` (new), `frontend/src/lib/utils.ts` (one new function), `frontend/src/pages/Chat.tsx` (wired `speak()` into the existing success path). No backend files changed.

## 2026-09-22 (Voice — Step 3: the orb reacts to Esmerelda's actual speech audio)

- **Time**: 2026-09-22, continuing the same day as Steps 1–2 above. Scope deliberately narrow per instruction: only connects the existing orb (`AiCore.tsx`) to the real audio already being played by Step 2's auto-speak. **No backend files were touched at all this step.** Moodle, assignments, document syncing, the TTS backend/Kokoro integration, and AI response generation are all untouched. The orb's existing SVG structure, gradients, and rotation/breathing keyframe animations are unchanged — this step only adds a real-time overlay on top of them.

### What changed

- **`frontend/src/lib/useEsmereldaSpeech.ts`** now also exposes `energyRef` — a plain mutable ref (not React state, so reading it never causes a re-render) holding a smoothed 0..1 loudness reading, continuously updated via `requestAnimationFrame` from a Web Audio `AnalyserNode` tapped onto the *same* `<audio>` element Step 2 already plays (`createMediaElementSource(audioRef.current)` → `AnalyserNode` → `audioContext.destination`, so the listener hears the exact same audio as before, just with a copy of it also going to the analyser). No second audio-playback system exists.
  - Reads `getByteTimeDomainData()` each frame, computes an RMS amplitude, and applies **asymmetric exponential smoothing** — fast "attack" (0.45) when the reading rises, slower "release" (0.12) when it falls — which is what produces a punchy reaction to a loud syllable but a graceful settle through a quiet pause, rather than raw per-sample jitter. This is not a fixed-interval pulse: the loop reads real audio data every frame and stops advancing entirely once both the audio has stopped and the smoothed value has decayed under a small threshold (so nothing runs forever after speech ends).
  - Web Audio setup (`ensureWebAudioGraph`) is wrapped in `try/catch` and attempted **exactly once**, lazily, the first time `speak()` creates the shared `<audio>` element (`createMediaElementSource` can only ever be called once per element). Any failure — no `AudioContext` support, the browser refusing it, anything — is caught, logged with `console.warn`, and leaves `energyRef` at a permanent 0; it never touches `audio.src`/`audio.play()`, so voice playback itself is completely unaffected by a Web Audio failure. If the analyser-specific wiring fails after the element's output was already captured by the graph, the code falls back to connecting straight to the speakers so audio isn't silently lost.
  - `stop()` (called at the start of every `speak()`, and from `Chat.tsx`'s "New conversation") now also cancels the energy `requestAnimationFrame` loop and zeroes `energyRef` immediately, so an interrupted response's orb reaction cuts cleanly rather than trailing into the next one.
- **`frontend/src/components/core/AiCore.tsx`**: added an optional `energyRef` prop. When provided (only `Chat.tsx`'s header orb passes it — the empty-state orb and `Dashboard.tsx`'s orb don't, and behave exactly as before), and only while `state === "active"` and the user doesn't prefer reduced motion, a `requestAnimationFrame` loop reads `energyRef.current` each frame and writes directly to three existing elements via refs (not React state, to avoid re-rendering 60 times a second): the breathing core circle's `transform: scale(...)`, the ambient glow div's `transform: scale(...)` + `opacity`, and the middle ring's `stroke-width`. All three also get a short CSS `transition` (120–150ms) so the frame-to-frame motion reads as fluid rather than stepped. The loop — and its transforms — are fully torn down (inline styles cleared back to `""`, so the pre-existing CSS classes/keyframes silently resume) the instant `state` leaves `"active"`, satisfying "no animation continues indefinitely after speech ends" directly, not just by convention.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Chat.tsx`**: the header `AiCore` now receives `state={pending ? "processing" : isSpeaking ? "active" : "idle"}` and `energyRef={energyRef}` (both from `useEsmereldaSpeech()`, reused as instructed — no new hook duplicating this). The status line now also shows "Speaking…" (with the same pulsing dot already used for "Thinking…") while `isSpeaking` is true. "New conversation" now also calls the hook's `stop()`, so switching conversations can't leave an orphaned audio clip (and its orb reaction) running.

### Visual approach (why it doesn't look like an equalizer)

Only three continuous, already-organic-shaped elements are modulated — a soft radial glow, a soft gradient core, one thin ring's stroke width — each nudged by a smooth, asymmetrically-smoothed 0..1 value, never a bar chart, never per-frequency-bin bars, and never anything that resets on a fixed timer. The existing slow ring rotation and 4.5s breathing-brightness keyframe animation keep running underneath, unchanged — the energy reaction is a layered addition, not a replacement, so at low/zero energy the orb looks exactly as it did before this step.

### Testing

- `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` — both clean. (One TypeScript fix needed along the way: `AnalyserNode.getByteTimeDomainData()`'s type signature required a `Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>` specifically, not the wider `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>` that `new Uint8Array(n)` infers under this project's TypeScript version — fixed by constructing it from an explicit `ArrayBuffer` and typing the ref accordingly.)
- **Real browser, Playwright-driven Chromium, against the real running app** (`uvicorn` + `vite` dev server), in four separate scripted passes:
  1. A real chat message through the real Groq-backed `/api/chat` and the real Kokoro Space: the reply rendered and `/api/speech` fired automatically (same as Step 2). The Kokoro Space itself returned its own real "ZeroGPU runs limit exceeded" error during this pass (the same public, shared-quota limit noted in Step 2, hit again from repeated testing today) — confirming, live, that a real TTS failure still leaves the orb in its normal idle "Online" state rather than stuck reacting to nothing.
  2. To test the orb's actual audio-reactivity deterministically (without depending on the third-party Space's quota, already proven working in Steps 1–2), `/api/speech` was mocked to return a **real WAV file previously generated by the real Kokoro Space** in Step 1's own testing — so the browser's Web Audio graph is analyzing genuine synthesized speech samples, not silence or a synthetic tone. Sampling the DOM every 150ms while "Speaking…" was shown: the core circle's `scale` fluctuated continuously between ~1.004 and ~1.125, and the ring's `stroke-width` between ~0.61 and ~0.95, tracking the real audio's loudness envelope — not a fixed cadence. The instant the clip ended (audio's own `ended` event), the status flipped to "Online" and every sampled transform/stroke-width became empty (`""`) on the very next sample — confirming the reaction stops immediately and completely, not just fades toward idle.
  3. Sent a second message while the first (mocked, real-audio) response was still playing: status stayed "Speaking…" continuously across the interruption (now driven by the second clip), and after it finished, status returned to "Online" with zero leftover inline transforms — confirming the old audio's orb reaction is fully replaced, not merely paused underneath.
  4. Forced `/api/speech` to return `502` for every request: the chat reply rendered normally, the header status never left "Online" (never got stuck showing "Speaking…"), and no orb transform was ever set — confirming a TTS failure can't strand the orb in a reacting state, mirroring Step 2's "chat still works" result.
- No frontend automated test runner exists in this project (unchanged from Step 2's note) — these were scratch Playwright scripts run manually, not committed as test files.
- Backend: unaffected — no backend files changed this step. Step 1's `pytest` result stands (12 passed; 5 pre-existing, unrelated `test_groq_agent.py` failures from a missing `pytest-asyncio` plugin in the local venv).

### Remaining limitations

- Still depends on the same free-tier `Remsky/Kokoro-TTS-Zero` Space (Steps 1–2) and its shared `ZeroGPU` quota, which was genuinely hit again during this step's own testing — orb behavior on a real TTS failure was verified live, not just simulated.
- The Web Audio setup path (`createMediaElementSource` + `AnalyserNode`) was exercised successfully in this session's real Chromium browser; it was not separately verified against Safari/WebKit's historically stricter autoplay/Web-Audio-resume behavior, or against a browser with Web Audio fully disabled — the latter is exactly the case the `try/catch` fallback exists for, and is covered by reasoning about the code path (audio playback never depends on the Web Audio branch succeeding), not a live test in that specific browser.
- `energyRef` is only wired into `Chat.tsx`'s header orb — the per-message avatar orbs in the chat transcript and the large idle-state orb remain static, unchanged from before this step, since only one response is ever audible at a time and the header orb already represents "Esmerelda" as a whole.

**Files changed this step**: `frontend/src/lib/useEsmereldaSpeech.ts` (added `energyRef`/Web Audio analysis), `frontend/src/components/core/AiCore.tsx` (added the optional `energyRef` prop and its rAF-driven visual reaction), `frontend/src/pages/Chat.tsx` (wired `isSpeaking`/`energyRef` into the header orb and status text; "New conversation" now also stops speech). No backend files changed.
