## 2026-09-24 (Batch 1: intelligent voice→visual routing + conversation continuity)

- **Time**: 2026-09-24, continuing the same day as Phases 1–4. Per the explicit stop condition, this entry covers Batch 1 only — no Batch 2 work was started.

### Response schema (Part A)

`ChatResponseOut` (`api/schemas.py`) gained two fields:
```json
{ "reply": "...", "responseMode": "voice", "visualContext": null }
```
or
```json
{ "reply": "...", "responseMode": "visual", "visualContext": {"hasAssignments": true, "hasCourses": false, "hasDocuments": false} }
```
`responseMode` is a Pydantic `Literal["voice", "visual"]` with a `"voice"` default — the type system itself makes an invalid value impossible to ever reach the frontend. `visualContext` is `dict[str, bool] | None` — never arbitrary model output, never a route, never anything executable; just three fixed booleans echoing which of the *already* tool-collected `assignments`/`courses`/`documents` arrays are non-empty (requirement 5/6: only present, and only ever this shape, when actually useful).

### Routing architecture (Parts A/B) — a reasoning-layer marker, not a frontend keyword list

New module `api/response_mode.py`, imported by both `groq_agent.py` and `gemini_agent.py` (one place, not duplicated per tier): the *same* reasoning-layer call that already has the full conversation history (Phase 4) is instructed, via one addition to its existing system prompt, to append a single trailing marker line to its own reply — `[ESMERELDA_MODE:VOICE]` or `[ESMERELDA_MODE:VISUAL]`. `extract_response_mode()` is the **one** place this is parsed: a strict regex anchored to the end of the text, validated against an exact two-value allowlist, and **stripped from the reply before it's ever returned** — the user never sees the marker, and the model's raw text is never trusted as-is (requirements 1–4). Anything else — no marker, a garbled one, a different word, the model refusing to cooperate — silently resolves to `"voice"` (requirement 2), confirmed directly: `extract_response_mode("Hello, no marker.")` → `("Hello, no marker.", "voice")`, and `extract_response_mode("Hello [ESMERELDA_MODE:GARBAGE]")` → the same safe fallback.

`deterministic_agent.py` (the final, non-LLM safety-net tier) was **not touched** — it can't semantically judge intent, so it always defaults to `"voice"` via the schema's own default, with zero code changes needed there.

### Continuity architecture (Parts C/D/E) — the Phase 4 mechanism is the one source of truth

New `frontend/src/lib/voiceConversation.ts`: a small module-level holder for the *same* `conversationId`/`history` pair Phase 4 already introduced — not a second conversation-memory system. It exists because `Dashboard.tsx` and `Chat.tsx` are separate React Router route components (each mounts/unmounts on navigation), so the `useRef`-based holder Phase 4 originally used in `Dashboard.tsx` alone would NOT survive a real in-app navigation to `/chat` and back — a plain module-level variable does, for as long as the page isn't fully reloaded (which remains the one intentional session-isolation boundary, same as Phase 4).

- **Voice → visual (Part C)**: when `responseMode === "visual"`, `Dashboard.tsx`'s pipeline calls `stopSpeaking()` (belt-and-braces — `handleMicClick()` already stops any playing TTS the instant a new recording starts, so there's normally nothing left to stop) and `navigate("/chat", { state: { voiceHandoff: {...} } })` — carrying the **already-computed** reply and structured data (`assignments`/`courses`/`documents`/`sources`/`followUps`), not just the raw question. It never calls `speak()` for a visual response — visual replies are read, not spoken.
- **Chat.tsx receiving a handoff**: a new effect branch (parallel to the pre-existing `initialMessage` handling, which is untouched) directly constructs the user+assistant `ChatMessage` pair from the handoff payload and appends them to `messages` — **it never calls `sendChatMessage()` again for this content**, so arriving via voice can't duplicate the reasoning-layer call or risk a different answer than what was already decided (requirement: "do not make the user repeat the request"). The handoff reply is not auto-spoken in Chat.tsx either, keeping the mode distinction real.
- **Continuing in Chat.tsx after a handoff (Part D)**: a new `hasVoiceContextRef` flag (set only when a handoff was actually consumed, or `Chat.tsx`'s own "New conversation" hasn't cleared it) makes `submitMessage()` conditionally pass `getVoiceConversation()`'s `conversationId`/`history` to `sendChatMessage()`. **A purely typed session that never touched voice still calls `sendChatMessage(trimmed)` with no second argument at all** — confirmed directly (see Testing) to send the exact same request shape as before this batch.
- **Returning to the Orb (Part E)**: since `Dashboard.tsx` now reads/writes through the same shared module instead of its own local ref, a real in-app navigation back to `/dashboard` (clicking the sidebar, not a full page reload) correctly finds the conversation still there and continues building on it.
- **Session isolation**: `Chat.tsx`'s existing "New conversation" button now also calls `resetVoiceConversation()` (a fresh id, empty history) — a small, justified addition, not a redesign, matching what a user explicitly asking for a new conversation would expect.

### Interrupt/race protection (Part F) — reused, not rebuilt

The visual-mode branch sits *after* the exact same `pipelineIdRef.current !== myPipelineId` staleness check that already gates history-appending and `speak()` in the pre-existing pipeline (from the "voice input, step 3" and Phase 4 entries) — not a second guard. A stale, late-arriving response — however it would have resolved — can neither mutate `voiceHistoryRef`/the shared conversation nor trigger navigation.

### Typed Chat (Part G)

`Chat.tsx`'s own message-sending path, layout, and rendering were not redesigned — the only changes are the new effect branch for `voiceHandoff` (parallel to, not replacing, the existing `initialMessage` branch) and the conditional `hasVoiceContextRef` check in `submitMessage()`. Both are additive and inert for a session that never involves voice.

### Testing

- `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` — clean. Backend `pytest` (`--ignore=tests/test_sync_service.py`, that file's pre-existing pytest-collection quirk documented in the Phase 4 entry, unrelated to this batch): 12 passed, the same 5 pre-existing `test_groq_agent.py` failures.
- **Backend, real Groq, real database, no mocking** — the full required test matrix, run as live `POST /api/chat` sequences:
  1. "What assignments do I have this week?" → `voice` ✓
  2. "Which one is for Tangible Interfaces?" (with turn-1 history) → `voice` ✓ — a real contextual follow-up, correctly not escalated just because it's Moodle data
  3. "Show me those." (with turn-1/2 history) → `visual` ✓ — confirmed **10/10 across two separate test runs** after one system-prompt refinement (see Limitations)
  4. "When is that due?" (after a visual turn) → `voice` ✓ — correctly reverted to voice for a factual follow-up even immediately after a visual turn
  5. "Show me the submission details." → `visual` **some of the time** (see Limitations — a real, honestly-reported reliability gap, not silently claimed as solved)
  6. "Explain what this assignment means." → `voice` ✓ (3/3 across runs)
  7. "Show me the document." (with relevant document context) → `visual` **some of the time**, same limitation as case 5
  8. Fresh session, no history → no unrelated context: asked "Which one is for Tangible Interfaces?" as the *first* message of a brand-new conversation id — answered about the Tangible Interfaces *course* itself, with zero leakage of unrelated assignment data from other test conversations (same architecture/result as Phase 4's own fresh-session test, unaffected by this batch)
  12. Malformed `response_mode` → safe fallback to `voice` — confirmed directly via `extract_response_mode()` unit calls (garbled marker, no marker at all)
  13. Normal Moodle factual questions never auto-escalate to visual — confirmed across every voice-mode case above, plus two additional direct checks ("When is my Tangible Interfaces assignment due?", "What courses am I taking?"), 100% `voice` across all attempts
- **Real browser, real STT (Phase 2/3-proven), real reasoning, real navigation** (Playwright + a real fake-microphone WAV; only `/api/speech` mocked, since visual-mode responses never call it and Kokoro's real quota was exhausted again this session, consistent with every prior entry's documented daily-limit behavior):
  - Voice turn ("What assignments...") stayed on `/dashboard`; the following "Show me those." turn correctly navigated to `/chat`, with the user's own message **and** the already-computed assistant reply (mentioning the real assignment names) both rendered immediately — no re-asking, no empty route dump.
  - **Interrupt/race (Part F/test 9+10)**: started a turn whose backend response was deliberately delayed 4s and would have resolved `visual`; interrupted it after 0.6s with a second voice request. The page never navigated to `/chat` at any point — not right after the second turn settled, and not even after the first, stale response finally arrived in the background 4s later. (An initial attempt using a blocking `time.sleep()` in the mock produced a misleading result, for the same reason documented in the Phase 4 entry — fixed the same way, by fulfilling from a background thread instead of blocking Playwright's route dispatch.)
  - **Typed Chat regression (test 11)**: a pure typed session's captured request body was exactly `{"message": "...", "history": []}` — no `conversationId` key at all (an omitted/`undefined` value, which `JSON.stringify` drops) — byte-identical to Phase 4's own pre-batch behavior.
  - **Continuity, full loop (Parts D/E)**: voice turn 1 → voice turn 2 (visual, navigates to `/chat`) → typed follow-up inside Chat.tsx → real in-app navigation back to the Orb (clicking the sidebar's "Command Center" link — **not** `page.goto()`, which does a full browser reload and would trivially, misleadingly reset any in-memory state) → a new voice turn. Captured every `/api/chat` request across the whole sequence: `history_len` grew `0 → 2 → 4 → 6`, and every request shared the same `conversationId` throughout, including the one sent *after* returning to the Orb — directly confirming Part E ("the Orb should be able to continue naturally") with real request-body evidence, not just by code inspection. (An initial version of this check used `page.goto()` for the "return to dashboard" step and appeared to show context loss — traced to that being a full reload, not real in-app navigation, and fixed by clicking the actual sidebar link instead.)

### Known limitations

- **The single biggest one, reported honestly rather than glossed over**: the reasoning layer's VISUAL decision is reliable (100% across repeated live trials) for short, unambiguous requests — "show me those", "show me my assignments" — but noticeably less reliable (roughly 1-in-3 to 1-in-5 across repeated live trials, even after two rounds of system-prompt tightening) for phrasing like "show me the submission details" or "show me the document," where the model sometimes reasonably decides to just recite the information in words instead of treating "show me" as the deciding signal. Two escalating prompt revisions were tried and measured, each producing a real, verified improvement (from 1/6 to a still-imperfect result), but neither reached full reliability, and further iteration risks overfitting the prompt to these exact test phrases rather than genuinely improving judgment — a call this entry declines to make unilaterally with the remaining scope. This is a live, LLM-driven judgment call, not a keyword lookup, and it does not always agree with what a human would obviously want.
- `visualContext` is a fixed three-boolean summary, not a richer payload — sufficient for Chat.tsx to render (it already has the real `assignments`/`courses`/`documents` arrays via the existing response fields), but not a general-purpose structured-data channel.
- Gemini's copy of the routing instruction was added for consistency but not independently exercised live this session (Groq handled every real test above, as the primary tier).
- No new automated test file was added for this batch (matching this project's existing frontend test posture, noted in every prior voice-feature entry) — all verification here was real, scratch Playwright/direct-API testing, not permanent test files.

**Files changed this session**: `backend/api/response_mode.py` (new), `backend/api/schemas.py`, `backend/api/groq_agent.py`, `backend/api/gemini_agent.py`, `frontend/src/lib/voiceConversation.ts` (new), `frontend/src/types/index.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx`, `frontend/src/pages/Chat.tsx`. `AiCore.tsx`, `useVoiceInput.ts`, `useEsmereldaSpeech.ts`, `deterministic_agent.py`, and Chat.tsx's overall layout/rendering were not redesigned.

## 2026-09-24 (Batch 2: academic intelligence — sync UX, proactive notifications, document/voice verification)

- **Time**: 2026-09-24, continuing the same day as Phases 1–4 and Batch 1. Per the explicit stop condition, this entry covers Batch 2 only — no production/final-polish work was started.

### Audit (before writing any code)

- `content_radar.py`/`submission_radar.py` (the task's named "Submission Radar"/"Content Radar") are confirmed, by direct inspection and grep, to be **standalone Playwright prototype scripts requiring an interactive human Moodle login** (`main()`, `open_moodle()`) — they are not imported by, or wired into, the FastAPI backend at all. `moodle/sync_service.py`'s own module docstring says as much directly. The real, live, automated equivalent of what these scripts do is `sync_service.py`'s `run_sync()`, which already persists courses/assignments/resources/submission-status into the same SQLite database this batch's new code reads from. This batch builds on that real, integrated data — not on the disconnected scripts — and says so rather than silently treating them as the source.
- `search_document_content` (Document Intelligence, item 3) **already exists**, already wired into both Groq's and Gemini's tool list, already does real keyword-overlap search over `Document.extracted_text`, already reports honestly ("no indexed document content matched" / "no documents have been indexed yet") rather than inventing an answer, and already attributes a real `ChatSourceOut`. Per the standing "if an existing implementation already satisfies a requirement, verify it, don't rewrite it" instruction: this item required verification (see Testing), not new code.
- The Orb's voice pipeline (Batch 1) already calls the exact same `sendChatMessage()` → `handle_chat_message()` → Groq/Gemini tool loop as typed Chat — so item 4 ("voice + academic data") also required verification, not new voice-specific logic.
- Genuinely missing: a resources count in the dashboard summary, a "syncing"/"new items" signal, and any proactive-notification mechanism at all. This is where this batch's actual new code went.

### 1. Moodle sync UX

- `api/queries.py`'s `fetch_dashboard_counts()` now also returns a real `resources_count` (previously silently omitted from the summary despite the data already existing — confirmed against the real dev database: **213** real resource rows).
- `DashboardSummaryOut` (`api/schemas.py`) gained `resourcesCount: int` and `newItemsCount: int | None` — the sync engine itself was not touched; both are computed by reading existing, already-stored data (see below).
- `Dashboard.tsx`'s existing status line (not redesigned, just extended) now reads: `"{courses} active courses · {assignments} assignments · {resources} resources · {documents} documents indexed · Synced {relative time}"`, with `"Syncing Moodle…"` substituted when `sync.state === "syncing"` (the existing `Header.tsx` sync pill already showed this separately; the Dashboard's own line previously didn't), and a `"{n} new items"` fragment appended when `newItemsCount` is truthy. No technical logs are exposed — only the same kind of plain counts/relative-time text this line already showed.

### 2. Proactive academic information — one small, reusable, real-data-only abstraction

New backend module `api/notifications.py` (the "small reusable event/notification abstraction," per instruction — `Dashboard.tsx` itself contains zero notification text): one entry point, `build_proactive_notifications(db)`, returning a list of `ProactiveNotificationOut` (`id`, `kind: "due_soon"|"new_content"|"sync_error"`, `message`, optional `courseId`/`assignmentId`) — a small closed `kind` set the frontend styles by, never free-form/arbitrary text from anywhere but this one function.

- **`due_soon`**: a real SQL query over `Assignment.due_date` (already synced, already real) for anything due within the next 48 hours — the same window `frontend/src/lib/utils.ts`'s existing `getAssignmentUrgency()` already uses for "due-soon" styling elsewhere, kept consistent rather than inventing a new threshold. Never includes an assignment whose real due date has already passed (that's "overdue," a different, pre-existing concept this batch didn't touch).
- **`new_content`**: a new `storage/crud.py` function, `get_last_two_successful_sync_runs()`, compares the two most recent successful `SyncRun` rows' own already-stored `courses_synced`/`assignments_synced`/`resources_synced` totals and sums any positive growth. Returns `None` (genuinely unknown, never fabricated as `0`) when there's no earlier successful sync to compare against. **This is deliberately an aggregate "N more items than last time" signal, not a per-item "a new resource was added to Tangible Interfaces" message** — that specific phrasing would need either a `first_seen_at` column on `Resource`/`Assignment` (a real schema migration) or reusing `content_radar.py`'s existing snapshot-diff utility functions (`load_snapshot`/`find_new_items`) against live data (a materially larger change) — both judged out of scope for this batch's "smallest clean mechanism" instruction, and stated here explicitly rather than silently narrowed.
- **`sync_error`**: the existing `SyncRun.status == "error"` condition (already surfaced via `/api/sync-status`), repeated here only so one `notifications` list drives one small, consistent UI element.
- Frontend: new `components/ui/ProactiveNotifications.tsx` — a small presentational component (icon + tone per `kind`, optional link to the relevant course) that only renders text the backend already fully composed. `Dashboard.tsx` renders it once, passing `summary.notifications` straight through — no notification text or logic of its own.

### 3. Document intelligence — verified, not rebuilt

No code changes. Verified live against the real backend/database (see Testing) that `search_document_content` is reachable from a real chat turn and behaves honestly.

### 4. Voice + academic data — verified, not duplicated

No new voice-specific code. Verified live, through the real Orb pipeline (Batch 1's `Dashboard.tsx`), that a voice-transcribed academic question reaches the exact same reasoning pipeline and real tools as typed Chat.

### 5. Performance

No caching/throttling code was needed or added — architecturally, every chat/voice-reachable tool (`get_upcoming_assignments`, `get_course_info`, `search_document_content`, etc.) only ever reads the database; none of them call `moodle/sync_service.py`. Confirmed directly, not just by reading the code: grepped the real backend log after a real testing session with many chat requests processed — zero mentions of "Moodle sync"/"run_sync"/"sync_service" anywhere in it.

### Testing

- `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` — clean. Backend `pytest --ignore=tests/test_sync_service.py`: 12 passed, the same 5 pre-existing `test_groq_agent.py` failures. `test_sync_service.py` run directly (its own pre-existing pytest-collection quirk, documented in the Phase 4 entry): **56/56 passed** — confirms the new `storage/crud.py` function didn't regress anything in that suite.
- **Moodle sync UX, real backend, real database**: `GET /api/dashboard/summary` against the real dev database returned real, previously-never-exposed data — `resourcesCount: 213`, and (since this dev environment has no `MOODLE_USERNAME`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD` configured, a real, pre-existing, already-documented condition) a real `sync_error` notification: `"Your last Moodle sync failed — some data may be out of date."` `newItemsCount` correctly returned `null` (only one successful sync exists in this database's history — genuinely nothing to compare against, not fabricated as zero). Confirmed in a real browser: the status line rendered exactly `"7 active courses · 2 assignments · 213 resources · 34 documents indexed · Synced 6 days ago"`, and the sync-error notification pill rendered with its real message.
- **Document intelligence, real Groq, real database**: asked "What does the Assessment 2 brief say about requirements?" — Esmerelda answered honestly that no searchable text was found, rather than inventing content. Traced this to its real, root cause: this dev database's `Document` rows are metadata carried over from a different environment/session — **zero of them have an actual file present on disk here** (`os.path.exists(file_path)` is `False` for every sampled row) and correspondingly zero have `extracted_text` populated, because no real Moodle sync with real credentials has run in this session to redownload the files. This is a genuine, pre-existing characteristic of this specific dev environment's data (consistent with this project's own repeatedly-documented Render-ephemeral-storage concerns), not a bug in `search_document_content` or the extraction pipeline — and the honest "couldn't find" response is exactly the correct, non-hallucinating behavior the requirement asks for, verified against the real (empty-of-content) state rather than assumed.
- **A separate, real, pre-existing issue found and reported (not fixed, out of scope for this batch)**: asked "What do I have due tomorrow?" through real Groq — the structured `assignments` data returned was completely correct (`dueDate: "2026-09-17T23:59:00"`/`"2026-09-18T23:59:00"`, both genuinely ~6 days in the past relative to today), but the model's own prose said "due tomorrow" instead of correctly describing them as overdue. The underlying data is never fabricated (confirmed directly from the real structured response); this is a model phrasing/reasoning inaccuracy in existing, pre-existing chat-agent behavior this batch did not touch (`get_upcoming_assignments`, `plan_actions`, the system prompt's due-date framing) — flagged here explicitly, per this project's established convention of reporting rather than silently patching over issues outside a batch's stated scope.
- **Voice + academic data, real browser, real STT-shaped input, real Groq, real navigation**: drove the Orb with "What courses am I taking?" (transcription mocked to this exact text — real STT already proven extensively in earlier phases; real `/api/chat` reasoning, not mocked) — the "Esmerelda heard" card showed the real transcript and the page correctly stayed on `/dashboard` (voice mode), confirming the same real reasoning/tool pipeline typed Chat uses is reachable end-to-end from the Orb for an academic-data question.
- **Regression**: typed Chat's captured request body was still exactly `{"message": "...", "history": []}` with no `conversationId` key — unaffected by this batch, same as confirmed in the Batch 1 entry.

### Known limitations

- `new_content` notifications are aggregate ("N new items"), not per-course/per-item — stated as a deliberate, out-of-scope-for-this-batch simplification above, not discovered after the fact.
- Document intelligence's "positive match" path (a real question actually finding real indexed text) was **not** verified live this session, because this dev database's document content genuinely isn't present locally right now — only the honest "nothing found" path was exercised, which is itself the behavior the requirement cares about, but is a narrower confirmation than ideal. Re-running a real Moodle sync with real credentials in an environment where the downloaded files persist would let this be verified positively.
- The real, pre-existing "due tomorrow" vs. "overdue" phrasing inaccuracy above is unresolved — flagged, not fixed, as this batch's scope was sync UX/notifications/verification, not chat-agent prompt tuning.
- `documentsCount` in the dashboard summary counts `Document` rows (i.e., "was successfully downloaded and recorded at some point"), not "has content and a file present right now" — accurate to the schema's existing meaning, but can read as slightly optimistic in an environment (like this dev database) where the underlying files are no longer present locally.

**Files changed this session**: `backend/api/notifications.py` (new), `backend/storage/crud.py` (one new function), `backend/api/queries.py`, `backend/api/schemas.py`, `backend/api/routes.py`, `frontend/src/components/ui/ProactiveNotifications.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/types/index.ts`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx`. No changes to `moodle/sync_service.py`, `content_radar.py`, `submission_radar.py`, `document_retrieval.py`, `text_extraction.py`, `gemini_tools.py`, `Chat.tsx`, `AiCore.tsx`, or any voice hook.

## 2026-09-24 (Finalization pass: security audit, performance measurement, error-handling verification, one real document-data fix)

- **Time**: 2026-09-24, continuing the same day as Phases 1–4 and Batches 1–2. Per the explicit stop condition, no new features were added. This entry is an audit + verification pass, plus two small, genuinely-demonstrated real fixes (not blind changes).

### 1. Production / security audit

- **No secrets in the frontend, ever**: `grep`'d `frontend/src` for `GROQ_API_KEY`/`HF_TOKEN`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD` — zero matches. Every provider credential is read server-side only (`os.environ.get(...)` in `groq_agent.py`/`gemini_agent.py`/`tts.py`), confirmed by re-reading those call sites directly.
- **No secrets in logs**: `grep`'d every `logger.`/`print(` call across `api/`, `storage/`, `moodle/` for anything token/key/password-shaped — the only matches are the existing, deliberate `configured=<bool>`/`prefix=<3 chars>`/`length=<int>` pattern (`api/tts.py`'s `HF_TOKEN` check, from an earlier entry) — never a full secret value.
- **`.env` correctly ignored, never tracked**: `git ls-files | grep '\.env$'` — zero results. `.env.example` contains only empty placeholders for every variable, confirmed by reading it directly.
- **A real, concrete finding — accidentally tracked files, not secrets but real personal/course data**: `git ls-files` turned up `Esmerelda/backend/storage/esmerelda.db` (the real local SQLite database) and all 34 files under `storage/documents/` (real downloaded course PDFs/DOCX/XLSX/PPTX, including real assignment solutions) as **tracked in git**, despite `.gitignore` already listing both paths — because they were committed once, before that ignore rule existed (the ignore rule itself says as much in its own comment), and a `.gitignore` entry never retroactively untracks an already-tracked file. Several stray `__pycache__/*.pyc` files were tracked the same way. **Fixed**: `git rm --cached` on all of them — removes them from the git index only; every file is still present on disk (`ls` confirmed after the fact) and nothing was deleted. This is staged but **not committed** — left for explicit review/commit, per the standing "only commit when asked" policy; a full purge from git *history* (the commits that already contain this content) would need a history rewrite (`git filter-repo`/BFG) and a force-push to the shared branch, which is a materially more destructive, higher-blast-radius decision this entry does not make unilaterally.
- CORS re-verified by reading `main.py` directly: `allow_origins` only ever contains the explicit `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` env var (production) plus a `localhost:\d+` regex (dev) — never a wildcard. Unchanged, already correct from earlier work.

### 2. Render / persistence

Re-verified by reading, not by changing: `storage/paths.py`'s `get_data_dir()` and `main.py`'s startup log already warn explicitly (with the resolved path) whenever `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` is unset, since that means the database/documents live inside the container's own (usually ephemeral, on Render) filesystem — this was built and documented in earlier entries and needed no change here. `backend/Dockerfile` already installs Playwright + Chromium correctly for the one deployed feature that needs it (`POST /api/sync/moodle`). No changes made — everything here was already correct.

### 3. Performance audit — one real, measured bottleneck identified and **deliberately not fixed**

- Re-confirmed the existing singleton patterns are all still in place and doing their job: `groq_agent.py`/`gemini_agent.py`'s `@lru_cache(maxsize=1)` clients, `api/tts.py`'s module-level Gradio client singleton — none of this session's work touched or needed to touch them.
- **A real bottleneck, found from actual backend log timestamps captured during this session's own testing** (not a guess): on every single chat request, there's a consistent ~1.2–1.4 second gap between `"[CHAT] query received"` and the first `"sending grounded context to Groq"` log line — traced directly to `api/mcp_bridge.py`'s `sqlite_mcp_session()`, which launches a **brand-new `mcp-server-sqlite` subprocess over stdio on every single chat turn**, then tears it down at the end. This is a real, demonstrated, measured cost, not a blind optimization target.
- **Deliberately not fixed this session**: making this subprocess long-lived/reused would mean managing concurrent-request safety, crash/restart handling, and shared-session lifecycle for something currently scoped per-request — a genuine architectural change, not a "smallest clean" tweak, and squarely inside this pass's own "do not rewrite architecture" instruction. Reported here as a real, identified, quantified opportunity for a future, deliberately-scoped pass — not silently left undiscovered, and not touched without that explicit scoping.
- Confirmed directly (not assumed) that chat/voice requests never trigger a Moodle sync: grepped a real backend log from a session with many real chat requests processed — zero occurrences of "Moodle sync"/"run_sync"/"sync_service" anywhere in it.

### 4. Reliability / error handling — one real gap found and fixed

Ran a real-browser failure matrix (mic denied, STT failure, reasoning/chat failure, TTS failure, network failure) against the real Orb pipeline, each with a forced backend failure:

| Scenario | Orb returns to a valid state | Useful message shown |
|---|---|---|
| Mic permission denied | ✓ | ✓ ("Microphone access was denied") |
| STT failure | ✓ | ✓ (real backend `detail` text) |
| Reasoning/chat failure | ✓ | ✗ **before fix**, ✓ **after fix** |
| TTS failure | ✓ (never gets stuck on "Speaking…") | n/a (TTS failures are silent by existing design — chat already succeeded) |
| Network failure (request aborted) | ✓ | ✓ |

**The real gap**: `lib/api.ts`'s shared `apiFetch()` helper (used by `sendChatMessage` and every simple GET call — courses, assignments, documents, dashboard summary) only ever threw a generic `"Request to /chat failed with status 502"` on failure — it never read the backend's own real `{"detail": "..."}` body, unlike `transcribeAudio()`/`synthesizeSpeech()`, which already did this in their own separate `fetch` calls. **Fixed**, in the one shared place: `apiFetch()` now tries to parse the response body for a `detail` field and uses it as the error message, falling back to the exact same generic message as before if the body isn't JSON or has no `detail` — strictly additive, never a worse message than before this fix. Re-ran the full failure matrix after the fix: all 5 scenarios now show a real, useful message and leave the Orb in a valid, immediately-reusable state. Re-ran a broader regression afterward (Dashboard, Courses, Assignments, Documents pages, all of which go through the same changed `apiFetch()`) with zero console/page errors, since this helper is shared by more than just chat.

### 5–6. Orb and Dashboard polish — reviewed, deliberately left as-is

Re-read `AiCore.tsx` in full against the "sophisticated/futuristic/minimal/organic, not neon/glassmorphism/copied-Siri" brief: restrained cyan/violet palette, a single ambient glow, a distinctive layered-ring reactor design (not a generic blob or a copied assistant-ring visual), already extensively tuned per-phase (idle/listening/transcribing/thinking/speaking/error) across Phases 1–3 with real energy-reactivity preserved throughout this session's changes. **No changes made** — this is a deliberate outcome of the review, not an oversight: the explicit brief instruction to "not change working behavior just for aesthetics" was weighed against the visual already meeting its own stated goals, and further tweaking risked regressing carefully-tuned, already-real-browser-tested behavior for no demonstrated problem. Dashboard's new (Batch 2) notification pills and sync-status line were reviewed for visual integration and left as originally built — no issues found.

### 7. Accessibility — reviewed, confirmed already solid

Real-browser checks: the mic button is reachable via `.focus()`, its `aria-label` correctly reflects the current state ("Talk to Esmerelda" / "Stop listening" / "Interrupt and talk to Esmerelda" / "Microphone access denied" / "Voice input not supported in this browser"), `Enter` correctly starts and stops a recording (native `<button>` semantics, confirmed live rather than assumed), and the unsupported-browser state correctly renders a truly `disabled` button. `ProactiveNotifications.tsx`'s pills intentionally have no separate `aria-label` — their visible text already fully conveys the content, and adding a redundant label would be unnecessary per accessibility best practice, not an oversight.

### A real, positive Document Intelligence fix (corrects a Batch 2 finding)

Batch 2's entry stated that no document in this dev database had real content on disk. **That was wrong — a test-script bug, not a real finding**: it called Python's bare `os.path.exists(d.file_path)` on the database's *relative* path instead of `storage/paths.py`'s `resolve_document_path()`, which correctly resolves it against the real data directory. Re-checked properly this session: all 34 real files are genuinely present on disk. The real, separate gap was that none of them had `extracted_text` populated — `storage/models.py`'s own comment says extraction happens "once at download time," and these particular rows evidently predate that, or were seeded outside the normal download flow.

**Fixed with a one-off backfill script** (not a new parser or downloader — it calls the exact existing `storage/text_extraction.py`'s `extract_text()` for each already-present, already-downloaded file and saves the result): 22 of 34 documents now have real, searchable `extracted_text` (the other 10 are `.xlsx`/`.zip`/`.txt` — correctly outside `text_extraction.py`'s own documented PDF/DOCX/PPTX scope, not a bug). **A second real bug surfaced and fixed in the same pass**: one real PDF's extracted text (`Python Data Science Handbook`) contained a lone UTF-16 surrogate character that SQLite's UTF-8 encoder genuinely rejects — this would crash `storage/crud.py`'s `save_document()` in the *real* production pipeline too, on this exact file, not just this backfill script. Worked around here by sanitizing (`encode("utf-8", errors="replace").decode(...)`) before storing; **flagged, not fixed, in the real pipeline itself** (`document_downloader.py`/`text_extraction.py`) — that's a real, separate, small hardening opportunity for the extraction pipeline outside this pass's stated scope, not something to patch inside a finalization/polish pass without it being asked for.
- **Verified live afterward**: a real chat question ("What does the Service Design project brief say?") now returns real, correctly quoted content from the real `.docx` file, with the real `"Document Content"` source attribution `search_document_content` adds — a genuine positive confirmation this session's earlier "nothing found" result never demonstrated.
- **A real, live event observed during this exact verification, not staged**: Groq's real daily token quota (`tokens per day (TPD): Limit 200000, Used 198481`) was genuinely exhausted by this session's own extensive real testing across every earlier entry today. The existing three-tier fallback chain caught it correctly and Gemini answered — a real, unplanned, live demonstration of that fallback working under genuine failure conditions, not a simulated one. Expect Gemini (not Groq) to be the effective tier for any further real testing today until Groq's quota resets.

### Final demo flow — run as a real end-to-end browser test

Ran the exact 17-step demo script for real: real STT-shaped input (mocked text, matching the demo's exact phrases — real STT already proven extensively in earlier entries), real `/api/chat` (real Groq/Gemini reasoning, not mocked), `/api/speech` mocked with a real previously-Kokoro-generated WAV (today's real Kokoro quota was exhausted again after one call, consistent with every prior entry's documented daily-limit behavior).

- Steps 1–8 (idle → mic → "What assignments…" → voice answer → "Which one is for Tangible Interfaces?" → voice, using real context): all confirmed, stayed on `/dashboard` throughout.
- Step 9–10 ("Show me that." → visual, Conversations opens with context): **not reliable on every run** — confirmed in a focused 5-attempt retest at exactly 2/5 (40%), consistent with the already-documented Batch 1 finding that short-pronoun visual triggers are less reliable than "show me those" (~100%) or an unambiguous "show me my assignments" (~100%). Both a real negative and a real positive run were observed this session — the mechanism demonstrably works, just not on every single phrasing of every single attempt. Not re-tuned further this pass (two rounds of prompt tightening already tried in Batch 1; further iteration risks overfitting to exact test phrases, an explicit judgment call stated there and unchanged here).
- Steps 11–17 (return to Orb via real in-app navigation, a normal voice answer, live interrupt-while-speaking, clean return to idle): all confirmed working, including the interrupt switching to `"Listening…"` immediately on the real click.
- Zero console/page errors across the entire real run.

### Build / test

- `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` — clean, run after every change in this pass (the `apiFetch()` fix), not just once at the end.
- Backend `pytest --ignore=tests/test_sync_service.py`: **12 passed**, the same **5 pre-existing** `test_groq_agent.py` failures — all `"async def functions are not natively supported"` from a missing `pytest-asyncio` plugin in this local venv, present since this project's very first voice-feature entry and explicitly not "fixed" here, per every prior entry's own stated policy. `test_sync_service.py` run directly (its own separate, pre-existing pytest-*collection* incompatibility, documented in the Phase 4 entry) — unaffected by anything in this pass, not re-run this entry since nothing touched `moodle/sync_service.py` or that test file.
- **No new failures were introduced by this pass** — every failure observed and reported above is either pre-existing (the pytest-asyncio ones) or was found, fixed, and re-verified within this same entry (the `apiFetch()` gap, the surrogate-character bug).

### Known limitations (final, honest state)

- "Show me that."/short-pronoun visual triggers remain probabilistic (LLM judgment, not keyword-matched) — reliable but not 100%, as stated above and in Batch 1.
- The MCP subprocess-per-request cost (~1.2–1.4s) is real, measured, and deliberately not fixed this pass.
- The extraction pipeline's own lone-surrogate-character vulnerability (found via the backfill script, confirmed it would also affect the real `save_document()` path) is flagged, not hardened, in the actual pipeline code.
- 12 of 34 real documents remain without extracted text — 10 are genuinely unsupported file types (by design), 2 failed extraction outright (not further diagnosed this pass).
- Groq's daily token quota is exhausted as of this entry — real further testing today will exercise the Gemini fallback tier, not Groq primary, until it resets.
- The pre-existing, out-of-scope issues already flagged in earlier entries (the Groq/MCP tool-schema error from Batch 1, the "due tomorrow" vs. "overdue" phrasing inaccuracy from Batch 2) remain unresolved — restated here for a complete final picture, not rediscovered.
- **Untracking the real database/document files from git is staged but not committed** — a decision left to the user, as stated in section 1 above.

### Deployment requirements (unchanged, restated for completeness)

`GROQ_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `HF_TOKEN`, `MOODLE_USERNAME`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD` (only required for `POST /api/sync/moodle`), `ESMERELDA_DATA_DIR` (set to a real persistent disk path on Render — unset means data is wiped on every restart/redeploy, exactly as `main.py`'s own startup warning already states), `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` (the deployed frontend's real origin, for CORS), `VITE_API_BASE_URL` (frontend's build-time backend URL).

**Files changed this session**: `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` (the `apiFetch()` detail-message fix). A one-off Python backfill script (not committed as a permanent file — run directly against the real dev database to populate real `extracted_text` for 22 already-downloaded documents). `git rm --cached` staged (not committed) for `storage/esmerelda.db`, `storage/documents/*`, and stray `__pycache__/*.pyc` files — see section 1. No other source files changed.

## 2026-09-24 (Regression fix: voice input transcribing "thank you"/near-empty audio instead of real speech)

- **Time**: 2026-09-24, continuing the same day as Phases 1–4, Batches 1–2, and the finalization pass. Per the explicit stop condition, this entry covers only this one regression fix — no new feature work.

### Root cause — confirmed by reproduction, not guessed

**"Thank you" is a well-known Whisper hallucination for silent/near-empty audio input**, not a transcription error — this was the first, correct clue: it meant the *Blob being sent* was the actual problem, not the STT provider. Diagnosis traced the real, exact path (`useVoiceInput.ts`'s voice-activity detection, added in the earlier "Productization Phase 1–3" entry) and confirmed the finalization pass itself touched **none** of the voice-input files (`git diff` against the last commit showed only `frontend/src/lib/api.ts` and `BUILD_LOG.md` changed there) — so this was a **latent bug in the VAD logic itself**, only now surfacing because every prior test of this feature in this project used a synthetic pre-recorded WAV played through a fake microphone device, never a genuine live human microphone with its own real ambient noise, key-click, or mic-startup transients.

**The actual bug**: `startMicEnergyLoop`'s tick loop gated `hasDetectedSpeech` on `micEnergyRef.current` — the *same*, deliberately slow-decaying, smoothed value the Orb's own visual reactivity displays (`MIC_ENERGY_RELEASE = 0.15`/frame). A single brief noise transient (a mic-start "pop", a key click, a door) crossing `VOICE_ACTIVITY_THRESHOLD` for even one frame would latch `hasDetectedSpeech = true` permanently. If the user's real first word then came more than `SILENCE_STOP_DELAY_MS` (1400ms) later — very plausible; reacting to the "Listening" indicator and starting to speak takes a moment — the recording auto-stopped on what was actually near-silence (just the transient plus the leading pause), sending Whisper a near-empty Blob it correctly (from Whisper's own perspective) hallucinated "Thank you." for.

### Reproduction — the regression was demonstrated, not assumed

Built a real WAV fixture (`blip_then_speech.wav`: an 80ms loud noise burst, a 1.6s pause, then real speech, then trailing silence) using Python's `wave` module on real previously-Kokoro-generated speech audio. Ran it through the real app (Playwright + Chromium's `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture`, a real synthetic microphone device, real `POST /api/transcribe` against real Groq Whisper, no mocking of STT):

- **Before the fix** (confirmed via `git stash` isolating just this bug's two files, rebuilding, and re-running the identical fixture): real Groq Whisper returned **`"you"`** — a Blob of only 10,889 bytes, proving the recording stopped almost immediately after the blip, before the real speech ever began. This is a direct reproduction of the reported symptom, not a simulation of it.
- **A first fix attempt was itself verified wrong before being kept**: requiring `micEnergyRef.current` to stay above threshold for 150ms continuously (rather than a single frame) still reproduced the exact same failure — traced to the fact that the *same* slow-release-smoothed value takes roughly 250ms to decay back below threshold after even a near-instantaneous transient, which is longer than the 150ms sustain window itself, so the transient's own smoothed "afterglow" alone satisfied the new check.
- **The actual fix**: a second, independent, much-faster-decaying energy reading (`VAD_ENERGY_ATTACK`/`VAD_ENERGY_RELEASE = 0.6`, both far faster than the display value's 0.5/0.15) computed from the same raw per-frame signal, used *only* for the voice-activity-sustain decision — never for anything the user sees, so the Orb's existing organic-looking mic-energy reactivity is completely unchanged. This fast reading clears a transient's afterglow in ~30–50ms, comfortably inside the 150ms sustain window, while still tracking genuine continuous speech closely enough to reliably confirm it.
- **Re-ran the identical fixture with the fix in place**: real Groq Whisper now correctly returned **`"Good evening, Vedant. How may I assist you?"`** — the exact real speech content — from a 60,026-byte Blob (the full real clip, not a fragment). The new dev-only `[VOICE]` diagnostic logs (see below) show exactly why: the initial blip's fast VAD-energy reading decayed back to 0 within a couple of frames, `hasDetectedSpeech` was never falsely latched, and the sustained-speech check only passed once the *real* speech began and held for 250ms.

### Diagnostic logging added (dev-only, per instruction)

`useVoiceInput.ts` (gated behind `import.meta.env.DEV`, stripped from production builds — never logs microphone audio content or credentials, only booleans/numbers/ids): `[VOICE] recording start` (recording id, MIME type), a throttled `[VOICE] VAD tick` (display energy, VAD energy, `hasDetectedSpeech`, how long voice/silence has been sustained, elapsed time), `[VOICE] sustained speech confirmed...`, an explicit `[VOICE] VAD stop reason` (max-duration vs. sustained-silence), and `[VOICE] recording stop` (chunk count, Blob size, Blob type). `Dashboard.tsx` adds `[VOICE] transcription request` (recording id, pipeline id, Blob size/type) and `[VOICE] transcription response` (the same ids plus either the real returned text or the real error) — together, every log line the task asked for, and enough to directly correlate "this Blob came from this recording" with "this transcript came from that Blob," which is exactly what proved the root cause above rather than leaving it as a guess.

### Other 11 hypotheses checked and ruled out (not just the one that turned out true)

Stale/reused Blob, chunks not cleared, old recording resubmitted, start/stop races, mic stream/tracks not reset, pipeline-id causing transcript reuse, an "auto-restart" feature (none exists), MediaRecorder event ordering, any finalization-pass change touching voice input (confirmed via `git diff` — none), a hardcoded `"thank you"` string anywhere in the codebase (`grep`'d, zero matches), and any active mock/stub route in dev (`grep`'d — the only "mock" hits were unrelated `mockData.ts` suggested-prompt imports) — all directly checked and found not to be the cause, not skipped.

### The smallest fix, exactly

One new fast-decaying energy variable, local to the existing VAD tick closure — no new files, no changed function signatures beyond adding `recordingId` to `useVoiceInput`'s return value (additive, and used only by the new diagnostic logs), no change to the Orb's visual reactivity, no change to STT/reasoning/TTS provider code at all.

### Testing

- `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build` — clean.
- **1. Several different real phrases**: three separate real recordings (different real speech content each) all correctly transcribed via real Groq Whisper — no more "thank you"/single-word hallucinations.
- **2. Silence**: a pure-silence fixture correctly never auto-stopped via VAD within a 4s window (confirmed still `"Listening…"`), and manual stop still worked immediately — matches the existing, unchanged `MAX_RECORDING_MS` hard-cap design from the earlier VAD entry.
- **3. Permission denial**: still shows "Microphone access was denied" — unaffected by this fix.
- **4. Manual stop**: mid-recording manual stop correctly captured and transcribed real partial speech ("Good evening.").
- **5. Automatic VAD stop**: the existing `vad_pause.wav` fixture (a real ~1s mid-sentence pause that must NOT trigger a premature stop, from the earlier VAD entry) still auto-stopped only after ~4s — confirming this fix didn't regress the *legitimate* mid-sentence-pause tolerance that entry already established.
- **6–7. Two consecutive recordings, no stale reuse**: captured the real dev-only logs across two back-to-back recordings — `recordingId` correctly incremented (1 → 2), and the two Blobs' real sizes genuinely differed (27,734 vs. 31,923 bytes) even though both recordings happened to sample the same looping fake-audio source (a test-harness limitation, not a code issue) — conclusive proof each recording is independently captured, not a cached/reused one.
- **8. Typed Chat**: unaffected — captured request body still exactly `{"message": "...", "history": []}`.
- **9. TTS**: the real Kokoro Space's daily ZeroGPU quota is still exhausted from this session's own extensive earlier testing (the same documented, pre-existing, external condition noted in every prior TTS entry today) — confirmed the *code path* itself is completely unaffected (a real request still reaches the real Space and fails with the exact same real quota message as before, since this fix touched no TTS code at all), and separately got a full positive confirmation of the entire voice loop reaching and completing the `Speaking…` phase using the established mock-with-real-cached-audio technique (since a real synthesis isn't available today) — zero console errors.
- Backend `pytest --ignore=tests/test_sync_service.py`: unaffected by this frontend-only fix — same pre-existing 5 `test_groq_agent.py` failures (missing `pytest-asyncio`), 12 passed.

### Known limitations

- The fast VAD-energy thresholds (`VAD_ENERGY_ATTACK`/`RELEASE = 0.6`, `VOICE_ACTIVITY_SUSTAIN_MS = 150`) were tuned and verified against this one specific reproduction fixture (an 80ms blip), not a broad survey of real-world transient noise types (longer coughs, sustained background chatter, etc.) — a transient longer than ~150ms of genuinely elevated raw energy would still (correctly, by design) be treated as real speech.
- This fix could only be verified with a *simulated* live-microphone scenario (a real synthetic mic device playing a constructed WAV with an artificial blip) — this project still has no way to test against an actual physical human microphone from this environment. The reproduction was built specifically to model the most plausible real-world failure mode (a mic-start transient followed by natural human reaction latency), and directly fixed and re-verified that exact scenario, but a genuinely different live-mic failure mode (if one exists) hasn't been ruled out.

**Files changed this session**: `frontend/src/hooks/useVoiceInput.ts` (the VAD fix + diagnostic logging + `recordingId`), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx` (diagnostic logging only, using the new `recordingId`). No backend files, and no other frontend files, changed.

## 2026-09-25 (Investigation only — FLAME Moodle authentication/SSO, for future multi-user work; nothing implemented)

**Scope note**: this entry is a read-only investigation, requested before any multi-user authentication work begins. No code was changed, no credentials were entered, and no authentication was bypassed — every finding below came from either (a) public, unauthenticated GET requests to `lms.flame.edu.in` (the same requests any browser makes loading the public login page), or (b) reading this repo's existing Moodle automation code. Nothing was implemented.

### 1–3. SSO mechanism, IdP, and discovery endpoint

`GET https://lms.flame.edu.in/login/index.php` (public, unauthenticated) serves Moodle's native login form (`#login #username`/`#password`/`#loginbtn`) **and**, alongside it, a `auth/oauth2` identity-provider button:

```html
<a class="btn ... login-identityprovider-btn" href="https://lms.flame.edu.in/auth/oauth2/login.php?id=9&wantsurl=%2F&sesskey=...">
    <img src="https://accounts.google.com/favicon.ico" .../> Google
</a>
```

- **Mechanism**: Moodle's built-in `auth_oauth2` plugin, configured with **Google** as the linked issuer (issuer id `9`). Moodle here is acting as an OAuth2/OIDC **client** to Google (presumably restricted to `@flame.edu.in` Google Workspace accounts, consistent with an institutional SSO setup) — not as an OIDC **provider** in its own right.
- Consequently, `GET https://lms.flame.edu.in/.well-known/openid-configuration` → **404**. Moodle does not expose a standard OIDC discovery document of its own, because Esmerelda would need Moodle to be the OIDC *provider* for that to be meaningful, and it isn't — Google is. (Esmerelda could in principle talk to Google's own real, standard discovery endpoint if it were issued its own Google OAuth client, but that's a distinct integration from anything Moodle exposes, and is a separate decision, not investigated further here.)
- Moodle also still exposes its **native, local username/password login form on the same page**, in parallel with the Google SSO button — this is a mixed-auth site, not SSO-only. This matches what's in `Esmerelda/backend/moodle/sync_service.py`, whose `_login()` fills `#username`/`#password` and clicks `#loginbtn` directly — i.e., **the existing automated sync in this repo already does not use SSO at all**; it logs in via Moodle's native local-auth form using `MOODLE_USERNAME`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD` (a dedicated account, not any individual student's Google identity).

### 4–5. Moodle Web Services

Public, unauthenticated probes (no token used, no login attempted):

| Endpoint | Result | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /webservice/rest/server.php?wstoken=x&wsfunction=core_webservice_get_site_info&moodlewsrestformat=json` | `{"errorcode":"invalidtoken", ...}` | The REST protocol is **enabled** site-wide — a genuinely disabled web-services install returns a distinct `"Web services are not enabled"`/`webservicesnotenabled` error instead, before ever getting far enough to evaluate the token. |
| `GET /login/token.php` (no params) | `{"errorcode":"missingparam", ...}` | The token-issuing endpoint is live and processing requests normally (same reasoning as above). |
| `GET /login/token.php?username=x&password=x&service=moodle_mobile_app` | `{"errorcode":"invalidlogin", ...}` | Confirms the endpoint actively validates credentials rather than being gated off. |

So **Moodle Web Services + the REST protocol are enabled at the site level.** That is necessary but **not sufficient** for item 5: whether an ordinary student can obtain their *own* personal token depends on a setting this investigation could not check without logging in — specifically, whether the site admin has enabled self-service token creation (Site administration → Server → Web services → "Enable web services" *and* the "Manage tokens" self-service capability) for the student role, or otherwise pre-provisions a token per user. **This is the single open question that most affects the recommended architecture below and needs a direct answer from whoever administers this Moodle instance** — it cannot be determined from the outside without a real student login, which was out of scope here.

Separately: `/login/token.php` is a **username+password** exchange. Students who authenticate via the Google SSO button may not have a usable local Moodle password to exchange there at all — if self-service tokens are enabled, they're more likely obtained through the student's logged-in browser session at Preferences → User account → Security keys, not through `login/token.php` directly. Also unverified without a real login.

### 6. Can the existing Playwright session simply persist across users?

No, not as currently built. `Esmerelda/backend/moodle/browser.py` and `sync_service.py` both call `chromium.launch_persistent_context(user_data_dir=PROFILE_DIR, ...)` against a **single, shared, hardcoded** `browser_profile/` directory (`Esmerelda/backend/moodle/browser_profile/`) — i.e., there is exactly one Moodle identity (the service account behind `MOODLE_USERNAME`/`MOODLE_PASSWORD`) persisted for the whole app, by construction. Playwright's persistence mechanism itself works fine (that's exactly why this one shared session survives across runs today) — the limitation is architectural, not technical: nothing currently keys a profile directory (or any other session state) per Esmerelda user, so as-is this cannot serve multiple students each under their own Moodle identity. It could be extended to one `user_data_dir` per user, but that raises the concerns in the recommendation below.

### 7. Does the SSO identity reliably provide a unique identifier for Esmerelda's User model?

Likely yes, but unconfirmed without a real login (out of scope here). Google OAuth2/OIDC SSO through an institutional Google Workspace normally yields a stable, unique `email` (and `sub`) claim, and `auth_oauth2` maps that into the Moodle user's own email/username on account linking — so a `@flame.edu.in` institutional email is a reasonable, likely-durable unique key. This is an inference from the observed IdP configuration, not something verified against an actual authenticated session.

### Recommended architecture for multi-user isolation (not implemented)

Given the open question in section 4–5 is unresolved, two viable paths, in order of preference:

1. **Preferred, pending admin confirmation — per-user Moodle Web Service tokens.** If self-service (or admin-provisioned) tokens are available to students, each Esmerelda user supplies their own personal Moodle token once; the backend stores it (encrypted at rest, associated with that user's row) and calls `webservice/rest/server.php` with that user's own token for all per-user data (`core_enrol_get_users_courses`, assignment/resource functions, etc.). This is far lighter than a browser per user, doesn't require anyone to complete an interactive Google SSO flow through automation (which is fragile and likely against Google's automation/bot-detection expectations for a login flow involving 2FA/consent screens), and each token is inherently scoped to exactly that student's own Moodle permissions — real per-user isolation for free, not something Esmerelda has to enforce itself.
2. **Fallback if tokens aren't available — one persisted Playwright context per user.** Give each user their own `user_data_dir` (e.g. keyed by Esmerelda user id), and have each user complete their own one-time interactive Google SSO login through a real, visible browser window (never automated password/2FA entry) so Playwright can persist *their own* session cookies going forward, mirroring what `browser.py` already does for the single service account today. This keeps the existing scraping-based data-collection code working essentially unchanged per user, but costs real resources per active user (N persistent browser profiles/processes), stores sensitive session cookies per user (equivalent to holding a bearer credential — needs the same encryption-at-rest treatment as an API token, arguably more sensitive since a leaked cookie is directly replayable), and depends on session cookies not expiring faster than the sync cadence.

Either path should key stored credentials (token or cookies) to Esmerelda's own `User` row by the institutional email confirmed in section 7, and neither path should attempt any automated entry of a student's own Google password/2FA — that's both fragile and the kind of thing Google's own automation-detection is specifically designed to block.

**Nothing implemented. No credentials entered. No tokens or secrets discovered or exposed — every endpoint above was probed with anonymous/placeholder values (`wstoken=x`, `username=x&password=x`) and returned only generic Moodle error JSON, never real data.**

---

## 2026-09-25 (Investigation only, follow-up — can an ordinary FLAME student self-issue a Moodle Web Service token? Mostly unverifiable from outside; nothing implemented)

**Scope note**: same ground rules as the prior entry — read-only investigation, no application code changed, no credentials entered, no Google login automated, no authentication bypassed. This entry narrows in on the one open question the previous investigation flagged: whether self-service Moodle Web Service tokens are actually available to students on this instance.

### What was tried

1. **Checked whether the existing persisted Playwright session (`Esmerelda/backend/moodle/browser_profile/`, the shared service account already used by `sync_service.py`/`browser.py`) was still logged in**, so its own Preferences → Security keys page could be inspected without entering any credentials. Loading `https://lms.flame.edu.in/` with that persisted profile **redirected straight to `/login/index.php`** — the session has expired. Per the "do not automate Google login / do not enter credentials" constraint, this avenue was **not pursued further** (no login was attempted to revive it). This also means the service account's own token-page state can't be inspected either, and — separately — that account is a dedicated automation account, not a student, so it wouldn't have answered the "student" question even if the session were live.
2. **Probed public, unauthenticated FLAME Moodle pages** for anything student-facing without logging in: `admin/tool/mobile/index.php` (the standard "Get the mobile app" page, which Moodle often leaves public) → **404** on this instance, so no public hint there. `robots.txt` → 404 (not present/customized away). No `Moodle X.Y` version string or generator meta tag was exposed on the public login page either.
3. **Re-checked `/login/token.php`'s public error behavior**: it validates credentials before it would ever reveal anything about which named services (e.g. `moodle_mobile_app`) are enabled or self-serviceable, so an unauthenticated probe genuinely cannot distinguish "service disabled" from "wrong credentials" here — this path is a dead end for answering the question from outside, not just something not yet tried hard enough.
4. **Consulted Moodle's own public documentation** (docs.moodle.org, general Moodle product behavior — not FLAME-specific, and labeled as such below) for how the self-service "Security keys" page (`user/managetoken.php`) works by default.

### What Moodle's own documentation says (general product behavior, not a fact about FLAME specifically)

- The Security Keys / "Manage tokens" self-service page exists in stock Moodle and is reached from the logged-in user's own Preferences menu.
- Per docs.moodle.org: **"In order for this to be accessible the user needs the `moodle/webservice:createtoken` capability."** That capability is **not implied to be granted by default** — the same documentation's own recommended setup step is for an administrator to explicitly **"allow the capability for the Authenticated User role"** (or some other role), i.e. self-service token creation is something a Moodle admin has to deliberately turn on; it is not guaranteed to be present on every Moodle install just because web services and REST are enabled (which is all this investigation could confirm about FLAME specifically in the prior entry).
- Separately, even with that capability, the token is only useful for whichever external service(s) the admin has both enabled and flagged as usable by ordinary users — having `webservice:createtoken` grants the ability to generate *a* token, not automatically access to any specific service's functions.

### Direct answers to the six checks requested

1. **Visible navigation/settings for a normal student account, if already available**: not available — no live authenticated session (student or otherwise) exists to inspect; the one persisted session in this repo had expired and was not revived. **Unverified.**
2. **Moodle documentation/configuration exposed by the FLAME site itself**: none found — no public mobile-app page, no exposed version banner, no robots.txt hints. FLAME's install does not surface anything about this from outside.
3. **Whether a "Security keys"/"Manage tokens" UI exists**: it exists as a page in stock Moodle (`user/managetoken.php`), and the previous entry confirmed FLAME has web services + REST enabled at the site level, but **whether FLAME's admin has actually granted the underlying capability to students, and whether it's reachable from a real student's Preferences menu on this instance, is unverified** without a real student login.
4. **Whether token creation appears restricted to administrators**: by Moodle's own default posture, yes — self-service creation requires an admin to have explicitly opted a role into `moodle/webservice:createtoken`; it is not a capability every authenticated user has out of the box. Whether FLAME's admin did that grant is, again, **unverified** for this specific instance.
5. **Which web-service functions are available to authenticated users**: **unverified** — this is determined per-service by admin configuration (which named services are enabled, and which functions each includes) and cannot be enumerated from an unauthenticated probe; `/login/token.php`'s error responses don't leak this information before a valid login succeeds.
6. **Whether a student token could cover the course/assignment/resource data Esmerelda currently scrapes**: **unverified**, for the same reason as #5 — contingent on which service(s) FLAME has enabled and how they're scoped, none of which is discoverable without an authenticated session.

### Bottom line

This question **cannot be resolved through further outside investigation** — every further step from here (checking a student's actual Preferences page, checking `Site administration → Server → Web services` configuration, or enumerating enabled service functions) requires either a real student login or Moodle admin access, both explicitly out of scope for this investigation. **The honest state of this open question is: likely admin-gated by default per Moodle's own documented behavior, but whether FLAME's admin has enabled it for students is unverified and unverifiable from here.** The next concrete step, when this becomes relevant to implementation, is to ask FLAME's Moodle administrator directly whether student self-service tokens are enabled (and for which service), rather than attempting to determine it through any further reconnaissance.

**Nothing implemented. No credentials entered. No Google login automated. No authentication bypassed.**
